# Changelog

Every PHP# release, newest first. PHP#, its engine and `mago` release together at one version. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/). Before 1.0, a minor release may break 0.x code, so each one opens with what to change when upgrading.

## [Unreleased]

### Upgrading from 0.6.54

- Drop `internal` from the `set` of each property that plain PHP outside its namespace writes, or move that write into the namespace: the engine now refuses it with `Error`, where 0.6.54 ran it as public. Plain PHP outside the namespace that lists an object's properties with `get_object_vars`, `var_export` or an array cast no longer finds an `internal` property there, so declare `public` each one such code reads.

### Added

- A law reads `join(separator)` of a `List<string>`, spec section 28: the strings with the separator between each two, and `""` of no strings. So `` new Notice("", [a, b]).checklist == `${a}, ${b}` `` proves, where 0.6.53 refused it with `unmodeled-construct`, and a law that states the wrong text of a join fails for the small values that break it.

### Fixed

- A computed property in a machine case's block is a member of that case's class, spec section 20.2, as a move of the block is: the machine compiles, where 0.6.53 refused the file with `lowering-panic`, and a law reads the property on a value of the case. A read through the machine's type, as `Readiness r = Readiness.open(); r.failureMessage;`, is refused with the test that narrows `r` to the case, as a move's call is.

### Changed

- The engine holds an `internal set` to its namespace, spec section 5, as it holds an `internal` member: plain PHP's `$ledger->views = 3;` from outside `App.Billing` throws "Cannot modify internal(set) property App\Billing\Ledger::$views from global scope, outside namespace App\Billing", where 0.6.54 ran it as a public set. The engine checks it where PHP checks `protected(set)`, so a write to any other property costs what it did. `get_object_vars`, `var_export` and an array cast that plain PHP makes leave out an `internal` property that code outside its namespace doesn't reach, where 0.6.54 listed it to `var_export` and an array cast. `Json.encode` still writes every one.

## [0.6.54] - 2026-11-23

### Upgrading from 0.6.53

- Write `public` each class that plain PHP outside its namespace creates or calls, a test's top-level code and a framework that resolves a method by name included, and each `internal` member such code uses, or move that code into the namespace: the engine now refuses such a use with `Error`, where 0.6.53 ran it as public.
- Require the project's Composer autoloader first in a preload file you write yourself, as the `.sharp/preload.php` that `mago compile` writes does: preloaded code that calls `toBytes()` on a `string`, an `int` or a `List<int>` now runs a method of the standard library, which the autoloader loads, where 0.6.53 ran a copy of its body.

### Fixed

- A law over a machine's `match (this)` whose last arm names the other cases with `or` proves within its time again, spec sections 20 and 28, as in 0.6.49. So beside a machine of seven cases with `public bool charges() => match (this) { PlanState.Trial => true, PlanState.Live live => !live.stopsAtEnd, PlanState.Draft or PlanState.Held or PlanState.Overdue or PlanState.Lapsed or PlanState.Closed => false };` and a `chargeable()` whose `or` arm calls `this.charges()`, `law anOverduePlanIsChargeable(Instant chargeAt, Instant retryAt, string why, Instant failedAt) => PlanState.enter().start(chargeAt).lapse(retryAt, 2, why, failedAt).chargeable();` proves with 8,682 thousand heartbeats, where 0.6.52 refused it with "the proof ran out of time before it finished". Each case of an `or` arm is an arm of the `match` on its own, with the arm's branch, where 0.6.52 wrote the whole `or` again for each case an arm before it left out, so `charges` took 12,826 characters of Lean, where 0.6.49's took 940 and now 537. A law over a 9-case `match` of the same shape proves with 4,653 thousand heartbeats, where the translation before this fix took 61,513.
- A compile proves each law on a thread of its own, so a file's laws prove beside each other, where 0.6.52 proved a file's laws one after another, and a compile whose slowest laws shared a few files proved on about one core. So the 13 laws of four machines, five of them over one machine's `at`, prove in a laws phase of 52 to 80 s, where 0.6.52 took 101 to 197 s and refused one machine, and the 11 of them that 0.6.49 proves take 38 to 45 processor seconds where 0.6.49 took 75 to 78, in alternating rounds.

### Changed

- The engine holds `internal` when the code runs, spec section 5: a call, a property read or write, a constant, and a callable string or array that `call_user_func` or `Closure::fromCallable` resolves reach an `internal` member only from its namespace and the namespaces below it, plain PHP and PHP# alike, and so does `new` of an `internal` class, where 0.6.53 ran them as public and only the checker refused a use. A member written `internal` is held, and so is a `public` member of an `internal` class, unless it replaces a member of a parent class or an interface that reaches further. A file's top-level code counts as the global namespace. So plain PHP's `$ledger->total()` from outside `App.Billing` throws "Call to internal method App\Billing\Ledger::total() from global scope, outside namespace App\Billing", and `new Ledger()` throws "Cannot instantiate internal class App\Billing\Ledger from global scope, outside namespace App\Billing", as does a PHP# name the checker can't follow that plain PHP calls. The engine checks where it checks `private` and keeps the outcome where it keeps that one's, so a use it already allowed costs nothing more. `Property.get` and `set`, and `Method.createInvoker` and `createDelegate`, refuse plain PHP outside the namespace too. The `#[\Sharp\Internal\Internal]` attribute is gone: reflection reads `Visibility.Internal` from the member's flag. An `internal set` still runs as a public one.

## [0.6.53] - 2026-11-23

### Upgrading from 0.6.52

- Make each extension's `WorkerReducer::collect()` clear what it returns: an analysis now runs the reducer after each analysis, where 0.6.52 ran it once when Mago stopped the hosts, so a reducer that keeps its state hands the same files over again in the next analysis of a watch or server run.

### Fixed

- `mago compile` and a whole `mago analyze` with an extension host whose file constructs a standard library class as it loads, as a host that does `new SystemFiles()` to build its services, run on a clean tree and after the cache is cleared, spec section 27. The run lowers the standard library alone into the hosts' folder in mago's cache before any host starts, then compiles the rest of the project with the hosts as before, and the next compile of the tree writes nothing. In 0.6.52 each host's file loaded before the run wrote the hosts' folder, so it failed with "extension host `files` could not load: CompileError: vendor/heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer/library/Sharp/IO/SystemFiles.sharp isn't compiled. Run vendor/bin/mago compile.", and that run left the folder unwritten, so every later run failed the same way.
- A run whose extension host fails to load still writes the `.sharp` folder and the hosts' folder, then fails with the host's error, so a host that fails once loads on the next run with no other step. A host's file that constructs one of the project's own classes as it loads, before the run compiled it for the hosts, is refused with "app/Order.sharp isn't compiled for the extension hosts yet: a host's file runs as its worker starts, before mago compiles the project's own files, so there it can construct only the PHP# library's classes. Construct it in a hook instead.", and the next run loads it. In 0.6.52 such a run wrote neither folder, so every later run failed with "app/Order.sharp isn't compiled. Run vendor/bin/mago compile.", the command that had just failed.
- A compile whose `.sharp` folder is current writes nothing under `.sharp` when only the extension hosts' folder in mago's cache is out of date, spec section 27, as after the cache is cleared or in a run under another `HOME` or `XDG_CACHE_HOME`: it compiles the hosts' folder alone. Each folder is current by its own record, where 0.6.52 compiled the whole project again for a stale hosts' folder and rewrote `.sharp/inputs.json` of a tree no file of which changed.
- Two `List`s of a type parameter bounded by `Equatable` compare element by element, each pair by the `==` of the type its values are, spec sections 11 and 19. So over `public static bool same<T : Equatable>(List<T> a, List<T> b) => a == b;`, `same([new Point(1)], [new Point(1)])` of a `struct Point` gives true, where 0.6.52 compared the two arrays with PHP's `===`, which tells two equal structs apart, and gave false.
- A compile whose download of the law checker drops, as on a network that drops the connection, downloads it again, three times in all, and proves its laws. So compiles that start together on an empty cache, as `sharp/bin/spec`'s do, each prove their laws, where 0.6.52 refused the laws of the compile that downloaded first with "The law checker could not be installed. Run vendor/bin/mago compile again to retry, with MAGO_LOG=debug to see why.". An install that still fails names the step and its cause in the refusal, as "The law checker could not be installed: …/mago/lean/lean-4.34.1-linux: downloading https://github.com/leanprover/lean4/releases/download/v4.34.1/lean-4.34.1-linux.tar.zst failed on each of 3 tries: Peer disconnected.", where 0.6.52 named it only under `MAGO_LOG=debug`. A build of the law checker that `lake build` fails names the error lines `lake build` printed in the same way, as "The laws could not be checked: the law checker could not be built in Mago's cache folder: lake build printed:" followed by each line that starts with `error`, where 0.6.52 named them only under `MAGO_LOG=debug`.
- A project's image on the runtime image reuses the `-dev` image's Lean with one line, `COPY --from=ghcr.io/heyjordanparker/php-sharp:<version>-dev /var/cache/mago /var/cache/mago`, as the README's runtime images section shows. The runtime image sets `XDG_CACHE_HOME` to `/var/cache`, as the `-dev` image does, and holds `/var/cache/mago` with mode 777, so the copy lands in a folder every user writes in. On 0.6.52's runtime image the copy made the folder root's with mode 755, and nothing named it as Mago's cache folder, so a container run as `www-data` failed its first compile with an extension host with "can't create the folder `/var/cache/mago/hosts/…`: Permission denied".

### Added

- An interface that extends `Equatable` has `==`, spec section 19: two of its values are equal when they are the same type and that type's `==` holds, so a `Set` holds one of two equal values and a `Map` keys them. So beside `public interface Failure : Equatable { }`, `enum Declined : Failure { case Card; case Fraud; }` and `enum Lost : Failure { case Gone; }`, a `Failure card` that holds `Declined.Card` gives `card == Declined.Card` true, `card == Declined.Fraud` false and `card == Lost.Gone` false, where 0.6.52 refused each with "`Failure` declares no `operator ==`". Every type that implements such an interface has `==`: an enum, a struct whose fields have `==`, or a class that declares or inherits `operator ==`, so `class Plain : Failure { }` is refused at its header with "`Plain` implements `Failure`, which extends `Equatable`, so it needs `==`: `Plain` declares no `operator ==`.". A law over such values proves, as `law keepsItsFailure(Failure f) => new Failed(f).failure == f;`, and through each implementer of a sealed one, so `Declined.Card != Lost.Gone` proves there, and so does `Failed.of(Declined.Card) == Declined.Card`, which compares a `Failure` with the case it holds.
- Production code that uses a class of a dev-only package is a compile error that says where tests go, spec section 5, since a production install leaves the package out. A dev-only package is one `composer.lock` lists under `packages-dev`, PHPUnit installed through Pest included, or, with no `composer.lock`, one `composer.json` lists under `require-dev`. So `internal class OptInTest : TestCase` in `app/`, with PHPUnit under `packages-dev`, is refused with "`OptInTest` extends `TestCase`, from phpunit/phpunit, a dev-only package that a production install leaves out. Tests go in a folder composer.json lists under autoload-dev, such as tests/.", where 0.6.52 refused it only with "Nothing uses `OptInTest`, so it never runs." and help about `[DynamicallyAccessed]`. The same class in `tests/`, a folder under `autoload-dev`, compiles, and so does production code that uses a package under `require`. It runs in projects and libraries alike.
- An extension's worker reducer reports issues: `WorkerReductionContext::report(Level $level, string $code, Issue $issue)` reports one as `LifecycleContext::report` does, coded under the extension's identifier. An analysis runs each reducer once, after every worker ran its hooks on every file and before the report, so a rule that needs the whole codebase gathers in an after-file hook on whichever worker analyzes each file and reports from `reduce()`, where 0.6.52 ran a reducer only when Mago stopped the hosts and a rule could report only from an after-analysis hook that walks every file on one thread. On a generated project of 2,128 PHP# files whose rule reports each method body an earlier file wrote, the reducer form reports the same 2,513 issues as the after-analysis form, and its analysis phase takes 0.15 to 0.29 s where the after-analysis form's takes 0.24 to 0.55 s, 0.12 to 0.38 s of it in the hook's walk, lower in each of 6 alternating rounds. The linter still runs its reducers when Mago stops the hosts.
- A test run checks each `extern` line's `uses`, spec section 29, as the README's testing section shows. Under `php -d sharp.effect_report=build/effects.json`, each call of a plain PHP method an `extern` line declares records each built-in it runs, past PHP# code it calls back, a class it loads and the files of its own package, wherever the project's `config.vendor-dir` installs it, and a fiber's calls count apart from those of the code that resumes it, and `mago effects build/effects.json` reads the report. A line whose calls ran a built-in with an effect it doesn't list fails it, as "`Validator.check` is declared `uses none` (app/Stubs/Libraries.sharp:7), but it ran PDO::query, which has the effect Database.", and a `uses any` line gets the `uses` it needed, as "`Formatter.format` is declared `uses any`, and used nothing in 2 calls: declare it `uses none`.". In 0.6.52 nothing checked a `uses` line. Without the setting the engine observes no call.

### Changed

- A cold `mago compile` proves its laws with less processor time: the law checker elaborates each generated module and each law's theorem on the thread that judges it, where 0.6.52 elaborated them in Lean's tasks. So a cold compile of a generated project of 2,246 PHP# files and 1,330 laws takes 78 to 95 processor seconds where 0.6.52's took 117 to 124, and its laws phase 22 to 30 s where 0.6.52's took 33 to 47, in three alternating rounds with the same outcome for every law. Every law counts the same heartbeats on each compile of the same code, whichever threads elaborated its modules.
- With `MAGO_LOG=debug`, the laws phase logs how each law ended, its time in the runners and the thousands of Lean heartbeats its proof counted, the most heartbeats first, as "The law App.Shared.Stamp.formatsApart ended proved after 11.64 s and 8213 thousand heartbeats of the provers' time.", where 0.6.52 logged only its time, the slowest first. A law's heartbeats are the same on every compile of the same code, under any load, so they show which law grew costlier between two releases. The runner sends each law's heartbeats with its judgment, so the first compile with this release proves every law of the project again once, as a release that changes the law checker does.

## [0.6.52] - 2026-11-23

### Upgrading from 0.6.51

- Move each test module whose file's class is abstract or doesn't extend PHPUnit's `TestCase` into the file of a test that does: such a module never ran, and it is refused now.
- Give a type in the code to each PHP# file that compiles only with a type an extension host gives, as a return type declared on the plain PHP helper it calls: `mago compile` refuses such a file now, as a whole `mago analyze` and `mago --no-extensions compile` already did.
- Fix each argument of an `extern` property's attribute that its parameter doesn't take: `mago compile` checks those attributes now, as it checks any property's.

### Changed

- A whole `mago analyze` with extension hosts reports from its compile's own analysis, which the hosts take over, where 0.6.50 loaded, scanned, populated and analyzed every file a second time with them. The hosts start beside the compile, which records each return-type request a host's provider targets. The hosts answer them, and only the files an answer types are analyzed again. Each file a codebase-scan hook selects is scanned again with the hosts, and a declaration a hook refines reaches every file that reads it. A stub a host declares reaches every file that reads it. Hosts with any other provider, an issue filter, an analysis hook, a before-analysis hook, an entry point or a worker reducer analyze every file again from the compile's scans. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project whose host answers return types takes 4.78 CPU-seconds where it took 6.61, and peaks at 647 MiB where it peaked at 729. With a codebase-scan hook as well, it takes 4.84 CPU-seconds where it took 6.77, and peaks at 666 MiB where it peaked at 747. Each is lower in each of 16 alternating rounds, with the same issues and `.sharp` folder.

### Fixed

- An extension rule reads a machine's case where an `is` test names it, as it does in `typeof`. In `status is not SubscriptionStatus.Active active` and `status is SubscriptionStatus.Active active`, `SourceFile.getResolvedName` of the `DottedIdentifier` gives `App\Billing\SubscriptionStatus\Active`, where 0.6.51 gave the machine `App\Billing\SubscriptionStatus`. In `status is SubscriptionStatus.Active`, `FileAnalysis.getExpressionType` of the pattern's expression gives the case's type, where 0.6.51 gave `null`.
- A law keeps the outcome of its proof across releases while nothing its proof reads changed: its statement, its theorem and the code it reaches, the law checker's Lean library and Lean version, and the heartbeats and fixed steps it is proved with. So the first compile after an upgrade proves only the laws whose proof the release changed, and none after a release that changes only the version, where 0.6.51 proved every law of the project again after any upgrade, which took 749 laws of a 2,400-file project about an hour of processor time.
- The container finds a class's services in the modules of its namespace and of each namespace above it, wherever the class's file is, spec section 32, as the checker does. So a program under `tests/lib` that declares `namespace App.Testing.Lib` and takes a `Runtime` that `App`'s module binds runs with that `Runtime`, where 0.6.51 compiled it and then threw "No module binds App\Shared\Runtime for App\Testing\Lib\Link", because it read only the modules in the file's folder and the folders above it. A module in that folder that declares `namespace Tests.Lib` serves the classes of `Tests.Lib` alone. A test module in a file that holds no test is refused with the file named, "`tests/lib/Link.sharp` holds no test, so its test module would never run.", where 0.6.51 compiled a program's `module : App { … }` under `tests/` and ran the program with `App`'s own services. A file holds a test when its class isn't abstract and extends `PHPUnit.Framework.TestCase`, directly or through a parent.
- A compiled file goes stale when a module of its namespace or of a namespace above it changes, wherever the file is, spec section 32, as the container reads those modules. So an edit to `app/Module.sharp` makes stale the compiled program `tests/lib/Link.sharp` of `App.Testing.Lib`, so the engine refuses it until a compile checks its services against the edited module, where 0.6.51 kept it current, because a compiled file named only the `Module.sharp` of its own folder and of each folder above it, and a program named none for its services. A module added to a folder that holds a file of the namespace or of a namespace above it, or to that folder's PSR-4 parent, makes the file stale too. A file added beside a module that isn't one makes nothing stale.
- `mago compile` with an extension host enabled writes the `.sharp` folder from the analysis without the hosts, as a whole `mago analyze` and `mago --no-extensions compile` write it, spec section 27, and the hosts' rules only report. So `mago compile` then a whole `mago analyze` writes no file under `.sharp`, and neither does the reverse order, where 0.6.51 rewrote `.sharp/compiled.bin` and `.sharp/inputs.json` on each, because `mago compile` compiled with the hosts and recorded a configuration that named them, and a file that compiles only with a type a host gives was written by `mago compile` and deleted by the next whole `mago analyze`. A host rule's error still fails `mago compile`, as "The extension hosts reported 1 error in PHP# files: fix it and run vendor/bin/mago compile again.", and its file compiles, where 0.6.51 refused it. A `mago compile` with hosts that finds nothing to compile still runs the hosts' analysis to report, and lowers nothing.
- An `extern` property's attributes are checked as any property's are, spec sections 1 and 14.3, so a database entity whose `extern` property takes an attribute that holds a `new` of constants compiles, as `[Field(label: "Who", config: new Config(@default: Visibility.Public.value))]` on `public extern Visibility visibility { get; set; }`, where 0.6.51 refused the file with `lowering-panic`, "the analysis types every expression of a file the checker accepted", for a template that reads a class constant, an enum case's `value` and a map literal alike. An argument the attribute's parameter doesn't take is an error there, where 0.6.51 never checked it, and `[Key("total_cents")]` on an `extern` property names the key `__get` and `__set` receive, as before.
- A law over a method that tests one value with a chain of `is` tests in `?:` proves again within its time, spec section 28, as it did in 0.6.49. So `law anOfferNotStartedComesBeforeItsEnd(Instant now, Instant starts, Instant ends, int claims) when now < starts => OfferAvailability.at(OfferAvailability.of(true, true, starts, ends, null, claims, null, null), now) is OfferAvailability.NotStarted;` proves over an `at` that reads `status is OfferAvailability.Available available ? … : (status is OfferAvailability.SoldOut soldOut ? … : (status is OfferAvailability.Purchased purchased ? … : status))`, where 0.6.51 refused it with "the proof ran out of time before it finished". The tests of such a chain are one `match` on the value with one arm for each case, where 0.6.50 and 0.6.51 wrote the rest of the chain again for each case a test left out, so `at` took 45,107 characters of Lean where 0.6.49's took 1,466, and it takes 1,508 now.

### Added

- The `-dev` image holds the sampling profiler `excimer`, which a framework app's development image installs, so an app's `RUN install-php-extensions excimer` on it builds nothing, where on 0.6.51 the app's image built it from source on every release. Both images hold `bcmath`, `calendar`, `exif`, `ftp`, `gd`, `igbinary`, `imagick`, `intl`, `pcntl`, `pdo_mysql`, `pdo_pgsql`, `pgsql`, `redis`, `shmop`, `sockets`, `sysvmsg`, `sysvsem`, `sysvshm` and `zip` beside the extensions PHP builds by default, as 0.6.51's did, and the `-dev` image holds `excimer` too.
- An interface states laws, spec sections 22 and 28, and each PHP# class that implements it proves each of them, with the interface's values read as its own. So `interface Ledger { int balance { get; } Ledger credit(int amount); law creditAdds(Ledger l, int amount) when amount >= 0 => l.credit(amount).balance == l.balance + amount; }` compiles beside a `MemoryLedger : Ledger` whose `credit` adds `amount`, where 0.6.51 refused it with "An interface has no laws: state `creditAdds` in a class or an enum, whose values it is about.". An implementer that breaks a law is refused on its own name with "`MemoryLedger.credit` breaks `Ledger.creditAdds`.", and a plain PHP class that implements such an interface is refused with "`App.Books.FileLedger` is plain PHP, which can't prove `Ledger.creditAdds`. Write it in PHP#.". A law over an interface's values takes the interface's laws as facts and calls one in its body as it calls any law, as `Ledger.creditAdds(l, 0);`, and a call of the interface's methods reads only what its laws say. A call of a method of an interface that states no law keeps its refusal.
- With `MAGO_LOG=debug`, `mago analyze` and `mago compile` log how long each phase of the run took, as "Phase laws took 75.77 s.": `load`, `scan`, `populate`, `analysis`, `lowering`, `laws`, `inputs`, `write` and `report`, and `hosts` for the extension hosts' own part, the compile of their folder and the answers they give when they take an analysis over. So a cold compile of a 2,129-file project with 1,330 laws shows that its laws took 75.77 s of its 84.34 s, where 0.6.50 logged no line for that time.
- With `MAGO_LOG=debug`, the laws phase of `mago compile` logs how long each of its parts took: the `translation` phase, of the files the laws reach that kept their compiled file, then "Found the proof keys of … laws in … s.", "The law checker's library took … s to find or build.", "Wrote the Lean package of … generated modules in … s.", and, for each runner, "A runner proved … laws with the fixed steps in … s.". Then it logs each law's time in the runners, the slowest first, as "The law App.Shared.Stamp.formatsApart took 11.64 s of the provers' time.", where 0.6.51 logged only the laws phase's whole time. The runner times each law it judges and sends the time with its judgment, so the first compile with this release proves every law of the project again once, as a release that changes the law checker does.
- A law that stays unproven runs on small values, spec section 28, and a false law prints the first values that break it, each parameter as PHP# writes its value. So `law depositNeverShrinks(Account a, int amount) => a.deposit(amount).balance >= a.balance;` is refused with "fails for: a = new Account(0), amount = -1" under its claim still to show, where 0.6.50 printed only "still to show: new Account(a.balance + amount).balance >= a.balance" for it and for a true law alike. The values are the ints `0`, `1` and `-1`, both bools, the strings `""` and `"a"`, each case of an enum and of a machine, each implementer of a sealed interface, the values of a class `new` builds from such values of its constructor's parameters, a list of none, one and two of them, the empty `Map`, and `null` where the type takes it. A law no small value breaks is still refused, with "no small value breaks it, so it may be true: write a proof body that shows it". A law over code a law reads only as each implementation gives it, as an interface's values, runs on no values. A law whose proof closes elaborates no search, so a cold compile of a 2,128-file project whose 1,330 laws all prove takes 83.4 processor-seconds over five rounds, as 0.6.51 takes 83.4, where compiling each law's search took it to 123.8.

## [0.6.51] - 2026-11-22

### Added

- With `MAGO_LOG=debug`, `mago analyze` and `mago compile` log how long each extension host rule took in the analysis round, slowest first, as "Host rule App\Rules\NoRawQuery::analyze took 4.21 s over 1,204 files.", then the five slowest files of the slowest rule, as "Host rule App\Rules\NoRawQuery::analyze took 1.20 s on app/Reports/Ledger.sharp.". A rule is each hook, provider and issue filter a host registers, as "Host rule App\Types\Models::getReturnType took 2.10 s over 640 files.". Each round of `mago analyze --watch` logs its own, and `mago lint` logs its host linter rules the same way when it ends. A host's worker times its rules only when `mago` logs at that level, so a run at the default level prints and costs what it did in 0.6.50.
- A law reads the classes of a family, spec section 25: the cases of a machine, the cases of an enum whose cases carry data, and the implementers of a sealed interface. It reads `typeof` of one, `Class.of` of a value, `==` and `!=` on two, and a `Class<X>` a law or a method takes. So `law closingCloses() => Class.of(ClassOfRepro.enter().close()) == typeof(ClassOfRepro.Closed);` proves, and so does a law over every pair `Class<ClassOfRepro> from, Class<ClassOfRepro> to` of what a method taking the two answers, where 0.6.50 refused them with "Law closingCloses reaches the plain PHP class Class", "reaches a type test" and "reaches `==` on values only the running code tells apart". A false law, as `Class.of(ClassOfRepro.enter()) == typeof(ClassOfRepro.Closed)`, is not proven, with its claim still to show. A false law's counterexample names each class and each implementer as PHP# writes it, as "when from == typeof(ClassOfRepro.Open) && to == typeof(ClassOfRepro.Open)" and "when status is Waiting", where 0.6.50 wrote "status is Status.Waiting" for an implementer of a sealed interface. `Class<T>` of any other class keeps its refusal.
- A law reads `isEmpty()` and `isNotEmpty()` of a `List`, `filter` of a lambda on a `Map`, spec section 12, and a string template, spec section 18, whose `string` parts join as they are and whose `int` parts join as the text PHP# writes for them. So a method that keeps a `Map<string, string?>`'s missing settings with `settings.filter(value => value == null)`, tests them with `isNotEmpty()` and returns `` `Missing setting ${missing.keys()[0]}, ${missing.count()} in all` `` proves `law aMissingValueIsNeverActive(Map<string, string?> settings, string key) when settings.has(key) && settings.get(key) == null => !Settings.active(settings);`, where 0.6.50 refused it with "reaches the Map method filter" and "reaches a string template", and `isEmpty()` of a filtered `List` proves what its `count() == 0` proves, where 0.6.50 refused it with "reaches the List method isEmpty". A template with a part of any other type, as a `float`, keeps its refusal. A law compares two `Map`s with `==` as spec section 19 does: as many entries, and each key of one a key of the other whose value is `==`, by the value's own `operator ==` where its class declares one. So `law keepsItsRequirements(Map<string, Requirement> requirements) => Readiness.active(requirements).requirements == requirements;` proves, where 0.6.50 refused it with "reaches a collection", and a false law, as `a == b` over any two maps, is not proven, with its claim still to show. The refusal of a collection a law cannot read names `isEmpty()` and `isNotEmpty()` among what it reads.
- `Property.isExtern` holds for a property its class declares `extern`, which the plain PHP parent's `__get` and `__set` serve, spec sections 14.3 and 25, so a storage layer picks an entity's columns out of `properties()` with `typeof(Invoice).properties().filter(property => property.isExtern)`. A property the class declares otherwise, and one its plain PHP parent declares, give false. 0.6.50 listed the `extern` properties with nothing to tell them from the others.

### Changed

- The runtime image holds the extensions a framework app's image installs, so an upgrade builds none of them again: every extension of PHP's own source that links no outside library, `calendar`, `exif`, `ftp`, `pcntl`, `shmop`, `sockets`, `sysvmsg`, `sysvsem` and `sysvshm`, beside `bcmath`, `intl` and `zip`, the database drivers `pdo_mysql`, `pdo_pgsql` and `pgsql`, and `gd`, `imagick`, and `redis` with `igbinary` as its serializer. An app's `RUN install-php-extensions exif gd imagick pcntl pdo_pgsql pgsql redis` can go, and a line that keeps them builds nothing. `xsl`, `gettext`, `ffi` and the development extensions stay the app's to install. The compressed image grows from 256.5 MB to 309.5 MB on linux/amd64 and from 246.8 MB to 297.3 MB on linux/arm64.
- A whole `mago analyze` whose extension hosts register return-type providers and nothing else reports from its compile's own analysis: the compile records each return-type request a host's provider targets, the hosts answer them, and only the files an answer types are analyzed again with the hosts, where 0.6.50 loaded, scanned, populated and analyzed every file a second time with them. The hosts start beside the compile, which reads what they register before it analyzes. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project whose host answers return types takes 4.78 CPU-seconds where it took 6.61, and peaks at 647 MiB where it peaked at 729, lower in each of 16 alternating rounds, with the same issues and `.sharp` folder. A project whose host also registers a codebase scan hook records nothing and runs as before, at 35.57 G instructions where it took 35.62 and 7.35 CPU-seconds where it took 7.37.

### Fixed

- A member's name never hides a name the law checker writes for a class's own code, whatever the member is named, so `null` stays `null` beside a static `none()`. A machine with `case Held(int id)`, `public static OptionalCaseFieldRepro.Empty none()` and a method that returns a bound case's id or `null`, with an `if` or with `? :`, proves `law noneHoldsNoId(int unused) => OptionalCaseFieldRepro.none().heldId() == null;` and `law heldHoldsItsId(int id) => OptionalCaseFieldRepro.Held(id).heldId() == id;`, where 0.6.50 refused every law of the class with "the checker translated the code it reaches wrongly". A false law, as `OptionalCaseFieldRepro.none().heldId() == 1`, is not proven, with its claim still to show.

- A law reads a computed property, spec section 6, written `=>` or with a `get` body, on a class and on a machine alike, as the method of no arguments it runs as, and a machine's cases read the machine's. So `law onlyALitLightIsOn(int level) => Light.enter().turnOn(level).isOn && !Light.enter().isOn;` proves for `internal bool isOn => match (this) { Light.On => true, Light.Off => false };`, where 0.6.50 refused it with "the checker left out Light.On.isOn", and `law brightIsOverSixty(int watts) => new Lamp(watts).bright == (watts > 60);` proves for `public bool bright => this.watts > 60;`, where 0.6.50 refused it with "Law brightIsOverSixty reaches the accessor of Lamp.bright". An invariant of a case reads it too. A false law, as `Light.enter().isOn`, is not proven, with its claim still to show. A `virtual` computed property reads as each override gives it, so a law proves only what holds for every override. A property with a `set` body keeps its refusal.

- `Property.set` of an `extern` property checks the value against the property's type before the plain PHP parent's `__set` runs, as it checks any property's, spec section 25. So `Property.set(row, "not a number")` on `[Key("total_cents")] public extern int totalCents { get; set; }` throws "Cannot assign string to property App\Row::$totalCents of type int" and `__set` never runs, where 0.6.50 passed the string to `__set`, which stored it in the `total_cents` column. A value the type takes reaches `__set` with its key as before.

- A method a plain PHP trait gives a PHP# class that breaks the signature of an abstract method of the class's parent is reported at the class's name with "Declared in `Greeting` here." beside the trait's method, as one that breaks an interface's method is, where 0.6.50 named no trait. A report at the class's name no longer repeats its place in an "In class `Greeter`" note.

## [0.6.50] - 2026-11-22

### Upgrading from 0.6.49

- Make each effect class extend an abstract one: declare its members in a `public effect abstract class`, and the real class as `public effect class SystemStore : Store`. The standard library's effects are abstract too, so plain PHP that wrote `new Sharp\Net\Http()` writes `new Sharp\Net\SystemHttp()`. Replace `Clock.fixed(instant)` with `new FakeClock(instant)` and `new Random(seed)` with `new FakeRandom(seed)`, from `Sharp.Testing`.
- Give each plain PHP call a module binding makes an `extern` line, as a method's call needs one.
- List your test folder under `autoload-dev` in `composer.json`, or each test module in it is refused.
- Move each class production code uses out of your `autoload-dev` folders: production code can't use a test file's class.
- Count `missing-extern` where a baseline or a script counted `incompatible-effects` for plain PHP no `extern` line declares: that refusal has its own code now.

### Changed

- A watch round after an edit that deletes, renames or moves a class analyzes again only the files that name the class, read a class it extended or implemented, or declare a class named like its namespace, where 0.6.49 analyzed the whole project again. Renaming one class of a 2,128-file project under `mago analyze --watch` takes 0.82 CPU-seconds a round where it took 7.44, with the same issues.
- A test file, one of a folder `composer.json` lists under `autoload-dev`, is a leaf of the project, spec sections 5, 23 and 32. It still declares the namespace it tests and reaches its `internal` members. Its imports are no edges of the namespace graph, so `tests/A/ThingTest.sharp` in `App.A` imports `App.B.User` while `App.B` imports `App.A`, where 0.6.49 refused both files with "`App.A` and `App.B` import each other". A module's `imports` line limits only its namespaces' production files, so a test file in `App.Shared.Entitlements` imports `Tests.Lib.TenantTestCase`, where 0.6.49 refused it with "`App.Shared.Entitlements` imports only `App.Shared.Ledger`." Production code can't use a class a test file declares, by an import or by a name of its own namespace: `new FakeOwners()` in `app/A/Billing.sharp`, beside a `FakeOwners` in `tests/A/`, fails with "Production code can't use `FakeOwners`, a test file's class.", where 0.6.49 compiled it.
- Every effect is an abstract boundary, spec section 29, so a unit test runs the real class that holds an effect over a fake. A project declares its effect as `public effect abstract class Store` with its members, its real implementation as `public effect class RedisStore : Store`, which only a module binding creates, and a fake as `public class FakeStore : Store`, which any code creates with `new`. Code has the effect by the type it holds, so a class takes a `Store`. 0.6.49 accepted a concrete `effect class` with no boundary, and `public effect class RedisStore` alone now fails with "`RedisStore` is an effect class, so it extends an abstract effect class that declares its members." Each of the standard library's twelve effects is abstract, with its real implementation beside it as `SystemHttp`, `SystemClock` and the rest, which `Sharp/Module.sharp` binds, so plain PHP that wrote `new Sharp\Net\Http()` writes `new Sharp\Net\SystemHttp()`. `Sharp.Testing` holds a fake of each, as `new FakeHttp([response])`, which keeps each request in `sent`. `Clock.fixed(instant)` is `new FakeClock(instant)`, which `advance(duration)` and `sleep` move on, and `new Random(seed)` is `new FakeRandom(seed)`.
- A compile lets go of what the analysis kept for lowering each file no law reaches once the laws are translated, where 0.6.49 held it for every file until the compile ended, though only the laws' refusals read it after that. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project peaks at 860 MiB where it peaked at 871 MiB, lower in each of 16 alternating rounds, with the same CPU, instructions and output.
- Plain PHP no `extern` line declares fails with its own code, `missing-extern`, spec section 29, so a project counts the lines it still has to write apart from real effect conflicts. "`Mailchimp` has no `extern` line." keeps its message and its help, where 0.6.49 reported it as `incompatible-effects`.
- A one-part `extern` target names a class, as any name where a class goes does, spec section 29. So `extern Printer uses none;` in `App.Shop` declares the plain PHP `App.Shop.Printer` with no import, and the help for a call of it gives that line, where 0.6.49 read the bare name as a function and its help asked for `import App.Shop.Printer as ShopPrinter;`. A bare name that names no class, as `extern now uses Clock;`, fails with "Cannot find class, interface, enum, or type alias `now`.", where 0.6.49 said "Function `now` does not exist.".
- A function plain PHP gives, in a `Function<…>` result, argument, local or property, is refused with the `extern` line that states its type, as a call of it is, spec section 21: "`Legacy.doubler()` gives a function whose types plain PHP doesn't declare, so it is no `Function<int(int)>`: state its type on the `extern` line of `Legacy.doubler`, as in `extern Function<int(int)> Legacy.doubler() uses none;`." A property has no line of its own, so a property's function names the line of a plain PHP method that returns it. 0.6.49 said to "hold it with `const`, and check what each call gives", and a call of that local is refused.

### Fixed

- A PHP# class that uses a plain PHP trait is checked in its own file, so a whole-tree `mago analyze` of models beside a vendor package passes. A plain PHP parent's `__get` and `__set` store the properties the class declares, spec section 14.3, so beside `public class Repro : Model, SoftDeletes`, the trait's `protected $forceDeleting` is the trait's own field. 0.6.49 reported it in the vendor file, once for each model that used the trait: "`Repro`'s parent `Model` stores properties through `__get` and `__set`, so a protected `forceDeleting` would never be saved." A trait's member narrower than an interface the class implements fails at the class's name, naming the trait, as "`Tagged` gives `label` as `protected`, from `HasLabel`, and `Labelled` states it `public`." for a trait's `protected $label` under `string label { get; }`, and so does a trait's `protected function greet()` under `string greet();`. 0.6.49 reported each in the trait's file, which `mago compile` doesn't check, so it compiled the class and PHP refused to load it with "Access level to Tagged::$label must be public (as in class Labelled)".
- A law reads a property that has no storage of its own, an interface's `{ get; }` and an abstract property alike, through a reader that each implementation answers, so a law proves what holds for every implementation. So beside `public interface IntegrationFailure { string value { get; } }` and a machine's `final case Inactive(IntegrationFailure failure)`, `law anInactiveIntegrationHoldsItsFailure(IntegrationFailure failure) => Readiness.inactive(failure).failure.value == failure.value;` proves, and so do the same read through `?.` and in a machine's invariant, where 0.6.49 refused each with "the checker translated the code it reaches wrongly". A law that claims `failure.value == "timeout"` for every failure is not proven. An abstract class's `public abstract string label { get; }` is no field of the class's value, so a subclass's `public override string label { get; init; } = "stripe";` reads as its own, where 0.6.49 gave the subclass two fields named `label` and refused its laws the same way.
- A law follows a nullable field through `is int` and `?:` after any move, as it follows one that takes no `null`. So beside `case Running(Cursor cursor, int position)`, whose `prepare(Map<string, int> arguments)` builds `new Cursor(this.cursor.step, arguments, this.cursor.marks)` from a cursor's `int? step`, `law preparingKeepsTheStep(Cursor cursor, int position, Map<string, int> arguments) => Prepared.Running(cursor, position).prepare(arguments).cursor.step is int at ? (cursor.step is int was ? at == was : false) : cursor.step is null;` proves, and so does the same law after a move that takes no `Map`, where 0.6.49 refused both with "law preparingKeepsTheStep is not proven" and the whole claim still to show. A law that claims the step grows by one is still not proven, with its claim still to show.
- `Class<T>.properties()` lists each `extern` property a class declares, spec section 25, with its type, its access, the access of its `set` and its attributes, its `[Key]` among them, after the class's stored properties, so a storage layer reads an Eloquent model's columns, casts and keys through it. 0.6.49 listed none, so on a model whose columns are all `extern` properties it gave only `Model`'s own properties. `Property.get` and `Property.set` of one call the plain PHP parent's `__get` and `__set` with its key, as the property's own read and write do, `getter()` and `setter()` give null, `isInitialized` holds, and `fields()` lists none, since an `extern` property holds no value. PHP still declares no property of its name, so plain PHP's `$row->total_cents`, `isset` and `unset` reach `__get`, `__isset` and `__unset` as before.
- A write of an `extern` property reaches the plain PHP parent's `__set` with its key whatever members the class declares, spec section 14.3, so beside `internal string? a => this.stored;`, `[Key("a")] internal extern string? stored { get; set; }` stores `row.stored = "written"` under `a`. 0.6.49 wrote the key as a property name, so the class's own `a` took the write or refused it as read-only, and the parent stored nothing. Each read, write, compound assignment, `++`, `--` and `??=` of an `extern` property calls `__get` and `__set`, its receiver running once, so a PHP# read and `??=` call `__get` alone where 0.6.49 called `__isset` first.
- A module binding's expression and a class constant's value are checked for effects as a method's body is, spec section 29. So beside `extern Remote.fetch uses Http;` and a `Holder` that takes a `Function<int(int) uses Clock>`, `singleton Holder = new Holder((int id) => Remote.fetch(id));` fails with "This lambda calls `Remote.fetch`, which has the effect `Http`, so a `Function<int(int) uses Clock>` cannot hold it.", as the same expression in a method does, and so do `new Holder(Remote.fetch)`, a constant `Function<int(int) uses Clock> RATE = (int id) => Remote.fetch(id)`, and plain PHP a binding reaches with no `extern` line. 0.6.49 compiled each of them. A lambda a binding or a constant builds also answers `is Function<…>` at run time as a method's lambda does, where 0.6.49 gave it no function type, so it failed every one.
- A program's constructor is checked for services as a bound class's is, spec sections 27 and 32, since the engine builds a program's class through the container before it calls `main`. So a `MainProbe` in `Tests.Lib` that takes an `Application`, beside `singleton Application = Application;` only in `App`'s module, fails with "`MainProbe` needs an `Application`, and no module binds one: the container can't build `MainProbe`." 0.6.49 compiled it, and running it failed with `No module binds …`. A test module in a file no `autoload-dev` folder holds, as `module : App { }` in a program's file, fails with "A test module belongs in the test file that uses it.", where 0.6.49 compiled it and it did nothing.
- `Method.createDelegate` holds a plain PHP method to the `extern` lines the checker holds a call of it to, spec sections 25 and 29: the method's own line, else the line of the class that declares it, and never a parent's. So beside `extern Base uses none;`, `extern Sub uses Http;` and `extern Sub.later uses any;`, the inherited `run` of a `Sub` fits a pure `Function<int(Sub)>`, `later` fails a `Function<int(Sub) uses Http>` with "may have every effect", and the `own` method of a `Leaf extends Base` that no line declares fails a pure function type the same way. 0.6.49 read `Sub`'s line for `run`, `Sub`'s `uses Http` for `later`, and `Base`'s line for `own`. A method a class takes from a plain PHP trait takes the trait's lines, as the checker reads them, so beside `extern Courier uses none;` and `extern Delivers uses Http;`, the `deliver` a `Courier` takes from `Delivers` fails a pure `Function<int(Courier)>` with "has the effect `Sharp.Net.Http`", where 0.6.49 read `Courier`'s line and made the delegate.
- A law follows an enum's case through a test that names one case, as it follows the other cases where the test fails, since each test of an enum, a machine and a sealed interface names every case and every implementer, and leaves no value to a catch-all. So beside `public enum Phase { case Idle; case Busy(int job); case Failed(string reason); }`, a `struct Slot` that holds a `Phase`, and a machine case whose `advance()` builds `new Slot(this.slot.phase, this.slot.count)`, `law advancingKeepsThePhase(Slot slot, int position) => Worker.Running(slot, position).advance().slot.phase is Phase.Busy(int at) ? (slot.phase is Phase.Busy(int was) ? at == was : false) : slot.phase is Phase.Idle || (slot.phase is Phase.Failed(string reason) ? true : false);` proves, and so does the same law after a move that copies the slot, where 0.6.49 refused both with "law advancingKeepsThePhase is not proven" and the whole claim still to show. `slot.phase is Phase.Idle` tests a case that carries no data beside cases that do, where 0.6.49 refused it with "a match on this subject". A law that claims the job grows by one is still not proven, with its claim still to show, and so is a law whose claim Lean computes to `false`, such as `Pending.start() is Paid` for a move that gives `Pending`.
- A law reads that each value of a class whose constructor only checks its arguments passed those checks, spec section 28, wherever the law reads the value: a value it takes, a value a field of one holds, and a value a machine case holds when a move starts. So `law laterIsAfter(Instant at) => at.plus(Duration.ofMinutes(5)) > at;` proves, and so does `law nanosecondInRange(Slot slot) => slot.at.nanosecond <= 999_999_999;` for a `struct Slot` that holds an `Instant`, where 0.6.49 refused them with "law laterIsAfter is not proven" and "law nanosecondInRange is not proven". An invariant `clock.now >= clock.start` holds after a move that adds `Duration.ofSeconds(1)` to `now`, where 0.6.49 refused it with "`nowNeverBeforeStart` breaks after `tick()`" when its proof ran out of time. A law reads `/` and `%` as the division and remainder they compute. A law's call of a law shows that each value it passes keeps its precondition, as it shows the called law's `when` condition. A law that is false for a value the constructor accepts, as `at.plus(Duration.ofMinutes(5)) == at`, is still not proven, with its claim still to show.

## [0.6.49] - 2026-11-22

### Upgrading from 0.6.48

- Rename each `foreign class` to `effect class`: `foreign` is no longer a modifier, so a class declared with it stops compiling.

### Changed

- An effect class is declared `effect class`. 0.6.48 spelled it `foreign class`. `effect` is a modifier only among a class's modifiers, spec section 29, so a parameter, a local or a property may be named `effect`, and `foreign` is a name like any other, so `public foreign class Store` fails as any unknown word before `class` does. The codes `foreign-in-static` and `foreign-outside-module` are `effect-object-in-static` and `effect-object-outside-module`.

### Fixed

- A machine's invariant proves from every invariant of the case a move or an entry point starts from, spec section 20.2, whichever case it gives. So `public Trail.Done finish() => Trail.Done(this.ids);` in `case Open(List<int> ids)`, beside `law openHoldsOne(Trail.Open o) => o.ids.count() > 0;` and `law doneHoldsOne(Trail.Done d) => d.ids.count() > 0;`, proves, where 0.6.48 refused it with "`doneHoldsOne` breaks after `finish()`". 0.6.48 assumed those invariants, but read what one computes only when it was the invariant it proved. A move that breaks an invariant is still refused with the moves that break it.
- A law reads a nullable `int` with `??` and `==` as the integer it holds, whichever branch built it. So beside `final case Open(int? left)`, which `public static Spots of(int? cap, int claims)` builds as `Spots.Open(cap - claims)` when `claims < cap`, `law leftIsNeverNegative(Spots.Open open) => (open.left ?? 0) >= 0;` and `law openCountsItsSpots(int cap, int claims) when claims < cap && claims >= 0 => Spots.of(cap, claims) is Spots.Open open ? open.left == cap - claims : false;` prove, where 0.6.48 refused them with "`leftIsNeverNegative` breaks after `of(cap, claims)`" and "law openCountsItsSpots is not proven".
- A law reads the fields of a machine with one case as it reads those of a machine with more, so every law that proves for a machine with two cases proves for the same machine with one. So beside `final case Open(int? left)` alone, which `public static Spaces of(int n) => n >= 0 ? Spaces.Open(n) : Spaces.Open(null);` builds, `law ofLeavesNoneNegative(int n) => Spaces.of(n) is Spaces.Open open ? (open.left is int left ? left >= 0 : true) : true;` proves, where 0.6.48 refused it with "law ofLeavesNoneNegative is not proven", and so do a `??` and an `==` on the field.

## [0.6.48] - 2026-11-22

### Added

- `typeof(T).encode(value)` gives the stored form of `value` as a value, spec section 10, what `Json.encode` writes as text: each JSON object a `Map`, each JSON array a `List` and each scalar itself, with every field `parse` reads at any visibility. `typeof(T).decode` reads it back, so `typeof(T).decode(typeof(T).encode(x)) == x`, and a storage layer in another namespace stores a class's `internal` fields, which its own `Field.get` can't read. A `Map` field that `decode` reads from a `Map` reads each key by its text, as it reads a JSON object's, so `["7": "top"]` gives a `Map<int, string>` the key `7`, which 0.6.47 refused with "shelves[\"7\"]: key expected int, got string '7'".

### Changed

- The law checker that proves a law with its fixed steps imports no more of Lean's own library than `Init`, where 0.6.47 also imported 111 modules of `Std` and `Lean` into every such check, which only the attribute that marked a law's statement read. A law's statement is the one definition of the project's code whose type is a `Prop`, so the generated code marks it with nothing. The check of the law a one-file edit adds to a 2,128-file project retires 2.41 G instructions where it retired 2.69 G, in 0.41 s of CPU where it took 0.46 s, and peaks at 410 MiB where it peaked at 473 MiB, medians of 20 alternating rounds, with the same answer. Every law of that project proves as before.

### Fixed

- A computed property whose type is a machine compiles at any visibility, spec section 20.2, since it stores nothing a write could change, so `public OrderStatus progress => this.status;` beside `public OrderStatus status { get; private set; }` compiles. 0.6.47 refused it with "`Order.progress` holds the machine `OrderStatus`, so its `set` is `private`." A property that stores a machine still has a `private set`.
- An entry point of a machine that takes a value of the machine keeps the machine's invariants from that value, spec sections 20.2 and 28, as a move keeps them from the case it moves from, since only entry points and moves build a case. So `public static Run at(Run status, Instant now) => status is Run.Open open ? (now >= open.idleAt ? Run.Idle(open.ids) : open) : status;` beside `law anOpenRunHoldsAnId(Run.Open open) => open.ids.count() > 0;` proves, where 0.6.47 refused it with "`anOpenRunHoldsAnId` breaks after `at(status, now)`". An invariant an entry point or a move leaves unproven names the goal still to show, as "= when status is Run.Open, still to show: open.ids.count() > 0".
- The search for the moves that break a machine's invariant passes an entry point or a move a value of the machine only when the value keeps the invariants of its case, spec section 20.2, since a program holds no other. So `public static Count at(Count c) => c;` beside `law positive(Count.Counting counting) => counting.n > 0;` proves, where 0.6.47 refused it with "`positive` breaks after `at(c: Count.Counting(n: 0)) → Counting(n: 0)`". A move that breaks the invariant from a value that keeps it is still refused with the moves that break it.
- PHP's `elseif` in a `.sharp` file, which fails as a typo does with "Unexpected `elseif`", gets the help "Write `else if`.", the one way PHP# writes an else-if, where 0.6.47 gave no help. A law's translation refuses an `if` with an else-if clause as a construct it doesn't model, where it would have translated the `if` without the clause's branch.

## [0.6.47] - 2026-11-22

### Upgrading from 0.6.46

- A class's method named `main` makes the class a program, spec section 27, so a `main` that isn't `public int main()` or `public void main()` stops compiling with "A class's `main` runs it as a program: declare it `public int main()` or `public void main()`." Rename such a method. A `#!` line anywhere but a `.sharp` file's first line stops compiling too, where 0.6.46 read it as a `#` comment.
- `Sharp.Security.Password` is gone. Keep a password as a `Hashed<string>` field, `new Hashed<string>(password)`, and check one with `hashed.verify(password)` in place of `Password.verify(password, hash)`. A plain PHP hash from `Password.hash` stays a `string` that `global.password_verify` checks.
- A PHP# class whose plain PHP parent's `__get` or `__set` serves a property, as an Eloquent model's does, reaches it only through an `extern` property or a line of the library that declares it, so a read or a write of any other name stops compiling. Declare each column the class reads or writes, as `public extern string email { get; set; }`, with `[Key("total_cents")]` where the key differs from the property's name, and make each property that isn't `extern` private, or declare it `extern`.

### Added

- A `.sharp` file whose class declares `public int main()` or `public void main()` runs as a program, spec section 27. `php app/Console/Import.sharp data.csv` runs it, and so does `./app/Console/Import.sharp data.csv` through a first-line `#!/usr/bin/env php`. When PHP runs the file as its script, the CLI's script, a server's script or `auto_prepend_file`, the engine loads the project's Composer autoloader, builds the class through the container, so its constructor takes `Environment`, `Console`, `Process` or any service a module binds, and calls `main`. An `int` it returns is the process's exit code, and a `void` `main` returns to PHP, so a prepend runs before the script. A file that `include` or the autoloader loads only declares its class, as before. `mago compile` writes `.sharp/autoload.php`, which requires the autoloader of the vendor folder `composer.json`'s `config.vendor-dir` names, else `vendor`. A program whose project has none is refused with "app/Console/Import.sharp runs as a program, and .sharp/autoload.php, which loads the project's Composer autoloader, is missing. Run vendor/bin/mago compile." A program's class and its `main` count as used, so an application's `public class Import` is neither unused nor too wide.
- `Secret<T>` and `Hashed<T>` in `Sharp.Security`, spec section 24, keep a password, a token or a key out of plain sight. A `Secret` holds its value in memory, `value` gives it back, and its stored form, which `Json.encode` and `serialize` write, is `{"encrypted":"…"}`: the value encrypted with XChaCha20-Poly1305 under the newest key of the key ring `SHARP_KEYS`, bound to the type and field that hold it, so text moved into another field never decrypts, and text under an older key still decrypts once a newer key is put first. `vendor/bin/mago generate-key` prints a new key. A `Hashed` keeps only the Argon2id hash of its value, `verify(value)` checks one, and its stored form is `{"hashed":"$argon2id$…"}`. A field reads the plain value too, as `{"password":"hunter2"}`. Both show as `**********` in a template and in `var_dump`. `hashed.value` is a compile error, "A `Hashed<string>` gives no value back: check one with `verify(value)`.", and so is `==` between two. A missing `SHARP_KEYS` throws `RuntimeException`, and a `Secret` outside a field, as `Json.encode(new Secret<string>("x"))`, throws the `Error` a type with no stored form throws.
- An `extern` property has `set` where the class inherits a plain PHP `__set`, spec section 14.3, so `public extern string email { get; set; }` on an Eloquent model writes the column through `__set`, checked against `string`, and `{ get; private set; }` lets only the class's own methods write it. `[Key("total_cents")]` names the key `__get` and `__set` receive, so `public extern int totalCents { get; set; }` reaches the column `total_cents`. 0.6.46 refused any `extern` property but `{ get; }` with "An `extern` property is the one plain PHP's `__get` gives, so it is get-only". One with `set` on a class that inherits no `__set` is refused with "`Auction.buyer` has `set`, which a plain PHP `__set` stores, and `Auction` inherits no `__set`."
- A law reads a `Map`, spec section 28: its literal and spreads, `count()`, `isEmpty()`, `isNotEmpty()`, `get`, `has`, `keys()`, `values()`, an index read and a loop over its entries, each modeled as a function of the map's entries in their order, with each key once. So `law everyPlanBoundsEveryLimit(Plan plan, Limit limit) => (plan.limits().get(limit) ?? 0) >= 1;` over a `public Map<Limit, int> limits()` proves when every plan's literal bounds every limit by at least 1, and is reported as not proven when one gives 0. A law about a value that holds a `Map`, as a machine's case or a struct built with `new Cursor(null, [:], [])` or taken as a parameter, proves over the value's other properties too. 0.6.46 refused each with "Law … reaches a collection". Another member, as `toList()`, is refused with "Read a Map with `count()`, `isEmpty()`, `isNotEmpty()`, `get`, `has`, `keys()`, `values()`, an index or a loop over its entries."
- A law compares two `List`s with `==`, spec section 28, as many elements on each side and each pair by the element's own `==`, so `law marksCompare(List<Mark> marks) => marks == marks;` proves, and so does `==` on two structs with a `List<Mark>` field. `[a] == [b]` on a class whose `operator ==` compares only amounts runs that operator, so a law that two such lists hold equal currencies is reported as not proven. 0.6.46 refused each with "reaches the class operator `==`". An edit to the element's `operator ==` now analyzes again each file that compares two `List`s, `Set`s or `Map`s of that type.
- A law builds a value whose constructor body only checks its arguments and throws, spec section 28, and the checks are a precondition of the value. So `law builds(int unused) => new Instant(1, 0).epochSeconds == 1;` proves, and so does `new Instant(s, n).nanosecond <= 999_999_999`, and a law over `Instant.plus`, as `SetupStatus.Installing(at).expiresAt() == at.plus(Duration.ofMinutes(15))`, proves too. 0.6.46 refused each with "reaches the body of the constructor of Instant in new Instant". A constructor body that does anything else is refused with "Write the constructor with promoted properties and a body that only checks its arguments and throws, and compute the values in a static method that calls `new`." Three faults that law reached are fixed with it: a `?:` or `match` that gives a value on one side and `null` on the other, a law in a file whose code reaches the standard library's `Int`, and a law that runs one method twice on the same values each made "the checker translated the code it reaches wrongly" or a proof that ran out of time.
- A law reaches `Instant.toString()`, spec section 28, which writes its UTC text, as "2026-10-22T18:30:00.250Z", in PHP# with no plain PHP and no zone's rules, and `(string)` of an int, which the checker reads as its decimal digits. So `law formats(int s, int n) => new Instant(s, n).toString() != "";` is checked, and reported as not proven where Lean's search runs out of time. 0.6.46 refused it with "reaches `TimeZone.rules`, which changes `TimeZone.rulesByName[this.name]`". The text each `Instant`, `Date` and `Duration` gives is the same.

### Changed

- A compile writes each compiled file as soon as it makes the file's bytes, on every core, where 0.6.46 held the bytes of every file it rewrote, in the `.sharp` folder and in the extension hosts' folder, until it had made them all. `compiled.bin` and the compile's record are still written after the last compiled file. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project, made in place, peaks at 864 MiB in `mago analyze` where it peaked at 890 MiB, medians of 16 alternating rounds, every round lower, with the same instructions, compiled files and issues.
- A `mago analyze` with an extension host reads each `vendor/` file Composer's autoload maps serve once, where 0.6.46 read each one from the disk again for the analysis with the hosts after the compile's analysis had read it. The analysis with the hosts serves the file the compile's served, the same file at the same path. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project, made in place, retires 39.9 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 40.6 G, medians of 8 alternating rounds, with the same compiled files and issues.
- A `mago analyze` with an extension host parses and scans each file once, where 0.6.46 did it twice: once for the compile's analysis, and again for the analysis with the hosts, for every file of the project and each `vendor/` file a scan reaches. The analysis with the hosts takes the compile's scan of each file it holds unchanged, by its name, path, type and bytes, scanned with the same PHP version and parser settings, and still scans again each file that names a class a host's stub declares in place of a default class. Nothing is kept past the run. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 45.7 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 49.4 G, medians of 8 alternating rounds, and its first run 55.9 G where it retired 59.1 G, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues. Peak memory on the edit is 1,000 MiB where it was 913 MiB.
- A compile stamps the listener table's inputs from the bytes its load read, once per run, where 0.6.46 read every PHP# file of the project from the disk again, listing its folder to check its name, and did all of it a second time for the extension hosts' folder. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 50.0 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 53.0 G, medians of 8 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A read or a write of a property only a plain PHP parent's `__get` or `__set` serves is a compile error in a PHP# class, spec section 14.3, where 0.6.46 read it as `Any?` and wrote any value, so a misspelled column compiled. `this.emial = "x"` on an `Order : Model` gives "`Order` has no property `emial`. Declare it: `public extern string emial { get; set; }`.", and `order.anything` gives the same with `…` for the type, which a read doesn't state. A property the library declares, as a real property or an `@property` line, reads as before, and a plain PHP class used directly keeps its `__get` properties. A property that isn't `extern` and isn't private, on a class whose plain PHP parent defines `__get` and `__set`, is refused too, since it would stay in memory and never be saved: "`Order`'s parent `Model` stores properties through `__get` and `__set`, so a public `name` would never be saved." with the help "Write `public extern string name { get; set; }`, or make it private to keep it in memory." A property the parent declares, as Eloquent's `table`, is the parent's and compiles as before.

### Fixed

- `.sharp/preload.php` requires the Composer autoloader of the vendor folder `composer.json`'s `config.vendor-dir` names, through `.sharp/autoload.php`, spec section 27, so a project with `"vendor-dir": "lib"` preloads. 0.6.46 always required `vendor/autoload.php`, and the preload failed with "Failed opening required".
- An `[Import]` method whose result is a function type, alone or nullable, gives the native closure its built-in returns as it is, spec section 23, as an `extern` result of a built-in does, since PHP never declares a closure's types. So with `[Import("set_error_handler")] public static Function<bool?(int, string, string, int)>? swap(Function<bool?(int, string, string, int)>? handler) uses PhpGlobals;`, `Errors.swap(h)` gives back the very handler plain PHP installed, as `fn (...$arguments) => true`, where 0.6.46 gave a new closure that wrapped it and checked each result. An `[Import]` result of any other type is checked as before, as `List<int>` is.
- A type test that names nothing, where it narrows the reads after it, makes the local it tests the value its type holds when it matches, spec section 21, so each read the test narrows reads the `Map` or the `Set` the type names. It narrows them as the condition of an `if`, an `else if`, a `while` or a `? :`, as the left side of `&&` or of `||` wherever it sits, and as a `match` arm, and so does a test under `!`, `is not`, `&&` or `||` in one of those. So `return value is not Map<string, int> || value.count() == 0;` gives `false` for `["a" => 1]`. `value is Map<string, int> ? value.keys().join(",") : "none"` gives `a` for a plain PHP array `["a" => 1]`, where 0.6.46 threw "Sharp\Internal\keys(): Argument #1 ($table) must be of type Sharp\Map|Sharp\Set, array given", and so do an arm `Map<string, int> => value.keys().join(",")` of a `match (value)` and the guard `if (value is not Map<string, int>) { return "none"; } return value.keys().join(",");`. A test that fails leaves the local as it was, and the local keeps the converted value after the test, so a change made through a narrowed read, as `value.add(2)`, reaches it. A test whose result is only a value, as a list's element or an argument, narrows nothing and leaves the local as it was, so `[value is Map<string, int>, value is List<int>]` gives `[true, true]` for an empty plain PHP array.
- A machine's move named like a member that changes a collection, as `remove`, `clear` or `removeLast`, is a move, spec section 20.2. So `this.slot = held.remove();` compiles after `this.slot is Slot.OnFile held` and in a `match` arm `Slot.OnFile held`, where 0.6.46 refused it with "`this.slot` holds the machine `Slot`, so it changes only by a move of its own", as it refused no move of another name. A call on a case's value never changes the name that holds it, since only a collection's extension takes its receiver by `ref`, spec section 26.

## [0.6.46] - 2026-11-21

### Upgrading from 0.6.45

- A PHP# call of a method a plain PHP class declares nowhere, which only its `__call` or `__callStatic` answers, stops compiling. Call the real method the magic forwards to, as `cache.get("key")` on the injected repository or `User.query().where("email", e)`, or the method a `@method` or `@mixin` line of the class declares.

### Added

- `Class<T>.tag()` gives the name a family stores for a variant, spec section 25, the inverse of `withTag`: the name its `[Key]` gives, or a backed machine's case's value. So with `public machine JourneyStatus : string { case Open = "open" { … } final case Completed = "completed"; … }`, `typeof(JourneyStatus.Completed).tag()` is `"completed"`, and a query that filters rows by state reads that name instead of writing `"completed"` a second time. `typeof(Card).tag()` is `"card"` from `[Key("card")]`, and `typeof(JourneyStatus).tag()` is null, since the machine is no variant. `withTag` now finds a case of a machine that stores as its value too, which 0.6.45 never listed as a family, so `typeof(JourneyStatus).withTag("completed")` is `typeof(JourneyStatus.Completed)` where 0.6.45 gave null, and `typeof(F).withTag(c.tag() ?? "")` is `c` for every variant `c` of `F`, an `int`-backed machine's `case Low = 1` included.
- `Class<T>.decode(value)` builds a `T` from a stored value already read into lists, maps and scalars, spec section 25, by the read `Json.decode` makes once it has read the text: a struct by its fields, a family's variant by its tag and a machine's case by its value. So `typeof(Page).decode(["title": "Home", "status": "published"])` gives a `Page` whose `status` is `LinkStatus.Published`, as `Json.decode<Page>` gives from the same value as JSON, and a program that holds a database row's decoded JSON column no longer branches on what kind of class each member is. A value that doesn't fit throws the `InvalidFields` `Json.decode` throws, as "title: expected string, got int 5; author: missing".
- `a + b` joins two `Bytes`, spec section 18: the bytes of `a` followed by those of `b`, so `([1, 2].toBytes() + [3].toBytes()).toList()` is `[1, 2, 3]`, and a signed payload built from a timestamp and a body joins them directly. 0.6.45 refused it with "`+` cannot apply to `Bytes` and `Bytes`: `Bytes` declares no `operator +`.", so a program wrote `Hex.decode(Hex.encode(a) + Hex.encode(b))`.

### Changed

- The walk of a project's folders reads the status of only the files it lists, where 0.6.45 read it for every file it met, as each `.md`, `.json` or `.js` file of `vendor/`, before it left that file out. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project, whose `vendor/` holds 3,373 files of neither `.php` nor `.sharp`, retires 52.6 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 53.1 G, medians of 8 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A compile places each file's compiled file by the real path the walk that found the file resolved, where 0.6.45 resolved that real path again on the disk at each use: to read the compiled file, to check it was the one the last compile wrote, to write it, and once more for each of those in the extension hosts' folder. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 52.9 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 55.7 G, medians of 4 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A call from PHP# code to a method a plain PHP class declares nowhere is `non-existent-method`, the error every undeclared method gets, on a class with `__call` or `__callStatic` too, spec section 23. So on a `Bag` that declares only `__call` and `__callStatic`, `bag.anything(1)` and `Bag.anything(1)` give "Method `Bag.anything` does not exist.", in a project and in a library. 0.6.45 compiled both, typed as `Any?` with any arguments, with no error. A method the class declares by its PHP code or its PHPDoc's `@method` or `@mixin` compiles as before, typed, and plain PHP files keep `non-documented-method`.
- A move is called on the case it moves from, checked at compile time, spec section 20.2: a move in a case's block is a member of that case alone, and one in the machine's body of every case that isn't `final`. So `public void pay(int cents) { this.status = this.status.pay(cents); }`, which 0.6.45 compiled and which threw `Sharp\StateError` from every case but `Draft`, stops compiling with "`pay` moves from `OrderStatus.Draft`, and `this.status` may be `Paid`, `Shipped` or `Cancelled`: test it first, as in `if (this.status is OrderStatus.Draft draft)`." Write `if (this.status is OrderStatus.Draft draft) { this.status = draft.pay(cents); }` in its place, or test it with `is not` and leave first: a write takes a move of the name a type pattern bound from the place it writes. No compiled program throws `Sharp\StateError` any more, and a case's class declares only its own moves. A `late` machine property takes its first value from an entry point in any method of its class, where 0.6.45 refused `this.status = OrderStatus.open();` outside a constructor with "`this.status` holds the machine `OrderStatus`, so it changes only by a move of its own". A struct holds a machine as `{ get; init; }`, and `line with { status: draft.pay(5) }` moves it, where 0.6.45 refused the property with "`Line.status` holds the machine `OrderStatus`, so its `set` is `private`."

### Removed

- `Sharp\StateError`, which a move called on a case it doesn't move from threw. That call is now a compile error, so no compiled program throws it. Plain PHP code that catches `Sharp\StateError` catches `Error` in its place.

### Fixed

- A family's `parse` reads a variant whose `[Key]` is a number, as `[Key("1")]`, spec section 10, and the problem for a name the family has no variant of lists that name too. PHP keeps the name `"1"` under the number `1`, so 0.6.45 never found the variant, and the process crashed while it listed the family's names for the problem, as it did for any name the family has no variant of. So `Tender.parse(["type": "1", "code": "V-7"])` gives the `Voucher` marked `[Key("1")]`, and `Tender.parse(["type": "cash"])` throws `InvalidFields` "type: expected 1, bank or card, got string 'cash'".
- A built-in call whose failure PHP# code reads from its result never reaches the app's error handler, so a handler that throws on every warning, as most frameworks set, no longer turns a documented failure into an `ErrorException`. `Regex.tryParse("([")` gives null, and `Regex.parse("([")` throws `ValueError` "Regex.parse: \"([\" does not compile: No ending matching delimiter ')' found", which names PHP's own reason where 0.6.45 named none. The same holds for `Compression.uncompress`, `Hex.decode`, `Serialized.decode`, `Files.copy`, `Files.move`, `Files.createDirectory`, `Files.deleteDirectory`, `Files.write` and `Environment.changeDirectory`, and for an app's own `global.file_get_contents(path)`. 0.6.45 called the handler first, so each threw `ErrorException`, as "preg_match(): No ending matching delimiter ')' found". A plain PHP `@` still calls the handler, as PHP does.
- An attribute on a machine case's field takes an object, as one on a constructor's parameter does, since an attribute's arguments follow one rule wherever the attribute stands. So `final case Forward([Route("/forward", middleware: [new Throttle(perMinute: 20)])] string? redirectUrl) = "forward";` compiles, and Reflection reads the `Route` on `Forward`'s `redirectUrl` with its `Throttle`. 0.6.45 checked the attribute's arguments as a constant's value and refused the `new` with "A constant's value is known before the code runs, and `new Throttle(perMinute: 20)` runs code: use a static field."
- `too-wide-member` never reports a case of a machine or of an enum whose case carries data, since a case takes no access modifier, spec section 20, and its reach is its type's. So in `public machine Status { case Open { public Status.Closed close() => Status.Closed; } final case Closed; }`, which code outside names only by `status is Status.Open`, `Open` and `Closed` report nothing, and the machine itself and its public methods are checked as before. 0.6.45 reported "`Status.Open` is public, and nothing outside `Status` uses it." with the help "Declare it `private`.", which the checker then refused, as it did for a data case such as `case Value(int amount);`.
- A `match` on a union of classes needs no `default` when its type arms name each member of the union, or a class or interface each member extends or implements, spec section 21, and `null` is one more member of a nullable union. So `match (this.part) { Leaf leaf => "leaf", Twig twig => "twig", }` on a `Twig|Leaf` compiles, and a `match` that leaves out a member names it: "This `match` misses `Leaf` and `null`." 0.6.45 refused each such `match` with "A `match` needs a `default` arm."
