A `?:` that tests a property and reads it again in its other branch, as `this.part is Twig twig ? fallback : this.part`, still reads the property at its declared type, since a property never narrows, and its error now says so and names the form that works: "`this.part` is a property, which never narrows: bind the value the test reads, as in `this.part is Leaf leaf ? leaf : fallback`." 0.6.45 gave "Invalid return type for method `Tree.leaf`: expected `Leaf`, but found `Leaf|Twig`." there. The note under "A `match` needs a `default` arm." names "every member of a union of classes" too, as the note under a `match` that misses a value does.
- A `match` over a sealed interface covers an enum that implements it by arms for each of the enum's cases, as it covers the enum by an arm for the enum, spec section 21. So with `public enum Open : Status { case Fresh; case Reopened(int times); }`, `match (status) { Open.Fresh => 0, Open.Reopened(int times) => times, Closed closed => closed.at, }` compiles, and a `match` that leaves out `Open.Reopened` names it: "This `match` misses `Open.Reopened`." 0.6.45 refused the first with "This `match` misses `Open`." and the second with "This `match` misses `Open` and `Open.Reopened`."
- A value of a type that never changes, a struct, an enum or a case of one, is readonly wherever `readonly` is asked for, spec section 13, so a function value takes it as its type is written. So `a.items.map((ReproItem item) => item.key)` compiles on a readonly `List<ReproItem>` of the struct `ReproItem`, where 0.6.45 refused it with "it takes a writable `ReproItem` where a readonly one is passed. Declare its parameter `readonly ReproItem`." and then warned on that `readonly` with "A `ReproItem` never changes, so `readonly` changes nothing: write `ReproItem`." A class that can change still needs its `readonly` there.
- A class of the file's namespace, or an imported one, that has the short name of a standard library effect hides the effect in `uses`, and the error says so: with `public static class Files { }` in `Probe`, `Function<void() uses Files>` gives "`Files` here is `Probe.Files`, a class of this namespace, which hides the effect `Sharp.IO.Files`." with the help "Import the effect under another name, as `import Sharp.IO.Files as FilesEffect;`, and write `uses FilesEffect`." 0.6.45 said "`Files` is not an effect: `uses` takes Cache, Clock, Console, Database, Dns, Environment, Files, …", which listed the name it refused.
- A tuple type is a call's type argument, as every type a type hint takes is, so `[1, 2].map<[int number, string label]>((int item) => [item, `${item}`]).map(([int number, string label] pair) => pair.number)` compiles: the lambda's literal lands in the tuple type the call names. 0.6.45 failed to parse it with "Expected `]`, found `number`", read `map<[` as a comparison, and typed the literal of the form without the type argument as a `List<int|string>`, which the next lambda refused.
- Runs of `mago` that write one project's `.sharp` folder take turns on its `write.lock`, from before a run reads the folder's record to after it deletes what it did not write, so `mago analyze` and `mago compile` started together on one tree each pass and leave the folder as one compile writes it. 0.6.45 let both write at once, and the one that finished first deleted the other's temp file, so the other failed, as 0.6.43 did with "can't delete the file /app/.sharp/.tmpacXnft". `compile.lock` stays the engine's, which holds it while the compile it runs takes `write.lock`.
- A law proves whatever its parameters, the fields it reaches and the members it calls are named, spec section 28, Lean's own words such as `until`, `while` or `exists` included, since the checker writes every name to Lean in `«»`, which keeps the name as written. So with `law pausingHoldsTheResume(Instant at, Instant until) => SubscriptionStatus.enter().go(at).pause() is SubscriptionStatus.Paused;`, both laws of its machine prove, where 0.6.44 refused every law of the file with "the checker translated the code it reaches wrongly. This is a bug in the PHP# checker; report it to its maintainers." A law refused that way is checked again by the next compile too, as `.sharp/proofs.json` now keeps only how Lean judged a law, where 0.6.44 kept the refusal and gave it again after the cause was gone until the file was deleted.
- A call of a function a tuple holds in a named element runs that function, spec section 30, as a call of a function a property holds does: its arguments are checked against the element's function type, its result has that type's result, and its effects are that type's. So `public static int direct([Function<int(int)> twice, string name] entry) => entry.twice(2);` compiles, and `direct([x => x * 2, "double"])` gives `4`. 0.6.44 refused it with "Method `twice` does not exist on `[Function<int(int)> twice, string name]`." and a help that named `List`'s members. A name the tuple does not write is refused as its read is: "`[Function<int(int)> twice, string name]` has no element named `missing`: its elements are `twice` and `name`."

## [0.6.45] - 2026-11-21

### Changed

- Name resolution finds the class-like a bare name in a `.sharp` file names by default, spec section 23, in one lookup by its short name, where 0.6.44 compared the name with the short name of each of up to 118 default class-likes. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 56.2 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 58.2 G, medians of 4 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.

### Fixed

- An interface may declare a property that holds a machine, spec section 20.2, as `{ get; }`: `public interface Payable { OrderStatus status { get; } }` compiles, and its implementer's `public OrderStatus status { get; private set; } = OrderStatus.open();` is checked as before. 0.6.44 refused the interface's property with "`Payable.status` holds the machine `OrderStatus`, so its `set` is `private`", whose help asked for the `{ get; private set; }` an interface may not state, so no interface could expose a machine.
- A type pattern reports a name no file declares in its type arguments, as a declared type does, with `non-existent-class-like`, and the test then reports nothing more. So `type is Class<UnitEnum>`, `value is List<UnitEnum>` and a test of a `Class<Object>` against `Class<UnitEnum>`, where no file declares `UnitEnum`, each give "Cannot find type `Probe.UnitEnum`.", where `mago compile` failed with `lowering-panic` for the first two and reported "This pattern never matches the value it tests." for the third.
- The name a type pattern binds is the type the pattern names, spec section 21, as a C# pattern variable has its declared type, in a `match` arm, after `is`, and after an `is not` that leaves. So over `Reply|Pending`, two interfaces, `result is Reply reply ? reply.status() : -1` and `if (result is not Reply reply) { return -1; } return reply.status();` compile, where 0.6.44 refused each call with "`reply.status()` calls a method on `Reply` but a function in a property on `Pending`", since it read `reply` as `Reply|Pending&Reply`. The help of that error, "Narrow `reply` to one class first, as in `if (reply is Reply reply)`", now names a pattern that works.
- A PHP# method that returns a `Bytes` declares its PHP result `\Sharp\Bytes|string`, spec sections 18 and 22, as one that returns a `Map` declares `\Sharp\Map|array`, since a plain PHP caller gets PHP's string of the bytes. So with `public class Digest { public virtual Bytes raw() => Hex.decode("ff00b1"); }`, a plain PHP subclass may declare `public function raw(): string`, and a PHP# call of `digest.raw()` reads its string as the `Bytes`, where 0.6.44 refused the subclass with "Declaration of Short::raw(): string must be compatible with BytesCrossings\Digest::raw(): Sharp\Bytes". A PHP# override that returns a `Bytes` under a plain PHP method keeps that method's type: under `: mixed` it gives every caller the string, which a PHP# call reads as the `Bytes`, and under `: object` the `Bytes` itself.

## [0.6.44] - 2026-11-21

### Fixed

- An extension host's deadline times only the call it bounds, never the start of the interpreter that serves it. Each analysis thread starts its interpreter, with its PHP settings and the SDK's `preload.php`, before its first call, and the call's deadline starts after that. So a whole-tree analysis on a loaded machine, which starts one interpreter per thread at once, starts its hosts however long those starts take, where 0.6.43 could fail it with "the extension hosts could not start: the call ran past its deadline of 1000 ms" on the first call, a `chdir` that takes microseconds. A provider call that runs past its own deadline still fails with "extension host `…` made no progress for … ms".
- A type test that binds a name gives it the value as the tested type holds it, spec section 21, whether a type parameter or the type itself spells the type: a PHP array plain PHP gave becomes the `Map` or the `Set` the type names, once, where the test binds it. So with `[Import("json_decode")] private static Any? decode(string json, bool associative);`, `match (decode(json, true)) { T value => value, … }` gives a `Map<string, int>` for `T = Map<string, int>`, and `(read<Map<string, int>>("{\"a\":1,\"b\":2}") ?? [:]).keys().join(",")` gives `a,b`, where 0.6.43 threw "Sharp\Internal\keys(): Argument #1 ($table) must be of type Sharp\Map|Sharp\Set, array given". `T = Set<int>` from a PHP list binds a `Set`, and `value as Map<string, int>` gives the `Map`. The tested value keeps its own, so an empty plain array an `as Map<string, Any?>` read is still a `List` to the next test.
- A property a constructor promotes is closed, spec sections 6.1 and 22, as a property in the class body is, in a class open to subclasses too. So with `public abstract class Notice { public Notice(public string message { get; set; }) { } }`, `typeof(Notice).properties()` reads `message` with `isVirtual` false, and `typeof(Notice).fields()` gives its storage, which a `Field` reads and writes. 0.6.43 read it as `virtual` and left it out of `fields()`, so code that stores each field of an object lost every field promoted in an open class. A subclass that declares `message` again is a compile error that names it closed, as for a body property, and since a parameter takes no `virtual`, its help says "Declare `message` in `Notice`'s body as `virtual` to let a subclass override it."
- The Composer package `heyjordanparker/mago-sharp` holds only what Composer installs again, as 0.6.42's did. 0.6.43's held Mago's Rust crates and their test fixtures, 6,531 files where 0.6.42's held 291, so a project whose analysis reads `vendor/` got hundreds of errors from them, and `mago compile` refused 153 of their `.sharp` fixtures. The runtime image builds from the checkout instead, with `docker build -f sharp/docker/runtime/frankenphp.Dockerfile -t php-sharp .`, as `sharp/README.md` shows.

## [0.6.43] - 2026-11-21

### Upgrading from 0.6.42

- Plain PHP gets a `Bytes` as PHP's string of its bytes, spec section 18, so plain PHP that called a `Bytes` member on what PHP# gave it reads the string instead: `$cache->get('k')->toText()` is `$cache->get('k')`, `$response->body->length()` is `strlen($response->body)`, and `$random->nextBytes(4)->toList()` is `array_values(unpack('C*', $random->nextBytes(4)))`.

### Fixed

- A `Bytes` reaches plain PHP as PHP's string of its bytes, spec section 18, wherever it crosses: an argument to a parameter of any type, an `[Import]` argument, a value PHP# returns or yields to plain PHP, an element of a `List`, a `Map` or a `Set`, and a property plain PHP reads. `[Import("file_put_contents")] public static int? write(string path, Bytes data) uses Files;` writes the 260 bytes of a zip, where 0.6.42 wrote the 348 characters of their base64. A `Bytes` argument is checked as a `string` against a plain PHP parameter, so one passed where plain PHP takes `object` is a compile error.
- An `extern` line may state a plain PHP `string` parameter or result as `Bytes`, spec section 23, so `extern Bytes StreamInterface.read(int length) uses none;` compiles, and its result holds every byte the stream gives. 0.6.42 refused it as "`StreamInterface.read` returns `string`, which the stated `Bytes` does not take."
- `git archive HEAD | docker build -f sharp/docker/runtime/frankenphp.Dockerfile -t php-sharp -`, the build of the runtime image from a source archive that `sharp/README.md` shows, builds the image. The archive holds `sharp/mago/Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `crates/` and `src/`, which the build reads. 0.6.42's archive left them out, so its build failed where the image copies `sharp/mago/crates/sharp-lean/lean/lean-toolchain`.
- A law follows a value typed as a union of one machine's cases, spec sections 20.2 and 28, as a value of the machine. A move called on it runs as the case it holds runs it, and one called on a case it doesn't move from throws, as its `Sharp\StateError` does. A type test on it tests which case it is. So with `go` typed `Step.B|Step.C`, `law twoMovesReachC() => Step.enter().go(false).go(true) is Step.C;` proves, and so does `law shutReopens() => Gate.enter().close(false) is Gate.Shut s ? s.open() is Gate.Open : false;`. 0.6.42 refused the first as "reaches a value of the type `App\Step\B|App\Step\C` … A law cannot know which implementation runs." and the second as "reaches a type test … It reads types while the code runs." A union across machines stays refused.

## [0.6.42] - 2026-11-21

### Changed

- A compile walks the project's folders once. Its record of the source files takes each file's status from the walk the loader lists the files with, before any file is read, where 0.6.41 walked every folder a second time for the record. The record now names exactly the files the compile reads: a file an `excludes` pattern leaves out, or one under the folder a `paths` glob starts with that the glob doesn't match, no longer makes the next compile run again when it changes. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 58.1 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 61.7 G, medians of 4 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A whole `mago analyze` of a project with no extension host enabled analyzes the project once: it reports the analysis its compile into `.sharp/` made, where 0.6.41 analyzed every file a second time for the report. A plain PHP project paid for that second analysis too. A whole `mago analyze` of 8,451 Laravel vendor files retires 74.8 G instructions where it retired 143.5 G, and 12.5 CPU-seconds where it took 23.9, medians of 3 alternating rounds, with the same issues. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project with no host retires 31.0 G where it retired 49.3 G, medians of 4 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues. A run with `--no-stubs` or `--substitute` still analyzes again, since it reads the files otherwise than the compile does.
- An extension host names each list of codebase reads by the XXH128 of the names it read, where 0.6.41 serialized the whole list to hash it, and a query's first reads of a kind take its names as they are, with no copy. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file PHP# project whose host's provider asks for 1,661 classes on each call retires 77.3 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 87.7 G, and takes 13.5 CPU-seconds where it took 15.0, two alternating rounds each of one binary with either SDK, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.

### Fixed

- A law reasons about a machine's value as the closed set of its cases, spec sections 20.2 and 28, as it does about an enum's. `is` and `match` on it test which case it is, `this` in the machine's own methods included, and `==` between two of its values compares the case and its fields, as the machine's `==` does when it runs. So `law turningOnLights() => Lamp.off().turnOn().lit();` proves where `lit()` is `this is Lamp.On`, and so does `law turningOnLightsTheLamp() => Lamp.off().turnOn() == Lamp.On;`. 0.6.41 refused the first as "a match on this subject" and then as "the checker left out On", and the second as "the checker left out Lamp.operator ==", each of those last two called a bug in the PHP# checker.
- A law reasons about a type test inside `or`, `and` and `not`, spec sections 21 and 28, as it does about one standing alone: of a sealed interface's implementer, a machine's case, and a list pattern. So a move whose arm is `Stop or Wait => this`, or whose value is `signal is not Go ? this : Light.Red(now)`, proves its law, and so does `xs is [] or [int]`. 0.6.41 refused it as "Law stopKeeps reaches a type test in Light.Red.answer. It reads types while the code runs."
- A law reasons about a union of one machine's cases, spec sections 20.2 and 28, as part of the machine's closed set of cases, and about `is` on a case that holds data. So a move typed `Counter.Counting|Counter.Done` proves `law twoCount() => Counter.start(2).step() is Counter.Counting;`. 0.6.41 refused the move as "reaches the type `Counter.Counting|Counter.Done` … Give it a type a law reasons about". A union across machines stays refused.
- A machine's invariant proves after a move whose branches give different cases, spec sections 20.2 and 28, as it proves after one that gives one case: the move keeps it from a value it holds on. So `law aCountIsOneOrTwo(Counter.Counting counting) => counting.n >= 1 && counting.n <= 2;` proves where `step()` is `this.n >= 2 ? Counter.Done : Counter.Counting(this.n + 1)`. 0.6.41 refused it as "`aCountIsOneOrTwo` breaks after `step()`", though no reachable move breaks it. An invariant a reachable move breaks is still refused with the moves that break it.
- A struct's or a class's stored form holds every stored field, spec section 10, whatever its setter's visibility, so `Json.encode`, `Json.decode`, `parse`, `Query.decode` and `serialize()` keep a `private set` property, a machine's among them, and a get-only property's value. For `public Money balance { get; private set; }` and `public AccountStatus status { get; private set; }`, `Json.encode(account)` writes `{"id":7,"balance":"12.50","status":"active"}`, where 0.6.41 wrote `{"id":7}` and `Json.decode` gave each such property its initial value, as a machine's entry case, whatever was stored. A computed property stores nothing. A field that has no stored form, as a private `Function` field, makes `parse` and `Json.decode` of its class a compile error that names it, as a public one does.
- A backed machine whose cases hold no data, and that is not marked `[Tag]`, stores as its case's value, spec section 20.2, as a backed enum does: `Json.encode` writes `"active"`, and `parse`, `Json.decode` and `Query.decode` read the case back from it. 0.6.41 refused to read such a machine as "`AccountStatus` is abstract". A machine with a case that holds data stores as a tagged family, as before.
- A key that two fields of a struct's or a class's stored form read, spec section 10, is a compile error where `parse`, `tryParse`, `Json.encode` or `Json.decode` of the type is called, and no longer where the type is declared, so a class that nothing stores compiles. It reads "`label` is read twice in `Bill`: by the main constructor parameter `label` and the property `label`."

## [0.6.41] - 2026-11-21

### Upgrading from 0.6.40

- A `string` is text and always valid UTF-8, spec section 18, and binary data is `Bytes`, a struct in `Sharp` that every file names with no import. `text.toBytes()` gives a string's UTF-8 bytes, and `bytes.toText()` reads bytes back as text, each byte sequence that is not UTF-8 becoming U+FFFD. Each byte member moves to `Bytes`:
  - `text.byteLength()` is `text.toBytes().length()`.
  - `text.byteSubstring(start, end)` is `text.toBytes().slice(start, end)`, with `.toText()` for its text. Its range error reads "`slice` takes a range inside the bytes, and the range is outside it."
  - `text.bytes()` is `text.toBytes().toList()`.
  - A `List<int>`'s `toByteString()` is `toBytes()`, which gives `Bytes`, so `[104, 105].toByteString()` is `[104, 105].toBytes().toText()`.
  - An `int`'s `toByteString()` is gone: one byte is `[n].toBytes()`.
  - `n.toBytes(order)` gives `Bytes`, and `Int.fromBytes(bytes, order)` takes it, so `Int.fromBytes("short")` is `Int.fromBytes("short".toBytes())`.
  - `Base64.encode`, `Hex.encode`, `Compression.compress` and `Compression.uncompress` take `Bytes`, and `Base64.decode`, `Hex.decode`, `Compression.compress`, `Compression.uncompress`, `Ip.toBinary` and `Random.nextBytes` give it.
  - `Hash.of`, `Hash.md5`, `Hash.sha1`, `Hash.crc32`, `Hmac.of` and `Hmac.verify` take their data and key as `Bytes`, so text is hashed as `Hash.sha1(text.toBytes())`. Each still gives its lowercase hexadecimal text.
  - `Files.read(path)` of a file that is not text, as an image, is the new `Files.readBytes(path)`, and its `Files.write(path, contents)` is the new `Files.writeBytes(path, bytes)`. `read`, `readLines` and `readLinesLazily` give text.
  - `ZipArchive.read(name)` of an entry that is not text is the new `ZipArchive.readBytes(name)`, and its `write(name, contents)` is the new `writeBytes(name, bytes)`. `read` gives text.
  - `HttpResponse.body` is `Bytes`, so a JSON body is read as `Json.parse(response.body.toText())`. `HttpRequest`'s `body` takes `Bytes`, `null` by default where it was `""`, so a JSON body is sent as `body: Json.encode(payload).toBytes()`.
  - `Cache.get` and `Cache.getOrCreate` give `Bytes`, and `Cache.set` and the function `getOrCreate` takes give it, so text is cached as `cache.set(key, text.toBytes())` and read back as `cache.get(key)?.toText()`.

  A call of a removed member is refused with the member to use, as `text.byteLength()`'s "A `string` is text, and `text.toBytes()` gives its UTF-8 bytes: count them with `text.toBytes().length()`." Plain PHP that calls one of these members gets the `Bytes` struct, where 0.6.40 gave a PHP string, and passes a PHP string where PHP# takes `Bytes`. PHP# passes `Bytes` to a plain PHP parameter that takes a `string` as the string it holds, so a plain PHP method that takes `string $data` gets every byte.
- A string literal whose escapes write bytes that are no UTF-8, as `"\xB1\x31"`, is a compile error that writes those bytes as `Bytes`, spec section 18: "This literal's escapes are no UTF-8 text; bytes are `[0xB1, 0x31].toBytes()`." Write the bytes as that `Bytes`, and a character as `\u{…}`, which writes its UTF-8 bytes, as `"caf\u{E9}"`.

### Changed

- The law checker proves that a recursion stepping an `int` one down or one up towards a bound ends with the step's own lemma and `omega`, and tries `grind` only when that fails, and it elaborates each generated module with Lean's linters off, since no answer reads their warnings. A cold compile of a 2,128-file project with 1,330 laws has its law checker retire 1,029 G instructions where it retired 1,183 G. Every law proves or fails as before.
- A compile proves its laws in one law checker process. A law with fixed steps that leave it unproven goes on to Lean's search in the same process, where 0.6.40 started a second process that imported all of Lean again. A compile whose every law has fixed steps still imports only the runtime library, and sends the laws its steps leave unproven to a second process after the first. On an Apple M2 Max at a load average of 100 to 150, medians of 3 rounds measured with `/usr/bin/time -l`: a compile of the laws fixture with laws with and without bodies takes 5.39 s of CPU where it took 5.97 s, and a cold compile of a 2,128-file project with 1,330 laws takes 336.7 s where it took 341.4 s. That compile's wall time is 176.8 s where it was 146.9 s, since one process now proves what two proved at once.
- An extension host's provider calls no longer wait on each other for their deadlines. Each analysis thread keeps its call's deadline to itself, and a call wakes mago's deadline thread only when its deadline comes before the one that thread waits for, where 0.6.40 locked one list every thread shared three times per call and woke the deadline thread twice. A `mago analyze` of a 4,000-file project whose host answers 340,000 provider calls takes 2.3 fewer CPU-seconds, and its system time falls from 1.3 s to 0.6 s.
- A call of an extension member, as a collection's `add` or `count`, checks that a generic member's receiver is of the value's kind of collection before it infers the member's type arguments, so a call on a `List` no longer infers those of `Set`'s `add` or of `Map`'s and `Set`'s `count`, and it writes the receiver's type out only for an error it reports. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project with an extension host retires 64.5 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 65.5 G, medians of 4 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- An extension host's provider receives its request as the objects mago's engine builds, the way a PHP extension builds the objects it returns, where the SDK's PHP decoded each request from its values: a return-type, callable-signature or assertion request's `Invocation` with its arguments, a property-type request's `PropertyAccess`, a call-forwarding request, and each `Type` they name, its atomic types and flags. A provider reads the same objects, with the same values and descriptions. A `mago analyze` of a 4,000-file project whose host answers 340,000 provider calls retires 80.1 G instructions where it retired 92.5 G, and takes 11.5 CPU-seconds where it took 12.0.
- An extension host sends each distinct list of codebase reads once per analysis, and names it by number after, where 0.6.40 sent every read of every provider call and hook again with its answer, and mago decoded each one again. A provider that reads the same symbols on every call now sends them once, and mago keeps each list once and joins each file's lists after the analysis. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file PHP# project with an extension host retires 172 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 271 G, and takes 25 CPU-seconds where it took 37.6, two alternating rounds each, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues. Its first, cold analysis retires 186 G where it retired 287 G.
- An extension host records the codebase reads of a query of several names in one step, by each read's operation and name, where it built a key for each read, and names each list of reads by its XXH128 rather than a key of every read it holds. A provider that asks for 1,661 classes on each call spends a tenth of the time it spent recording them. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file PHP# project whose host's provider does that retires 89 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 168 G, and takes 15 CPU-seconds where it took 24.5, two alternating rounds each, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A law with a body has fixed steps too: the law checker closes each of its cases as it closes a law without one, with the rewrites of the facts the body's calls state, and runs Lean's search only for a law the steps leave unproven. A recursion on an `int` proves with them, and one on a list still goes on to the search. They prove some laws the search left open, as `m.times(n).currency == m.currency`, which holds by `add`'s definition whatever the body calls. A cold compile of a 2,128-file project with 1,330 laws has its law checker retire 720 G instructions where it retired 964 G, and every law proves as before.
- A compile stamps an input that is not there without listing its folder. It lists a folder only to check the case of a file's name where something is at the path, where 0.6.40 listed the folder of every input it read from disk, the absent `Module.sharp` of each folder included. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 63.1 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 64.4 G, medians of 5 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A whole `mago analyze` loads the project's files once. Its analysis with the extension hosts reads the files its compile loaded, where 0.6.40 walked the project's folders and read every file a second time once the compile was done. A one-file edit of a 2,128-file project retires 60.8 G instructions in `mago analyze` where it retired 63.6 G, medians of 4 alternating rounds, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.

### Fixed

- `Class<T>.genericDefinition()` of a plain PHP generic class written with type arguments gives the class, spec section 25, so `Method.type` of a method that returns `Collection<int, string>` gives `typeof(Collection)`, with `typeArguments()` of `[typeof(int), typeof(string)]`. 0.6.40 gave null, since it counted only a PHP# class's type parameters and a plain PHP class declares its own only in its docblock, so code that sorted a class's methods by their generic return type, as a relation method's `MorphTo<…>`, found none.
- `Json.encode` writes a NaN or infinite `float` as `null`, spec section 24, alone and inside a `List` or a `Map`, since JSON has no number for it. 0.6.40 threw `JsonException` "Inf and NaN cannot be JSON encoded", so a page that encoded a number computed from a visitor's input, as `Math.log(0.0)` gives negative infinity, failed for that visitor.
- `vendor/bin/mago` unpacks the `mago` it downloads with the system `tar`, which streams the archive, so it installs under PHP's default `memory_limit` of 128M. 0.6.40's archive is 74 MB and unpacks to a 144 MB binary, and 0.6.40 unpacked it with `PharData`, which held it all in memory and failed with "Allowed memory size of 134217728 bytes exhausted", so every command that ran `vendor/bin/mago` failed until the binary was unpacked. The unpack now takes 2 MB of PHP's memory. An archive `tar` can't read fails with `tar`'s error, and the archive is deleted.
- `vendor/bin/mago` shows its download's progress line only when STDERR is a terminal, as curl and Composer do. 0.6.40 wrote it on every chunk wherever STDERR went, so a hook that captured `vendor/bin/mago`'s output got 13,500 progress lines from one download of 0.6.40. A captured STDERR now holds only "Downloading mago 0.6.40 for aarch64-apple-darwin..." and "Downloaded.".
- A plain PHP closure that crosses into a PHP# function type has each call's result checked, spec section 14, as every other crossing checks its value: a result of another type throws `TypeError`, as "Function<Sharp.Bytes()>: Return value must be of type Sharp.Bytes, int returned", text that is not UTF-8 becomes valid, and a string a `Bytes` result takes is its bytes. 0.6.40 let the result in unchecked, so a result of the wrong type failed later, where PHP# code used it, as `cache.getOrCreate("k", fn () => "text")` from plain PHP failed inside `Cache` reading the string as `Bytes`, and a closure's text kept each byte that is not UTF-8. A function type returning `void` or `Any?` takes the closure as it is.
- A plain PHP string that crosses into a PHP# `string` is valid UTF-8, spec section 18: each byte sequence that is not UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD, as a request value such as `?q=%E9` gives one. It does in a parameter plain PHP passes, a variadic's values, and the elements of a `List` and the keys and values of a `Map` it holds. A plain PHP string that crosses into `Bytes` keeps every byte. 0.6.40 let `"caf\xE9"` in, counted it as 4 characters, and threw `JsonException` "Malformed UTF-8 characters" from `Json.encode` of it, so one bad byte in a request failed a page. Two keys of a `Map` that become one throw `ValueError`, as "This `Map` holds both `"a\xE9"` and `"a\xFF"`, which are one key once each byte sequence that is not UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD".
- Reflection lists the plain PHP traits a class uses, spec section 22: `Class<T>.traits()` gives the class's own, each followed by the traits it uses, then those of the classes it extends, each once, as `interfaces()` gives its interfaces. So `typeof(Novel).traits()` gives `[typeof(Copies)]` when `Novel` extends a `Book` that uses `Copies`, the traits `is` tests. 0.6.40 had no such member, and refused `type.traits()` as "Attempting to access a method on a non-object type (`Class<Object>`).". `x is SoftDeletes` and `x is not SoftDeletes` already test the class, its parents and their traits on 0.6.40, and report no redundant condition on a value of an open class.
- `mago compile` decides which file a law's refusal says has errors from the errors the analysis reported, as `mago analyze` does, spec section 28. A law that reaches a file with no errors that the compile left untranslated, as one whose lowering panicked, is refused as the checker's bug, "the checker left out Money.add, which the law reaches. This is a bug in the PHP# checker; report it to its maintainers.", where 0.6.40 said "it reaches Money.add, whose file has errors." of a file with none.
- A law that passes a state of a sealed interface where an open interface the state implements is expected proves, spec section 28, as `Pending.keep(pending)` does when `Pending : OrderStatus, Printable` and `keep` takes a `Printable`. 0.6.40 refused it as "the checker translated the code it reaches wrongly. This is a bug in the PHP# checker".
- A law that takes a value of a class that is a state of two sealed interfaces is refused with its reason, spec section 28, as "Law keepsCard reaches the class Card in Card.keepsCard. It is a state of both `Colored` and `Shape`, and a state belongs to one sealed interface in a law.". 0.6.40 gave the class the Lean type of the first interface by name, so a law that passed it as the other was refused as the checker's bug, and a law that passed it as the first proved.
- A law gets the same answer on a busy machine as on an idle one, spec section 28: Lean's heartbeats alone bound its proof. Mago stops the law checker only when it has written nothing and used no processor time for its deadline, 480 seconds, so a checker that computes on a machine that gives it little of a core is never stopped, and one that waits still is. 0.6.40 stopped the checker once it wrote nothing for 480 seconds, so on a machine at a load of 150 a law whose search ran that long failed the compile with "The laws could not be checked: the law checker did not finish within 480 seconds.", though it proved on the same tree at a lower load.
- An `internal` member of a case's block reaches the namespace its file declares, spec sections 5 and 20.2, as every `internal` member does. So in `namespace App;`, the machine `Lamp`'s own methods and laws, and every class of `App`, call its case `Off`'s `internal turnOn()`, and a class of `Web` is refused with "`Off.turnOn` is internal to `App`, and `Web` is outside it.". 0.6.40 read the namespace from the class the case runs as, `App\Lamp\Off`, so it refused the machine's own law and methods with "`Off.turnOn` is internal to `App.Lamp`, and `App` is outside it.".

## [0.6.40] - 2026-11-20

### Upgrading from 0.6.39

- A cached value or a queued job whose type changed since it was written now throws when read, spec section 10. `unserialize` builds a PHP# struct or class through its `parse`, so its constructor runs, and data its type no longer reads throws the `Sharp.InvalidFields` that `parse` throws or the constructor's own error, where 0.6.39 filled the properties with no constructor run. `serialize` writes the stored form, the fields `Json.encode` writes, so data 0.6.39 wrote under a renamed `[Key]` or a private property's name no longer reads. Clear such caches and drain such queues when deploying.
- `Class<T>.createLazy` is gone, spec section 25, since it filled an object its constructor never built and hid a query behind a property read. Load late in the open instead: a `lazy` property, as `public lazy Plan plan => this.planner.plan(this.planId);`, a stored id the caller looks up, as `int ownerId` beside `Accounts.find(ownerId)`, or a function value, as `Function<Account()> owner`.
- A test double of a PHP# class can't be made, spec section 9: PHPUnit's `createMock(Catalog::class)` throws "App.Catalog is built only through its constructor, which this creation doesn't run", where 0.6.39 made one. A plain PHP class that extends a PHP# class is built only through a constructor chain that runs the PHP# constructor. Build the real object in a test, and give it a real binding from the test's module where it reaches an effect.
- `mago compile` writes no `.php` file and no `.gitignore` beside a `.sharp` file, decision 81, where 0.6.39 wrote a `.php` file that loaded each `.sharp` file that declares a type. A framework that lists a folder's `*.php` files no longer finds PHP# classes there, as Laravel's console kernel, Laravel's event discovery and Spatie's route attributes list them. Name each PHP# class through the framework's explicit registration instead: a console command with `->withCommands([SendReport::class])`, a controller in a route file or with `RouteRegistrar::registerClass`, and a listener with `Event::listen(Login::class, RecordLogin::class)`. Then delete the files earlier compiles wrote, once, from the project root. Each is matched by the line `mago compile` wrote at its top:
- The arm64 Linux `mago` a release publishes needs glibc 2.39 or later, as Ubuntu 24.04 and Debian 13 have, where 0.6.39's needed glibc 2.35. It links the PHP# engine, which now builds on Ubuntu 24.04 for arm64. The x86_64 Linux `mago` still needs glibc 2.35.
- `Sharp.Text.Regex` is a value that holds a pattern that compiles, spec section 31, so a pattern that comes from data, as one a user types into a form field, is read with `Regex.tryParse(pattern)`, which gives null when the pattern doesn't compile, and a pattern the code writes is read with `Regex.parse(pattern)`, which throws `ValueError` as `Regex.parse: "/[/" does not compile`. `matches`, `match`, `matchAll`, `replace`, `split` and `filter` are its methods and take only the text, and `escape` stays static. 0.6.39's static methods took the pattern on every call and threw `ValueError` for one that didn't compile, so a bad pattern from data could be handled only by catching a bug. Change `Regex.matches("/^[a-z]+$/", text)` to `Regex.parse("/^[a-z]+$/").matches(text)`, and `Regex.replace(pattern, replacement, text)` to `Regex.parse(pattern).replace(replacement, text)`. A failure while matching, as PCRE's backtrack limit, still throws `ValueError`.

### Changed

- `mago analyze` of the whole project compiles it before it analyzes with the extension hosts, spec section 27: it writes the `.sharp` folder as `mago --no-extensions compile` writes it, laws proven, and the hosts' folder, from one analysis without the hosts and one lowering, then reports each issue of the analysis and of the compile once. A save runs `mago analyze` alone. 0.6.39's `mago analyze` compiled only the hosts' folder, from an analysis of its own, so a save that also ran `mago compile` analyzed the project three times and lowered it twice. On a project of 496 PHP# files with one host, a one-file edit's save costs 4.1 CPU-seconds, where the two commands cost 6.1, with the same `.sharp` folder and issues.
- A PHP# object is only built through its constructor, spec section 9. Plain PHP's `ReflectionClass::newInstanceWithoutConstructor`, `newLazyGhost` and `resetAsLazyGhost`, PDO's `FETCH_CLASS` and `fetchObject`, and SOAP's class map throw "App.Account is built only through its constructor, which this creation doesn't run" for a PHP# struct or class and for a plain PHP class that extends one, where 0.6.39 created the object with its constructor's checks skipped. `new`, `ReflectionClass::newInstance`, `newInstanceArgs` and a lazy proxy's factory run the constructor as before. `serialize` of a type with no stored form throws an `Error` that names it and why, as "`serialize` writes the stored form of App.Stamper, the fields `Json.encode` writes, and the main constructor parameter `clock` holds no property.", for a field that holds a function and a `Map` keyed by a type without text too. A class whose plain PHP parent declares `__serialize` and `__unserialize`, as an ORM's model does, keeps the parent's.
- An extension host's provider answers each call in less time. After each answer the SDK checked which classes the call declared by listing every declared class, interface and trait. It now skips that when the interpreter declared none and asked its autoloaders for none, which mago's engine tells it through `\Mago\Engine\class_count()`.
- A release `mago` holds the law checker built for its platform, and unpacks it into its cache folder on the first compile that proves a law, so no machine builds the checker with `lake`, and a `mago` runs only the checker built with it. 0.6.39 built the checker with `lake build` on each machine's first compile that proved a law. A `mago` built without the checker, as `cargo build` builds it, still builds it with `lake`. On a cold compile of a 187-file project with an empty cache folder, on an M-series Mac, the first compile takes 4.3 s and 14.7 CPU-seconds, where it took 10.5 s and 23.3 CPU-seconds.
- A compile stamps each input the analysis loaded from the bytes it loaded, with the modification time the loader took before reading it, so it reads each source file once where it read it twice. A whole `mago analyze` of a 2,128-file project from nothing retires 71.4 G instructions where it retired 74.0 G.

### Fixed

- `.sharp/compiled.bin` keeps only the reads a compile reads back, so it no longer grows with the project's PHP# files times the declarations an extension host reads. 0.6.39 kept every read of each file, and each file reads every declaration a host read, so a 2,232-file project whose host read many classes wrote a 1.63 GB `compiled.bin`, read it back on each compile and failed to write it once `.sharp/` outgrew a 2 GB filesystem. On a 2,220-file project whose host reads 1,661 classes, `compiled.bin` falls from 138.8 MB to 0.71 MB, and a compile after an edit holds 1.30 GB where it held 2.10 GB.
- Reflection reads every type a plain PHP signature declares, spec section 25, so `methods()` lists a class with a plain PHP `callable` parameter or result, a PHP# class that inherits one included, as a model of an ORM whose methods take callbacks does. A `callable` reads as `callable`, the function type of any shape spec section 7 names: its class value's `kind()` is `TypeKind.Function`, its `parameterTypes()` is empty, its `returnType()` is `Any?`, and it holds what PHP's `is_callable` holds. A `false` or a `true` reads as `bool`, as the checker reads it. 0.6.38 and 0.6.39 threw "The engine gave a type text that names no type." from `methods()` on each such class.
- A read of bad fields in a request whose autoloader doesn't reach the standard library throws a `ValueError` with the message `Sharp.InvalidFields` gives, as "grams: missing; speed: missing", spec section 10. 0.6.39 threw no error there from `Json.decode<T>`, `Query.decode<T>` or a struct's `parse`.
- A law compares a nullable value of a class with `==` or `!=` as the code compares it, spec section 28: null equals only null, and two values run the class's `==`. `new Stamp(at).at == at` on an `Instant?` and an `Instant` proves, and so does `==` on two structs with an `Instant?` field. 0.6.38 and 0.6.39 refused both, as "Law keepsTheInstant reaches the class operator `==` in Stamp.keepsTheInstant. Compare the values the objects hold, such as their properties."
- `mago analyze` refuses a law that reaches a package's PHP# file with errors where `mago compile` refuses it, spec section 28, as "Law addKeeps is not proven: it reaches Money.add, whose file has errors. Fix them, then run vendor/bin/mago compile again.", and still reports none of the package's own issues. 0.6.39's `mago analyze` reported no issue for such a law. A law that calls a law no translation states, in a file with no errors, is refused as the checker's bug, as one that reaches any other declaration is, where 0.6.39 always said its file had errors.
- An extension host's shutdown function that calls `getenv()` runs as its analysis thread ends. 0.6.39 aborted `mago` there with "panic in a function that cannot unwind", exit code 133, since the interpreter read mago's variables after the thread had freed them.
- An extension host runs with the PHP settings its `ini` table sets, as `php -d` set them for a worker process before 0.6.39, so `[extension-hosts.reports]` with `ini = { memory_limit = "2G", error_log = "var/log/mago-host.log" }` reads `2G` from `ini_get('memory_limit')`, and its `error_log()` lines go to that file, from the configuration's directory. 0.6.39 ran each host with no memory limit and refused an `ini` table as "unknown field `ini`", and a host's log lines reached only the error of a request that failed, since mago runs hosts inside itself. A setting PHP doesn't know fails the run as "PHP refused the setting `no_such_setting` = `2G`", and two hosts that set one setting to different values fail it naming both, since all hosts share each interpreter.
- `redundant-type-comparison` reports an `==` or `!=` of a class value and a `typeof` only when the class value is that one class, spec section 25, since two class values are equal when they name the same class and a `Class<T>` also holds each class that extends `T`. 0.6.37 to 0.6.39 reported each later comparison of `type == typeof(Order) || type == typeof(Invoice) || type == typeof(Refund)` on a `Class<Any>` as "Redundant condition: variable `type` (type `Class<Any>`) is already known to be `Class<Invoice>`.", and `early || type == typeof(Order)` on a `Class<Order>` too. Both report nothing now, and after `const type = typeof(Order);` the same `type == typeof(Order)` is still reported.
- A class's type parameter that only its members' code reads, through `typeof`, `is`, `as`, a type argument or a pattern, is used, spec section 11, since generics are reified. 0.6.37 to 0.6.39 reported `RecordReference<TRecord>`, whose one method reads `typeof(TRecord)`, as "Type parameter `TRecord` is never used in class `RecordReference`.". A PHP# interface's type parameter is never reported, since each implementer gives it a type argument that reflection and `is` read, where 0.6.38 and 0.6.39 reported `T` of `I<T>` though `A : I<int>` gave it one. A class's type parameter that no signature and no member's code names is still reported.
- `is` and `is not` of a plain PHP trait on a value of a type parameter bounded by an open class may match, spec section 22, as on a value of the open class itself, since a subclass may use the trait. 0.6.37 to 0.6.39 read the test as never true, so in `RowQuery<TRow : BaseModel>` the `if (this.getModel() is not SoftDeletes)` was reported as "This condition (type `true`) will always evaluate to `true`.", and `this.getModel() is SoftDeletes trashable` as a pattern that never matches. Both now compile, and `trashable.getDeletedAtColumn()` reads the trait's member. A test on a value of a final class that doesn't use the trait still never matches.
- A parameter of a method written `override` keeps its parent's name with no case warning, spec section 24, since `incompatible-parameter-name` holds it to that name. 0.6.37 to 0.6.39 warned on `public override Any? get_var(string? query = null, int column_offset = 0)` over a plain PHP `get_var` as "Parameter name `column_offset` should be in camel case.", and refused the rename to `columnOffset` as "Name it `column_offset`, as the parent does.", so no spelling cleared both. A parameter of a method that overrides nothing still warns, and the rename is still refused.
- `SplFileInfo` is a standard class, decision 83, which a `.sharp` file names bare, with no import and no `extern` line of its own, since a plain PHP library hands PHP# code one, as a file finder's results are an `SplFileInfo` or a subclass of it. The standard library declares it `uses Files`, and `getPathname`, `getFilename`, `getBasename`, `getExtension`, `getPath` and `__toString`, which read only the path it holds, `uses none`, so `file.getPathname()` compiles in a pure method, on a package's subclass too, and `file.getSize()` there is refused for `Files`. 0.6.39 refused a bare `SplFileInfo` in `namespace App;` as "Cannot find type `App.SplFileInfo`.", and `import SplFileInfo;` as "Imported class, interface, trait, or enum `SplFileInfo` does not exist.", so a file that took one could not compile.
- A DOM document built and read in memory is pure, spec section 29: the standard library declares each class of `Dom` `uses none`, and only the members that load, save, validate against or include a file `uses Files`, as `HTMLDocument.createFromFile`, `HTMLDocument.saveHtmlFile`, `XMLDocument.saveXmlFile`, `Node.C14NFile`, `Document.schemaValidate` and `XMLDocument.xinclude`. `XMLDocument.createFromString` `uses Files` too, since its options can make it load a DTD or an included file. 0.6.39 declared every `Dom` class `uses any`, so `HTMLDocument.createEmpty().createElement("p").tagName` in a pure method was refused as "`Page.paragraph` calls `Element.tagName`, which may have any effect", and a project's own `extern` line for the class was refused as a duplicate.
- The typed declaration that binds a `match` arm's value for its `when` draws no redundancy finding when the value already has its type, spec section 21, since only a typed declaration binds a name: `string n when n != "wp"` on a `string` compiles clean. 0.6.37 to 0.6.39 reported it as "Redundant type assertion: `n` is already `string`." and its `when` as "Redundant `when` operation: left operand is always `true`". A type test that binds nothing and always holds, as `name is string` on a `string`, is still reported.
- A law that builds, passes or compares a value whose type it cannot read, and reads nothing inside it, proves, spec section 28, as `law holdingKeepsTheId(int id) => new Holder(id, null).id == id;` over a `Holder` whose `payload` is a `JsonValue?`, which holds an `Any?`. 0.6.39 refused it as "Law holdingKeepsTheId reaches Any in JsonValue. It reads types while the code runs." A law that reads inside such a value, as `new Holder(id, payload).payload?.value`, or calls one of its type's methods, is still refused with that message.

### Added

- `machine`, a value that moves between cases and changes by its moves alone, spec section 20.2 and decision 99. It declares its cases as an enum does, each with an optional value, and a case's block holds the moves out of it, as `case Draft = "draft" { public OrderStatus.Paid pay(int cents) => OrderStatus.Paid(cents); }`. Only its entry points, its moves, `parse` and `Json.decode` build a case, and a property of a machine type has a `private set`, starts at an entry point and changes only as `this.status = this.status.pay(cents)`. The checker refuses a case no entry point or move reaches, a case with no move out that isn't `final`, a `final` case with one, and a case that reaches no `final` case when the machine has one. A move called on a case it doesn't move from throws `Sharp\StateError`, as "OrderStatus is Paid, and pay moves from Draft.", and a `match` over a machine lists every case and takes no `default`.
- A law whose one parameter is a case of a machine is an invariant, spec section 28: the checker proves it after every entry point and every move that can give the case, and a failed proof prints the moves that break it, as "`paidIsPositive` breaks after `open() → Draft`, `pay(cents: 0) → Paid(cents: 0)`". A `throw` in the code a law reaches proves nothing on its path, so a move that throws on a bad argument keeps its invariant.
- A machine marked `[Tag]` stores as a family does, spec sections 10 and 20.2: `Json.encode` writes a case's name under the tag's key with its fields, and `parse` and `Json.decode` read it back, refuse a name the machine has no case of and a field of the wrong type, and run each invariant of the case, throwing a `ValueError` that names the one it breaks, as "OrderStatus.Paid breaks paidIsPositive.". A backed machine's values name its cases, so `[Tag("status")]` alone stores `{"status":"paid","cents":7}`, and a `[Key]` on a case of a backed machine is refused, as "`OrderStatus.Paid` is named `paid` by its value, so it takes no `[Key]`.". A machine with no backing names each case with `[Key]`.
- `mago machine App.Shop.OrderStatus` prints a machine's cases and moves as a Mermaid state diagram: an arrow from the start to each case an entry point gives, one for each move, named by the move and its parameters, and one from each `final` case to the end.

## [0.6.39] - 2026-11-19

### Upgrading from 0.6.38

- Every `extern` line ends in `uses`, spec section 29: `uses none` declares the plain PHP pure, a list names its effects, and `uses any` says it may have any effect. A line without it, as `extern Formatter.format;`, is refused as "State its effects: `uses none`, `uses <effects>`, or `uses any`.", where 0.6.38 read it as pure. Add `uses none` to each such line, after a stated signature too, as `extern string Routing.route(RouteName name) uses none;`.
- Every plain PHP class and function PHP# code calls, creates or reads a property of has an `extern` line, spec section 29. `Mailchimp.lists().addListMember(…)` with no line is refused as "`Mailchimp` has no `extern` line.", where 0.6.38 gave the call an unknown effect. Write `extern Mailchimp uses any;` for the 0.6.38 behavior, and name its effects in place of `any` when you know them. `mago convert` writes such a line for each class and function a converted file reaches.
- A call takes the `extern` line of the class that declares its method, spec section 29, and nothing from the call that returned its object, so `Order.all().sum("total")` needs `extern Collection uses none;` beside the line of `Order.all`'s class, where 0.6.38 gave the returned `Collection` the effects of `all()`. A line of a method a class only inherits, as `extern RemoteFeed.get uses Http;` for `Feed`'s `get`, is refused as "`RemoteFeed.get` is a method of `Feed`, so `extern Feed.get` declares it.", where 0.6.38 read it first. A method's own line overrides its class's line.
- A call of a function plain PHP gives is refused until an `extern` line states its type, spec section 21, as "The type of `Legacy.doubler()`'s function is unknown, so it is never called.", whose help names the line, `extern Function<int(int) uses none> Legacy.doubler() uses none;`. 0.6.38 called it unchecked with an unknown effect. A call of a function a plain PHP property holds has the effects of the `extern` line of the class that declares the property.
- A struct or an enum calls only plain PHP declared `uses none`, spec section 29, where 0.6.38 let it call plain PHP no `extern` declared.
- `mago convert` writes its declarations file to `Stubs/Externs.sharp` in the first PSR-4 folder, in that folder's `Stubs` namespace, where 0.6.38 wrote `Externs.sharp` in the folder itself. Move the `extern namespace` lines of an existing `Externs.sharp` there, or leave the file, whose lines keep working.
- An extension host names the PHP file that registers its worker in `file`, which mago runs inside itself, on the PHP# engine linked into the `mago` binary. Replace each host's `command` with the script it ran: `command = ["php", ".mago/worker.php"]` becomes `file = ".mago/worker.php"`. A relative `file` resolves from the configuration file's directory, which each interpreter starts in. The options `command`, `workers`, `working-directory`, `inherit-environment`, `maximum-payload-size`, `shutdown-timeout-ms` and `stderr-tail-size` are gone, and a configuration that sets one fails to load, naming it. A host that read its arguments from `$argv` reads them from `environment` through `getenv()`.
- All hosts share each interpreter, so two hosts that set one `environment` variable to different values are an error naming both hosts and the variable, and two hosts that declare one class are an error naming both hosts and the class.
- The SDK's contexts no longer carry `cancellation`, and `Mago\Sdk\CancellationTokenInterface` and `Mago\Sdk\Exception\CancelledException` are gone. Delete each `$context->cancellation->throwIfCancelled()` and `isCancelled()` call, and each `catch (CancelledException …)`. A hook that runs past its host's `request-timeout-ms` is interrupted at its next PHP instruction, so it needs no check of its own.
- `PluginRegistry::enableProviderMemoization()` is gone, so delete each call. Mago asks a provider each time an analyzed call matches its target, in the analysis thread's own interpreter, where the call costs what the provider's PHP costs.
- A before-analysis hook runs in one interpreter, so what it keeps on its plugin's object is there alone, and the other analysis threads' interpreters never see it. A provider that answered from such a cache reads what it needs when it's asked, as through `$context->codebase->findMethods()` inside `getPropertyType()`. The codebase caches each answer in that interpreter for the rest of the analysis. A plugin that cleared a cache of its own in `beforeAnalysis` keys it by the new `$context->codebase->generation()` instead, which changes each time Mago analyzes the project again, and fills it again when the generation changes.
- The Linux `mago` a release publishes needs glibc 2.35 or later, as Ubuntu 22.04 and Debian 12 have, where 0.6.38's needed glibc 2.17. It links the PHP# engine, which builds on Ubuntu 22.04 for each architecture.

### Changed

- The standard library's `Builtins.sharp` ends each line in `uses`: each pure function and exception class `uses none`, each class of PHP's DOM `uses any`, since `XMLDocument::createFromFile` reads a file, and `UnitEnum` and `BackedEnum` `uses none`, spec section 29. `ReflectionAttribute::newInstance`, which runs an attribute's constructor, and `ReflectionFunction::invoke`, which runs the function, say `uses any`, and `ReflectionProperty::getHook` says `uses none`. It also declares the PHP classes the standard library runs on: `PDO` and `PDOStatement` `uses Database`, `Random\Randomizer` and `Random\Engine\Mt19937` `uses Random`, `SplFileObject` `uses Console, Files`, and PHP's `Error`, `WeakMap` and `ReflectionEnumUnitCase` `uses none`. A project's own line for one of them is refused as already declared, so delete it.
- `mago analyze`, `mago lint`, `mago compile` and `mago convert` run each extension host inside mago, so a project's hosts run with no `php` installed. The engine and every library its extensions need link into the `mago` binary, which needs only the system's own libraries. Each analysis thread runs one interpreter, which loads mago's own copy of the extension SDK and then every host's file, so a host always speaks the protocol of the `mago` that runs it, whatever SDK version `vendor` holds. An interpreter ignores `php.ini` and runs without a memory limit. 0.6.38 started each host's command as a pool of `php` processes and exchanged frames with them over pipes. A hook that makes no progress for `request-timeout-ms` is interrupted, and its interpreter starts again before its next request; the error no longer names a worker number.
- `mago extension list --json` lists each host's `file`. 0.6.38 listed its worker count and whether the count was adaptive.
- A law without a body proves first with fixed steps, in a law checker that imports only PHP#'s Lean library, spec section 28: each rewrites with every definition the law uses, unfolds each recursion once or until it ends, then closes with `omega` or `decide`. A law with a body proves with Lean's search in a law checker that starts beside the steps' one, and a law the steps leave unproven goes on to that search, as in 0.6.38, so every proof ends as it did, with the same goals reported. 0.6.38 imported all of Lean and ran its search for every law. The agent's loop, a compile without the hosts then an analysis, takes 4.7 s for 5.2 s on a 96-file project with 60 laws and 27 s for 36 s on a 1,536-file one with 960, the law checker built, and 14 to 23% less CPU.

### Fixed

- A law reaches a class that extends other classes, spec section 28. Its value holds the fields of every PHP# class above it, so a law reads a field, calls a method and runs `new` through `: super(…)` with what a PHP# parent declares, at any depth, and `super.m(…)` in a method a law reaches runs the parent's `m`. 0.6.38 refused every law that reached such a class, as "Law carries reaches the class Entry, which extends Stamped in Entry. Declare the members the law reaches in the class itself, in place of inheriting them.", even one that only took the value as a parameter. The first plain PHP class above it, as an ORM's base model, is plain PHP: a law carries the value, and a read of a member that class declares is refused at the read, as "Law readsBase reaches the plain PHP property Base.x in Shape.readsBase. Write the code the law reaches in PHP#, or pass the value plain PHP computes to the law as a parameter.", and so is a `new` that runs its constructor.
- A compile whose law checker Mago's cache folder already holds starts no `lake`, spec section 28. 0.6.38 ran `lake --version` twice before every compile's first proof, 0.20 to 0.98 seconds together on a 96-file project at load 77 to 100. A compile that builds the checker starts `lake build` alone. The checker's build is now named by the Lean version its package pins, so the first compile after upgrading builds the checker once more, and a build whose Lean was uninstalled is built again by the Lean there is.
- A compile that proves a law no longer has Lean derive a `Repr` instance for each class, enum and sealed interface a law reaches, spec section 28. No proof prints a value, so the instances were never read. The law checker runs 2.4 to 3.5% fewer instructions on a 1,536-file project with 960 laws. The law checker's library changes, so the first compile after upgrading builds it once more.
- A law runs on the Lean version Mago pins, found one way, spec section 28: the Lean Mago installed in its cache folder, else elan's toolchain of that version in `$ELAN_HOME` or `~/.elan`, else the Lean Mago downloads into its cache folder. 0.6.38 ran the `lake` first on the `PATH`, whatever Lean it belonged to, so a `lake` of another Lean version built the law checker on that version, and a `lake` that failed refused every law as `law-checker-unavailable`. A `lake` on the `PATH` is never run now, and elan need not be on the `PATH`.
- `mago analyze` refuses a law that reaches the standard library or another package's PHP# file only where `mago compile` refuses it, spec section 28. It translates each PHP# file a law reaches, in `paths` or `includes`, where 0.6.38 translated only the files of `paths` and refused each law that reached any other as "Law keepsTheInstant is not proven: it reaches Instant, whose file has errors. Fix them, then run vendor/bin/mago compile again.", though that file had none. A law is refused for the errors of a file it reaches only when the analysis reported an error in that file.

## [0.6.38] - 2026-11-19

### Added

- An `extern` line's parameter takes the type `callable`, spec sections 7 and 23, so `extern bool WordPress.add_action(string hookName, callable callback, int priority = 10, int acceptedArgs = 1);` states a plain PHP hook's callback, and `WordPress.add_action("save_post", this.saved, 10, 3)` passes any function value, a method value, a lambda or a `Function<…>` value, which plain PHP receives as the closure it is. 0.6.37 refused `callable` as "Unexpected `callable`". Anywhere else, a local, a property, a return type, a PHP# parameter or a type argument, `callable` is refused as "`callable` is only the type of an extern line's parameter.", and a string passed for a function value, as `WordPress.add_action("init", "my_function")`, is refused as "Argument #2 of function `WordPress.add_action` is a string, and a string is never a function value.", for a `Function<…>` parameter too, where 0.6.37 reported "Possible argument type mismatch".
- An `extern` line's parameter takes a literal default, as a method's does, spec section 23, and a PHP# call that leaves the parameter out passes the line's default to plain PHP by the library's name, whatever default the library declares, so `WordPress.add_action("deleted_post", this.deleted)` passes `priority: 10, accepted_args: 1`. 0.6.37 refused every default on an `extern` line. A default that is no literal is refused as "An `extern` line's default is a literal, which a call that leaves `at` out passes to plain PHP.", and one the parameter's type does not take, as `int priority = "10"`, as "Default value for parameter `priority` is not assignable to its declared type.".

### Fixed

- A call in a law that leaves out an argument passes its parameter's default, as the call does at run time, spec section 28, for a constructor, a static method and a method alike. 0.6.37 refused it as "Law enqueuedHoldsNoError reaches a default argument in Pending.enqueued. Pass every argument, the ones with a default value included.", so with `Pending(string? error = null)`, a factory `Pending.enqueued() => new Pending()` could not appear in a law, and each law restated each default by hand. `Pending.enqueued().error == null` now proves. A default a law can't read is refused, naming its parameter, only where a call leaves its argument out, as "Law cappedAtMax reaches the operator `**` here in the default of `limit` in Shape.capped.".
- A law compares two values of a struct that declares no `operator ==` field by field, each with that field's own `==`, and two values of a class that declares `operator ==` with it, spec section 19. 0.6.37 refused both as "Law deliveredAt reaches the class operator `==` in Pending.deliveredAt. Compare the values the objects hold, such as their properties.", so a law could not say that `new Pending().deliver(at).doneAt == at` over two `Instant`s. It now proves, and a claim the fields break is not proven. A struct with a field whose `==` a law can't read, as an `Instant?`, keeps that refusal.
- A law orders two values of a class that declares no `<=>` through the `operator <=>` of the sealed interface it implements, the one the checker runs, spec sections 19 and 28. 0.6.37 refused `new Book(low) < new Book(high)` over a `Priced` interface's `<=>` as "Law cheaperFirst reaches the operator `<` here in Book.cheaperFirst.", though the code compiled and ran it. It now proves, and a false order is not proven. An operator's use now records a reference to the operator, as a call records one to its method, so a law reaches exactly the operators it applies.
- `mago analyze --scan-only` refuses no law, spec section 28. A scan reads no method body, and 0.6.37 still translated each law over the methods it reaches, so with `public bool meets(Level other) => this.rank >= other.rank;` and `law meetsItself(Level a) => a.meets(a);` it refused the law as "Law meetsItself reaches the method Level.meets in Level.meetsItself. A law reads a method's body, and this method has none it can read." and exited 1, which stopped a project that builds its class list from a scan. The scan now prints no issue, and `mago analyze` and `mago compile` refuse laws as before.

## [0.6.37] - 2026-11-19

### Upgrading from 0.6.36

- `Method.invoke` is gone, spec section 25. Write `method.invoke(receiver, arguments)` as `method.createInvoker<Function<Any?(Object, List<Any?>) uses …>>()(receiver, arguments)`, with the effects the method has, and a static method's `method.invoke(null, arguments)` as `method.createInvoker<Function<Any?(List<Any?>) uses …>>()(arguments)`. A call of `invoke` is refused as "Method `Method.invoke` does not exist.". A plain PHP method with no `extern` may have every effect, so no invoker holds it, as no delegate does.

### Added

- `Method.createInvoker<TFunction>()` gives a value of the function type `TFunction` that calls the method with its arguments as one `List<Any?>`, after the receiver of an instance method, spec sections 25 and 29. So a dispatcher held to `uses Database` calls `pause(Subscription s, VerifiedUser u)` and `create(string name, Plan plan = Plan.Normal)` by name, each through one invoker, where `invoke` may have every effect and no `uses` accepted it. The method fits `TFunction` as it fits `createDelegate`'s, so a method with `Mail` throws "`App.Billing.notify` has the effect `App.Mailer`, and `Function<Any?(Object, List<Any?>) uses Sharp.Data.Database>` allows only `Sharp.Data.Database`." at `createInvoker`, which is pure. A call checks each value against the parameter at its place and throws `TypeError` naming it, and a parameter the list leaves out takes its default when that runs no code, as `Parameter.defaultValue()` reads it.

### Changed

- `mago convert` writes a static call through a class held at run time, `$kind::label(7)`, and its method value, `$kind::find(...)`, through the method's invoker, spec section 25: `kind.methods().first(method => method.name == nameof(Order.label)).createInvoker<Function<string(List<Any?>) uses Database>>()([7])`. The invoker lists the effects the analysis solves for a PHP# method, or those a plain PHP method's `extern` line declares, and gives the result the method declares where PHP checks it. Where only PHPDoc gives the result, it gives `Any?`, which `as` checks, as 0.6.36 did. A plain PHP method no `extern` declares may have every effect, which no `uses` lists, so the file stays plain PHP, and the refusal names the line to add, as `extern App.Order.label;`.

### Fixed

- The extension SDK tells a member the compiler generates from one the source writes: `FunctionLikeMetadata::$isGenerated` and `PropertyMetadata::$isGenerated` are true for a member no source declares, which `MetadataFlags::GENERATED` marks in `$flags` too. 0.6.36 showed a generated member exactly as a written one, with the same file, a name location and the variant as its class, so a rule over the methods a program declares counted a `[Tag]` variant's `parse` and `tryParse`, or read the source again to skip them. They are now generated, as are a struct's or a PHP# class's `parse` and `tryParse`, an enum's `cases`, `from` and `tryFrom` and its `$name` and `$value`, a case's factory, an `Iterable<T>` type's `getIterator`, an indexer's default `offsetExists`, `offsetUnset` and `offsetSet`, and a constructor the code leaves out. A generated member keeps all its other metadata.
- A law reaches the standard library from its PHP# source, as it reaches the project's code, spec section 28. 0.6.36 refused every standard library class but a fixed list of members, so a law over one state of a sealed family was refused when another state held an `Instant`, as "Law postponeHoldsTheError reaches the standard library class Instant in Done.", though the law never reads that state. `Instant` now translates from its declaration, and a law that orders two `Instant`s with `<` proves through `Instant`'s own `operator <=>`, as `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` and `<=>` on two values of any class that declares `<=>` do. A standard library member that calls plain PHP is refused where the law reaches it, with the reason project code gets, as "Law parses reaches the plain PHP function `global.preg_match` in Instant.tryParse.". `Math.max`, `Math.min` and a List's `count()`, `map` and `filter` keep the checker's own model.
- A watch keeps an extension rule's error that an after-file hook reports in another file until the hooks of the file that reported it run again, as a one-shot `mago analyze` of the same tree reports it. 0.6.36 kept such an error with the file it points at, so when `src/audit.php`'s hook reported an error in `src/date.php`, an edit of `src/date.php` alone dropped it, and no later round brought it back, since the hook of `src/audit.php` did not run again. An error the hook stops reporting, as after an edit of `src/audit.php`, leaves at that file's next round. The worker now names the file whose hooks reported each after-file error, so the analyzer protocol is 1.16, and an extension host runs the `heyjordanparker/mago-sharp` of the same release as `mago`.

## [0.6.36] - 2026-11-19

### Added

- `ghcr.io/heyjordanparker/php-sharp:<version>-dev`, the runtime image with the law checker's Lean installed in Mago's cache folder, `/var/cache/mago`, which every user reads and writes. A compile with laws in a container built from it downloads nothing: in a 6-CPU arm64 container, the first compile of a project with 9 laws took 6.6 to 7.7 seconds there, run as root or with `--user`, and 17.9 to 19.1 seconds in `<version>`, where `mago` downloads the Lean first. `ghcr.io/heyjordanparker/php-sharp:<version>` stays the runtime image, at its size, for production.
- `heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer` ships the spec its release follows, at `vendor/heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer/spec.md`, so a project can read each section a refusal or the standard library cites, as `Sharp/Method.sharp` cites section 29. The release copies the release tag's `docs/spec.md` into the package's tag, so the two never differ.

### Changed

- `mago compile` installs the law checker's Lean itself, from Lean's release archive, which it unpacks while it downloads, on threads of their own, into `lean/` in Mago's cache folder. It installs nothing else: 0.6.35 installed elan, which downloaded the whole archive and then unpacked it on one core. The archive's SHA-256 is pinned for each platform, and the Lean moves into place only once the download matches it. In a 6-CPU arm64 container with an empty cache, the first compile of a project with 9 laws took 28.2 to 29.2 seconds on 0.6.35 and takes 17.9 to 19.1. A `lake` on the `PATH` is still used first. The `elan/` folder and `elan.lock` an earlier `mago` left in Mago's cache folder are no longer read: delete them to free about 3 GB.
- Spec section 5's strict rules run on every package whose `composer.json` does not say `"type": "library"`: a public type or member too wide, a public member whose types hold `Any`, and, from section 24, a string fixed at compile time that selects. 0.6.35 ran them only where `composer.json` said `"type": "project"`, so an application that left the type out, or had no `composer.json`, compiled a public member nothing outside its class used. A package with no type, one with no `composer.json` above it and one whose `composer.json` does not parse are now projects, so no line left out turns a rule off. A library adds `"type": "library"` to its `composer.json`. The standard library and every package installed in `vendor/` stay exempt whatever their type.

### Fixed

- `mago analyze` reports every refusal of a law that `mago compile` reports before the proof, spec section 28: a construct the law reaches that a law cannot reason about, as a type test of an interface that is not sealed or a standard library class, a declaration it reaches in a file with errors, and a law that calls itself in a cycle. 0.6.35 reported them only at the compile, so a watch showed a file clean while `mago compile` refused it with "Law shapeIsShape reaches a type test of the interface Figure in Shape.isShape. A law tells apart only the implementers of a sealed interface. Declare `Figure` sealed.". `mago analyze` and its watch now report it, in each round, and never start the law checker, so a law's proof is still checked only at the compile.
- An `Any` joined with an `Any` is an `Any`, spec section 12, where one of them is an element read that may be past the end of its `List`. 0.6.35 joined such a pair as `Any?`, so `List<Any> write = written < request.byteLength() ? [this.streams[0]] : [];`, with `streams` a `List<Any>`, was refused as "Invalid assignment to `write`: it is declared as `List<Any>`.", though 0.6.34 compiled it, and so was `Any picked = flag ? this.streams[0] : held;`. Both now compile. An `Any` joined with an `Any?` is still an `Any?`.
- `mago convert` writes a class, an interface or an enum of a project that nothing outside its namespace uses `internal`, spec section 5, as it already gave each member the narrowest access its uses need. 0.6.35 wrote every converted type `public`, so the compile refused one only its namespace used, as "`Basket` is public, and nothing outside `App` uses it.", and the conversion's own proof kept the file plain PHP. A type that code outside its namespace uses, or that plain PHP or a framework reaches through an `[Export]` method or a marked attribute, stays `public`, and a library's types stay `public`. The converter and `too-wide-member` read a type's uses through one function, so the two never disagree.
- A plain PHP call on a side of `??`, in a branch of `? :` or in a `match` arm, whose value goes into a declared `Map` or `Set`, runs with no run-time array test, spec section 12, since the call is already read as the `Map` or `Set` the place declares. 0.6.35 still tested the whole value, so `Map<int, string> named = given ?? Calc.names();` ran `\is_array($crossing = $given ?? \Sharp\Internal\returned(…)) ? \Sharp\Map::from(…) : $crossing` on each evaluation, though that test never held. It now runs `$named = $given ?? \Sharp\Internal\returned(…);`. A plain PHP property, or a call whose result type PHP# can't write, beside it still crosses into the object.

## [0.6.35] - 2026-11-19

### Added

- A law tells apart the states of a sealed interface and reads the data each state holds, spec section 28. A sealed interface's implementers are every value it has, so `is` and `match` over its value test which implementer it is, and the branch reads that implementer's get-only properties. With `public sealed interface OrderStatus { }`, its implementers `Pending` and `Paid`, and `public OrderStatus settle(int at) => new Paid(at);`, the laws `p.settle(at) is Paid paid ? paid.paidAt == at : false` and `Pending.start() is Pending` prove, and `Pending.start() is Paid` is refused as not proven. 0.6.34 refused each such test as "It reads types while the code runs.". A data case's fields read in a law too: through its pattern, as `PaymentResult.Paid(int paid) => paid > 0`, which 0.6.34 refused as reading types, and as a property of a value a pattern names, as `paid.amount`, which 0.6.34 refused as "A law tells the cases of an enum apart, and reads none of the data a case carries.". A type test of an interface that is not sealed is now refused as "A law tells apart only the implementers of a sealed interface. Declare `Figure` sealed."

### Fixed

- A list literal on a side of `??`, in a branch of `? :` or in a `match` arm takes the declared type of the place the value goes into, spec section 12, as a literal written there alone does. 0.6.34 stopped at the operator, so `Set<string> tags = maybe ?? ["a"];` was refused as "Invalid assignment to `tags`: it is declared as `Set<string>`.", though `const tags = maybe ?? ["a"];` and `Set<string> tags = ["a"];` compiled. It now compiles, and the literal runs as a `Set`, as do `flag ? ["b"] : tags` and a `match` arm `true => ["c"]` where a `Set` is declared.
- A `??` or a `match` whose value goes into a declared `Set` or `Map` runs with no run-time array test when none of its sides can be a PHP array, spec section 12, as a `? :` already did. 0.6.34 tested every such value, so `Set<string> owning = maybe ?? fallback;` ran `\is_array($crossing = $maybe ?? $fallback) ? new \Sharp\Set(…) : $crossing` on each evaluation, though both sides are `Set`s. It now runs `$owning = $maybe ?? $fallback;`. A side that plain PHP gives still crosses into the object, as in `Map<int, string> named = given ?? Calc.names();`.
