- `mago convert` finds the `extern` line of a call of a plain PHP trait's member as the checker does, spec section 29: the trait's line covers every class that takes the member from the trait. 0.6.34 looked from the call's class only, so with `extern Dispatchable.dispatch uses Http;`, an enum's `ReceiveEvent::dispatch($this->value)` converted, though the checker gives that call the effect `Http`, which an enum never has. It is now refused as "`ReceiveEvent::dispatch` has the effect `Sharp\Net\Http`, and a PHP# enum has no effects (spec section 29)".
- A plain PHP call on a side of `??`, in a branch of `? :` or in a `match` arm keeps the keys plain PHP gave it where the value goes into a declared `Map`, spec section 12, as the call written there alone does. 0.6.34 read the call as a `List` and renumbered it, so with `Calc.names()` returning `[5 => 'a', 9 => 'b']`, `Map<int, string> named = given ?? Calc.names();` held `{0: "a", 1: "b"}` when `given` was null, with no error. It now holds `{5: "a", 9: "b"}`, as `Map<int, string> named = Calc.names();` does.
- `mago compile` works out once which files each file's bodies reach, and the inputs that reach them, where 0.6.34 walked each file's chain of body reads again from scratch and then took the inputs of every file on it, on one thread. On a generated project of 2,000 classes that each read the one before through a call, in a 6-CPU container, the step took 1.64 to 1.74 seconds of a 2.6 to 2.9-second compile and now takes 0.17 to 0.23 seconds. Each compiled file lists the same inputs as before.

## [0.6.34] - 2026-11-19

### Changed

- A `public` type that nothing outside its namespace uses is too wide, spec section 5, as a member is: a class, an interface, a struct, an enum and an event alike, so `public event Quiet(int id);`, which only `App.Shop` creates, is refused as "`Quiet` is public, and nothing outside `App.Shop` uses it." 0.6.33 checked only members, so a public type that only its namespace used compiled. Declare such a type `internal`. A use of the type or of a member of it counts, an `extends` or an `implements` included, and so do plain PHP calling one of its `[Export]` methods and a framework reading a marked attribute. A class whose only reach is a listener, a method written with `on`, is refused too, since PHP# creates it whatever its access.

### Fixed

- `[DynamicallyAccessed]` on a class, struct, enum or interface that is no attribute class is a compile error, spec section 5, as on a member it already was. 0.6.33 accepted `[DynamicallyAccessed] public struct DomainRecord { … }`, and the mark did nothing, so each of its members was still refused as too wide with no message saying why. It is now refused as "`[DynamicallyAccessed]` marks an attribute class, and `DomainRecord` is not one. Mark the attribute that `DomainRecord` carries." On an attribute class, or on the `extern` line of a plain PHP one, it compiles as before.
- A `new` of a plain PHP class whose `extern` line states its constructor's signature takes the line's parameters, each function type with the effects its `uses` lists, as a call of a method or a function already did, spec sections 23 and 29. 0.6.33 checked the constructor's arguments against the library's own docblock, so with `extern void Registry.__construct(Map<string, Function<Any?(Any?) uses Database>> functions);` over a docblock `array<non-empty-string, callable>`, `new Registry(handlers)` was refused as "expected `Map<string, Function<Any?(Any?)>>`, but possibly received …", and a closure with an effect the line leaves out passed. A closure with `Mail` is now refused as "This lambda calls `Mailer.send`, which has the effect `Mail`, so a `Function<Any?(Any?) uses Database>` cannot hold it.". A write that fits a `Map` or `List` declared with a type keeps that type, so after `handlers[name] = (Any? ...arguments) => …` the local is still `Map<string, Function<Any?(Any?) uses Database>>`, not a union with the closure's own `Function<scalar?(Any?)>`. A loop that fills such a `Map` with closures and passes it to `new Registry(handlers)` now compiles.
- A union of `string`, `int`, `float` and `bool` keeps its four types, spec section 7. 0.6.33 merged them into `scalar`, which PHP# can't write, so a call of a method declared `(string|int|float|bool)?` stopped the compile of its file with "The lowering of app/Schema/Runner.sharp panicked though the checker accepted it", and errors named the type as `scalar?`. The call now compiles, and an error names it as written, as "expected `int`, but possibly received `(bool|int|float|string)?`". Plain PHP files print the four types where they printed `scalar` too.
- An `extern` line of a plain PHP trait's member covers every class that takes the member from the trait, spec section 29. 0.6.33 looked the call up from the class it was made through, and its parent classes, never the trait, so with `extern Dispatchable.dispatch uses Database;` and a PHP# `ReceiveEvent : Dispatchable`, `ReceiveEvent.dispatch(…)` was refused under `uses Database` as "`Inbox.receive` calls `ReceiveEvent.dispatch`, which has no `extern` declaration". It now has the effect `Database`. A class that writes `dispatch` itself runs its own body, which the line does not cover.
- `too-wide-member` names the narrowest access level that reaches every use of a `public` member, `private`, `protected` or `internal`, spec section 5. 0.6.33 checked only whether code outside the namespace used it, and named only `internal`, so a method that only its own class calls and a subclass in another namespace overrides stayed `public`, as `Report.title` of `App.Shop`, which `Sales` of `App.Web` overrides. It is now refused as "`Report.title` is public, and nothing outside `Report` and its subclasses uses it." with "Declare it `protected`.", and a member only its class uses as "nothing outside `Cart` uses it." with "Declare it `private`.". A `virtual` or `abstract` member is never told `private`. A property override now counts as a use from the overriding class, as a method override does, so a `virtual` property that a subclass in another namespace overrides is no longer refused as `internal`.
- A local without a written type keeps each tuple its value holds as that tuple, as a `Map`'s value or a `List`'s element, spec section 30. 0.6.33 widened such a tuple to a `List` of its element types, so with `const positions = Parser.positions(html)`, a `Map<string, [string tag, string? attribute]>`, `const [tag, attribute] = positions.get(token) ?? throw …` was refused as "Only a tuple unpacks, and `positions.get(token) ?? throw …` is a `List<string?>`", and so was a `List<[string, int]>` local read with `getOrNull(0)`. Both now unpack, each local with its element's type, and `[string tag, string? attribute] one = positions.get(token) ?? throw …` compiles.
- `isNotEmpty()` and `isEmpty()` narrow a `List` to a non-empty one where the test says it holds an element, inside the `if` and past an early `return`, spec section 12, as any function whose body returns a count compared with `0` does, a plain PHP `return count($items) > 0;` included. 0.6.33 narrowed nothing there, so `if (members.isNotEmpty()) { CheckedType.union(members); }`, where `union` takes a `non-empty-list`, was refused as "Argument #1 of method `CheckedType.union` takes a non-empty `List<string>`, and `members` may be empty.", and so was the call after `if (members.isEmpty()) { return; }`. A plain PHP `@psalm-assert-if-true non-empty-list $items` with no element type, or `non-empty-array`, now narrows a list too, and a plain PHP `is_string($value)` body narrows its caller's value in a file with a `namespace`, as it did in a file without one.
- A plain PHP method that a class reaches through a `@mixin` and that returns `$this` or `static` gives a PHP# call the receiver's type, type arguments included, spec section 25, as a method the class declares itself does. 0.6.33 gave it `Self`, so with `RowQuery<TRow> : Builder<TRow>` over Laravel's Eloquent `Builder`, whose `orderBy` its `@mixin` of the query builder declares, `RowQuery.of(rows).where("status", "published").orderBy("id")` was a `Self` in errors, as "Self cannot be used as RowQuery<Post>". It is now a `RowQuery<BaseModel>`, through a chain of such calls too, and the same return where `RowQuery<Post>` is declared is refused as "RowQuery<BaseModel> cannot be used as RowQuery<Post>.".
- The check of a string fixed at compile time reads declared types, spec section 24, so a member read through a value from data is its member's declared type, as `limit.value` of a string-backed enum is a `string`. 0.6.33 read the union of the cases' values instead, so `limit.value == feature.value` was refused as "`==` compares `limit.value` with `feature.value`, a string fixed at compile time, so it selects one of a closed set of values.", and so were `limit.value.equals(feature.value)`, `names.contains(feature.value)` and `counts[feature.value]` as a `Map` key. Each now compiles. A literal, a constant and a member of a case named in the code are still fixed, so `order.status == "paid"`, `feature.value == "reports"` and `status == Feature.Reports.value` are still refused.
- A list literal beside a `Set` in `??`, `? :` or a `match` whose value goes into no declared type is that `Set`, spec section 12, as a literal compared with a `Set` in `==` already was. 0.6.33 accepted it but ran it as a PHP array, so `const owning = owners.get(key) ?? []; owning.add(key);` compiled and then threw "Sharp\Internal\addKey(): Argument #1 ($table) must be of type Sharp\Set, array given". It now runs, and so do `flag ? tags : []` and a `match` arm `0 => []` beside a `Set`. A list literal beside a `Map` there is refused as "`[]` is an empty List. An empty Map is written `[:]`.", and one beside both a `List` and a `Set` as "`[]` could be a `List<string>` or a `Set<string>` here. Declare the one it is, as in `Set<string> values = [];`."

## [0.6.33] - 2026-11-19

### Changed

- `Parameter.defaultValue()` is pure under every `uses`, and throws `ValueError` for a default that runs code, a `new`, a call or a lambda, without running it, spec sections 25 and 29. 0.6.32 gave it the effects of every parameter default the analysis loaded, so with only constant ones a method held to `uses none` read any parameter's default, a plain PHP vendor class's `__construct(Transport $t = new SmtpTransport())` included, and ran `SmtpTransport`'s constructor inside pure code. `Parameter.hasConstantDefault` now tells a default that runs no code apart, so a reader that fills a call's missing arguments reads those and leaves out the others, and the call then runs those defaults under its own effects. A read of such a default now throws "`defaultValue` reads a default that runs no code, and `tag` of `total` has one that runs code, which `defaultValue` never runs". `Property.defaultValue()` and `Field.defaultValue()` keep the effects of the initial values they can run.

### Fixed

- `mago compile` works out once which source files each declaration's signature edit reaches, and builds each compiled file's list of inputs from that. 0.6.32 walked the dependencies again from scratch for each file, and for each file whose bodies it reads, on one thread and on every compile, warm or cold, so the step grew with the files times their reach and a one-line body edit took as long as a compile from nothing. On a generated project of 2,000 classes that each take the one before as a parameter, the step took 374 seconds of a 378-second compile and now takes 1.6 seconds, and the compile takes 6 seconds and 3.3 seconds of processor time instead of 378 and 372. Each compiled file lists the same inputs as before.

## [0.6.32] - 2026-11-19

### Changed

- `Field.set` writes a lazy object that has not loaded without loading it, and replaces `Property.setBeforeLoad`, which is gone, spec sections 25 and 29. `setBeforeLoad` ran the property's `set` accessor, so it may have had every effect, and 0.6.31 refused it in every method held to a `uses`, as "`Ghosts.reference` reaches `Property.setBeforeLoad`, which may have every effect, so `uses Database, Events` on `Saves.reference` cannot accept it." So no method with a `uses` could give a `createLazy` ghost its identity before it loads. Write it with `typeof(Row).fields()[0].set(ghost, id)`, which runs no accessor and is pure, as PHP 8.4's `setRawValueWithoutLazyInitialization` writes. A read of that storage never loads the ghost, and the ghost keeps the value once it loads. A call of `setBeforeLoad` is now refused as "Method `Property.setBeforeLoad` does not exist.". `Field.get` on a ghost that has not loaded still throws `Error` rather than load it.
- `fields()` lists every property of a plain PHP class that declares no hook, at every visibility, whether or not a subclass may replace it, spec section 25. 0.6.31 listed only its private ones, so an ORM's ghost could not reach the protected key, table and attribute properties of a plain PHP base class through `fields()`. `Field.get` and `Field.set` throw `Error` where the receiver's class gives the property an accessor, as a PHP 8.4 subclass may by declaring it again, as "`set` writes the storage of Lib\PlainLedger::$code, and Lib\HookedLedger gives it an accessor, which `set` never runs", and run neither accessor. 0.6.31 read and wrote such storage past the accessor.

### Fixed

- `mago compile` reports an extension host's issues on each `.sharp` file of an `includes` folder outside `vendor/`, as on a file under `paths`. 0.6.31 left them out on every file of `includes`, so with `includes = ["vendor", "lib"]`, a `lib/Shop/Report.sharp` that broke a project rule compiled and ran. A host's issue is now left out only on a Composer package's `.sharp` file, the standard library's included, which only the checker's own errors refuse.

## [0.6.31] - 2026-11-19

### Fixed

- A method that a class outside its namespace overrides or implements is never too wide, spec sections 5 and 22. A method must be visible to each class that replaces it, and 0.6.30 counted only the code that calls it. So with an abstract `Base` in `App.Shop` whose `name()` and `invoke()` only `App.Shop` calls, and `One` in `App.Web` that overrides both, it refused each, as "`Base.name` is public, and nothing outside `App.Shop` uses it.", though `One` reaches both from `App.Web`. An override from outside the namespace now counts as a use from there. A method that only its own namespace and the namespaces below it override and call is still refused.
- An edit of a method body in one `.sharp` file leaves every file that reads it running, spec section 27. 0.6.30 refused each file that names the edited class, as `typeof(Money)`, a parameter of type `Money` or a call of `money.amount()` does, with "Label.sharp is out of date (Money.sharp changed). Run vendor/bin/mago compile.", until the next compile, and that compile then restamped each such file. Each compiled file now names the hash of its file's declarations, and a file that reads another `.sharp` file of the project records that hash beside its stamp, so the engine runs it while the other file's compiled file names the hash, reading only that file's header. The edited file itself is still refused until it compiles, and the compile lowers a reader again when a return or an effect it took from the edited body changed. On a 1,628-file project with an extension host, a body edit of one class now rewrites 1 compiled file instead of 46, and takes 0.38 to 0.47 seconds of `mago`'s processor time instead of 0.46 to 0.49. A signature edit still makes each reader out of date once the edited file compiles, and an edit of a plain PHP file, a file in `vendor/`, a source a law reaches, or a `.sharp` file that states an `extern` line or declares an event or a struct makes each reader out of date as before. Compiled files from 0.6.30 are refused as compiled for a different engine until `mago compile` runs.
- `mago analyze` refuses each call whose effects a `uses` rules out where the call reaches a package's `.sharp` file, the standard library's included, as `mago compile` refuses it, spec section 29. 0.6.30's `mago analyze` read no body of a package, so a method implementing a pure interface method with `typeof(Step).properties()[0].set(step, next)` passed it, and only `mago compile` refused it, as "`Reflects.transitionTo` reaches `Property.set`, which may have every effect, so the pure `Publishes.transitionTo` cannot accept it." `mago analyze` now summarizes the bodies of each package's `.sharp` files and reports nothing else of them, so both commands give the same refusals. A cold `mago analyze` of a project of about 650 PHP# files and the standard library's 92 now takes 0.92 seconds of processor time instead of 0.84 and 0.32 seconds of wall time instead of 0.30, and a watch round reads each unchanged package file's summaries again without analyzing it.
- An error writes the class a method was called on as `Self`, at any depth of the types it names, spec section 25. 0.6.30 wrote it as the class the code is in, so in a class with `public virtual BelongsTo<Offer, Offer> parent() => this.belongsTo(typeof(Offer));`, whose body gives a `BelongsTo<Offer, Self>` from Laravel's `$this`, it refused the method as "Returned type `BelongsTo<Offer, Offer>` is less specific than the declared return type `BelongsTo<Offer, Offer>`", and against an interface declaring `BelongsTo<Offer, Self>` as "incompatible with parent return type `BelongsTo<Offer, Offer>`", two types that read the same. The refusals stay, since a subclass's override makes `Self` the subclass, and they now read "Returned type `BelongsTo<Offer, Self>` is less specific than the declared return type `BelongsTo<Offer, Offer>`" and "incompatible with parent return type `BelongsTo<Offer, Self>`". Declaring `BelongsTo<Offer, Self>` compiles against both.
- An extension host's rules report only on the project's own files, those under the configuration's `paths`, in `mago compile` as in `mago analyze`. `mago compile` checks each package's `.sharp` file beside the project's, the standard library's included, and 0.6.30 reported a host's issues on them too, so a project rule that bans a word in names refused the standard library's `Sharp/IO/Console.sharp` and printed "vendor/heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer/library/Sharp/IO/Console.sharp comes from the Composer package heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer. Update the package, or report the error to its maintainers." A host's issue on a file of `includes` is now left out, from its before-analysis, after-file and after-analysis hooks alike, and a package's `.sharp` file is refused only for the checker's own errors.

## [0.6.30] - 2026-11-18

### Fixed

- With extension hosts, `mago analyze --watch` compiles each edit for its hosts from its last compile. 0.6.29 compiled the whole project again for the hosts after every edit, with a new analysis, every input stamped and every folder listed, 1.2 to 1.9 seconds of every recheck. The watch now keeps that analysis and the record of the compiled files, analyzes and lowers again only the edited files and the files whose record lists a read of a declaration the edit changed, stamps only their inputs, and keeps unread each compiled file whose key and input stamps hold and which is the file the last compile wrote. On a 1,628-file PHP# project with an extension host, a body edit of one class now takes 0.43 to 0.49 seconds of `mago`'s processor time instead of 2.59 to 3.13, a signature edit 0.83 to 0.93 instead of 3.09 to 3.39, or 2.39 to 2.69 instead of 4.63 to 4.94 with a host whose hooks read the edited classes, and an edit of 188 files 2.28 to 2.79 instead of 4.31 to 4.75. Each round writes the hosts' folder a fresh compile of the tree writes, `compiled.bin` byte for byte included, and reports the same issues. `mago compile` keeps each such compiled file unread too.
- A `for` loop's update step reads the locals as the loop's condition narrowed them whenever the body leaves them alone. 0.6.29 refused `for (Node? node = head; node != null; node = node.next) { }` at `node.next`, as "Attempting to access a property on a possibly `null` value.", with an empty body, a body that only calls something, as `node.touch();`, or a body that always breaks, and so it refused an update step that only reads `node`, while the same loop with `total += node.value;` in its body compiled. A body that writes `node` again still leaves the update step a `node` that may be `null`, and it is refused there once, where 0.6.29 refused it twice.
- An event's parameters are never too wide, spec sections 5 and 15. They are its payload, public get-only properties that take no modifier, so 0.6.29 refused each one no code outside the event's namespace read, as "`Changed.id` is public, and nothing outside `App.Shop` uses it.", and asked for `internal`, which no event parameter can take. An event nothing uses is still refused, as "Nothing uses `Lost`, so it never runs."
- A struct's or a PHP# class's fields count as used where code encodes it with `Json.encode` or reads it with `parse`, `tryParse` or `Json.decode`, spec sections 5 and 10, inside a `List`, a `Set`, a `Map`, another struct's field and each variant of a family too. 0.6.29 counted only a read by name, so with `public struct Row { public int id { get; init; } public string name { get; init; } }` in `Demo`, encoded by `Json.encode(row)` and read by `Row.parse(input)` in `Other`, it refused each field, as "`Row.id` is public, and nothing outside `Demo` uses it.", though making it `internal` takes it out of the JSON. A public field the JSON never holds and no code outside its namespace reads is still refused.
- An `extern` line states any return type a PHP# method declares, spec section 23. 0.6.29 refused `extern Function<void()> Factory.make();` with "Unexpected `Function`", and read neither `extern [int, int] Factory.pair();` nor `extern (int|string)? Factory.either();` as an `extern` line, so both failed to parse. Each now compiles, and `Factory.make()()` calls the function the line states, so `Factory.make()(1)` is refused for its argument. A union in parentheses still takes `?` after it, as on a method's return type.
- A class value that passes `is Class<Linkable>`, where `Linkable` is an interface, is a class value of both its own type and `Linkable`, spec section 21, as an object that passes `is Linkable` is. 0.6.29 narrowed a `Class<Entity>` whose class does not itself implement `Linkable` to `never`, though a subclass of `Entity` may, so `if (type is Class<Linkable>) { return type.name(); }` was refused as "Method `name` does not exist on `never`", and so was the same test on a `Class<T>` of a type parameter. The value is now a `Class<Entity&Linkable>`, which passes as either, and a `Class<T>` stays a `Class<T>` whose bound holds `Linkable`. A `Class<Label>` of a final class that does not implement `Linkable` still never passes, and the test is refused as impossible.
- An error never asks a `.sharp` file to write a PHP built-in class it cannot name, decision 83. 0.6.29 refused an `int` passed to a plain PHP parameter declared `\Closure(): \PDO` with "Change the argument value to match `Function<PDO()>`, or update the parameter's type declaration.", and a local that holds such a function, given an `int`, with "Assign a `Function<PDO()>` value", though no `.sharp` file names `PDO`. The argument's error now says "`Function<PDO()>` holds `PDO`, which a `.sharp` file cannot name, so make this call to `Pool.using` from plain PHP.", and the local's says to give the value a local of its own. A value of that type the library itself returns still passes to the parameter.
- An `as` whose value is never of its target type where it runs is refused at the cast, as one error that names both types, spec section 21. 0.6.29 refused `(x as Paid).amount` after `if (x is not Paid)`, where `Payment` is an open interface, as "Attempting to access a property on a possibly `null` value." and "Property `Payment.amount` does not exist.", and the same cast on a sealed `Payment` as "This pattern never matches the value it tests.", "Redundant ternary operator: condition is always `false`." and "Attempting to access a property on an expression that is always `null`.". Each is now refused once, as "`x` is a `Payment` that is never a `Paid` here, so `x as Paid` always gives `null`.", and the read of `.amount` reports nothing more. An `as` whose value's type can never be the target, as `circle as Square` or `this.total() as string`, takes the same error in place of the one it had. An `as` that can succeed is unchanged.
- A call of an enum case's `name` never runs the name as a function, spec section 14, which calls only a property that holds a function like a method. With `audience: Level | Interaction`, an enum and an interface that declares `name()`, 0.6.29 refused `audience.name()` as ambiguous and also as "Function `High` does not exist." and "Function `Low` does not exist.", one for each case. The ambiguity is now the only error. `level.name()` on a `Level` alone is refused once, as "Method `Level.name` does not exist.", where 0.6.29 gave the two errors that named a function. A case's `value` is treated the same way.

## [0.6.29] - 2026-11-18

### Changed

- `mago compile` proves a project's laws on its threads, in one Lean process. 0.6.28 ran one proof at a time, under a processor-time limit on the whole Lean process, and started Lean through Lake. On a 1,638-file project with 20 laws, fresh compiles that took 381 to 1,236 seconds now take 132 to 247, with the same processor time. Each law's proof now has a budget of Lean's own: 200,000 heartbeats, Lean's default for one command, about 140 processor seconds on an M2 Max. A proof past it is refused alone, as "Law X is not proven: its proof ran out of time before it finished.", and the other laws still prove. Each case of a proof gets half of that budget, so a case that runs out of it is refused with what it still has to show, and the cases after it still run. Lean runs under the limits `mago` runs under, and the `lakefile.toml` and manifest an older `mago` wrote in `.sharp/.lean/` are deleted.
- `mago compile` lowers each PHP# file from the analysis that checked it. 0.6.28 analyzed every file a second time to lower it, and sent each extension host's provider requests again. On a 1,536-file PHP# app, a fresh compile now retires 15.0 to 15.5 G instructions instead of 16.5 to 18.0 G and takes 0.90 to 1.00 seconds of user time instead of 1.17 to 1.22, its peak memory 278 to 286 MB instead of 256 to 264 MB, and each compiled file is byte for byte the one the second analysis gave. A compile after a watch's incremental analysis still analyzes each file it lowers again, since that analysis skips the methods it keeps.
- `mago analyze` and `mago compile` do less work for the same output, byte for byte. On the same machine against 0.6.28: analyzing a 1,536-file PHP# app retires 7.8-8.0 G instructions instead of 14.8-15.2 G, compiling it 18.9-19.9 G instead of 27.7-28.4 G, analyzing it with an extension host after an edit 30.8-31.3 G instead of 50.9-51.5 G, and analyzing 14,861 plain PHP files 159.2-159.7 G instead of 171.9-172.5 G. Names are looked up by their interned word, with no interning or lowercasing per lookup and no lock; the codebase's signatures and types are filled on every thread; a compile walks the project once, reads only what it needs from the disk, and writes and stamps its files on every thread; a file's imports merge into place; and a one-shot `mago analyze` exits without freeing its analysis value by value.

### Added

- `mago compile` writes `.sharp/preload.php`, which preloads every type it compiled, spec section 27. A production `php.ini` names it in `opcache.preload`. It requires the project's Composer autoloader, then each accepted `.sharp` file that declares a type, the packages' in `vendor/` included, so each type gets its parents from the autoloader, as in a request. It then loads each type an anonymous class names in a file it loads, as the one `illuminate/reflection`'s `helpers.php` declares over `ReflectsClosures`, so opcache links the class and warns of nothing. A test in an `autoload-dev` folder stays out. Until now a project that preloaded PHP# classes wrote and kept its own list of files.

### Fixed

- Two `mago compile` runs of one tree write the same `.sharp/compiled.bin`, byte for byte, on any number of threads. 0.6.28 kept each compiled file's reads of other declarations and the classes of its namespace in the order a hash table gave them, which changed from run to run. Each list the record keeps is now in order. The record starts with the build ID of the `mago` that wrote it and the hash of the configuration, so only the same build under the same configuration writes the same bytes.
- A `??=` into a `Map` a property holds keeps what its value writes to the same property, whatever accessors the property has. 0.6.28 lost that write when the property had a `set` body: with `counts { get; set => field = value; }`, `this.counts["a"] ??= this.bump()`, where `bump` writes `this.counts["z"] = 1`, gave `{"a":1}`, where the same property without a `set` body gave `{"z":1,"a":1}`. The value now runs before the write takes the property, as it does in every other change, so both give `{"z":1,"a":1}`.
- `mago analyze --watch` gives a class-like it fills again after an edit what `mago analyze` gives it. 0.6.28 filled it again over what its last fill derived and cleared only part of that, so a subclass of a PHP# base class over a plain PHP model whose trait takes type arguments, as Eloquent's `Model` and its `HasCollection` trait, gained another set of the trait's type arguments with each edit of the base class: after one edit, it reached the trait both through `Collection<int, static>` and through a bare `Collection`. The checks of the members a class inherits merge each set they find, so on a subclass the watch had filled again they read other type arguments than `mago analyze` reads. A class-like filled again now starts from its scan, as in a full analysis.

## [0.6.28] - 2026-11-18

### Fixed

- `mago analyze --watch` on a project with Composer's autoload maps keeps watching through deletes, renames and edits. 0.6.27 stopped with "Failed to report results: Files error: file missing" in the round after one that analyzed the project from scratch, as deleting a class's file does, whenever an issue pointed into a vendor file the maps served: that analysis found the files the last one served still held, served none, and forgot them, so the report's tree lacked them. Each analysis from scratch now serves its vendor files as a cold run does. A watch's report leaves out an issue that names a file the tree no longer holds, which the round that checks the file again reports, and a report that fails is logged while the watch goes on.
- With extension hosts, `mago analyze --watch` no longer analyzes the whole project again after an edit of a PHP# file. 0.6.27 started the whole analysis again, with every worker, whenever an edit changed a compiled file, though the workers load only their own rule classes. Each worker now tells mago which project classes it loaded, and which it asked for and never declared. The watch starts again only the workers that loaded a class whose compiled file changed, and checks again the edited files and each file whose hooks ran after the worker loaded that class. An edit of an app class no worker loaded is checked as an edit without hosts is, by the workers already running.
- An extension host starts all its workers when it starts: as many as mago's thread count, unless its `workers` option sets the count. 0.6.27 started 3 of them, and started more only after 512 requests that averaged 1 ms each, so an analysis whose provider requests each take less ran on 3 workers to its end.
- `mago compile` after an edit lowers again only the `.sharp` files whose key the edit changed, and proves again only the laws whose key it changed. A file's key is its source and the declarations, inferred returns, sets of names, library forms and effects its check read, and `.sharp/compiled.bin` keeps them, so the next compile checks them without lowering the file. A law's key is its statement and proof, those of each law it calls, and the text of every declaration they reach, and `.sharp/proofs.json` keeps how each proof ended, so an unproven law is reported again without Lean. A compile writes a compiled file only when its bytes change, and an edit of a plain PHP file a `.sharp` file never reads, as a test, no longer makes that file's compiled file out of date for the engine. In a 1,610-file project with 20 laws, a body edit of one class lowered 1 file instead of 1,610 and proved 1 law instead of 6, and a signature edit of a class 46 files import lowered 52. Each compiled file is byte for byte the one a fresh compile writes. 0.6.27 lowered every file and proved every law of every file an edit reached on each compile. A file the analysis refuses is refused with the analysis's errors, without the second analysis its lowering runs.
- `Parameter.defaultValue()`, `Property.defaultValue()` and `Field.defaultValue()` have the effects of the defaults they can run, spec section 29, so a method held to `uses Database, Environment` reads a parameter's, a property's and a field's default in a program whose defaults are constant, as `null`, `0`, `[]` or an enum case. 0.6.27 refused each read there, as "reaches `Parameter.defaultValue`, which may have every effect", so a reader that fills a missing constructor or action argument from its parameter's default could not run under a `uses`. `Parameter.defaultValue()` takes on the effects of every parameter default the program declares that is not constant, and the other two those of every such initial value: a default of `new Stamp()`, whose constructor has the effect `Clock`, gives each read `Clock`, and a caller without it is refused at the read. A plain PHP parameter's default that is not constant runs code no `uses` names, so while the program declares one `Parameter.defaultValue()` still may have every effect.
- A call that leaves an argument out has the effects of that parameter's default, as if it wrote the default itself, spec sections 7 and 29. 0.6.27 gave the call none of them, so with `touch(Stamp stamp = new Stamp())`, whose `Stamp` constructor has the effect `Clock`, `stamped.touch()` compiled in a method held to `uses Database` and in a law. The call now takes on `Clock` and is refused there, and `stamped.touch(given)` takes on only `given`'s effects. A `new` and a `: super(…)` call take on their constructor's defaults the same way, a call through a class runs the default of the override it reaches, and an implementation's defaults are held to the `uses` of the declaration it implements. A constant default adds nothing.
- An attribute constructor's parameter defaults have no effects, as its body and its initial values have none, spec section 1. 0.6.27 checked only the body and the initial values, so a `[Column]` whose constructor defaults a parameter to `new Stamp()` made reading the attribute have the effect `Clock` and compiled. It is now refused at the default, as "The default of `stamp` in the constructor of `Column` reaches `Stamp.Stamp`, which calls `Chrono.now` with the effect `Clock`, and an attribute has no effects".
- Under opcache, `Json.decode<T>`, `Query.decode<T>` and a struct's or a family's `parse` that throw `InvalidFields` release the class name they look `InvalidFields` up by. Opcache gives that name as a copy of the request's own while its shared memory holds none, as in the request that first loads the class, and 0.6.27 kept the copy, 51 bytes, until the request ended, which a debug engine reported as a leak at exit.
- A branch that leaves when an `is` with a name matches, as `if (audience is Level level) { return level.name; }`, leaves the tested local its other types for the rest of the block, as the same test without a name does, spec section 21. 0.6.27 narrowed only the name, so with `audience: Level | Audience`, an enum and a sealed interface, `audience.name()` after that branch still saw the enum's `name` and was refused as "`audience.name()` calls a method on `Audience` but a function in a property on `Level`". The value now counts as an `Audience` there, after `return`, `throw` or a call that never returns alike, and inside the branch it counts as a `Level` too.

## [0.6.27] - 2026-11-17

### Fixed

- `mago analyze --watch` on a project with Composer's autoload maps reports what `mago analyze` reports and keeps watching. 0.6.26 stopped the watch with exit 2 right after its first analysis, with "Failed to report results: Files error: file missing", whenever an issue pointed into a vendor file the maps served, as the declaration of a private method the project calls: the watch rendered its reports from its own tree, which holds only the files it watches, and the one-shot command from the tree that holds each file the analysis read. The watch now renders from that tree too.
- With an extension that takes a method's return from its body, `mago analyze --watch` checks again only the files an edit reaches, as without one. An edit of such a method's body changes the return its callers read, so it checks again the files that call it, and the files that call a method whose return is taken from a body that calls it, along the whole chain. 0.6.26 analyzed the whole project again after every edit once an extension took any method's return from its body: in a 6-file project, an edit of one such body checked all 6 files again, and now checks the 4 the edit reaches.

## [0.6.26] - 2026-11-17

### Upgrading from 0.6.25

- A vendor function or constant declared in a file outside Composer's `autoload_files.php` is unknown to `mago analyze`, `mago compile` and `mago convert`, as it is to PHP, which never loads that file: a call of it reports `non-existent-function`. 0.6.25 read every file of the vendor folder and knew it. Require the file that declares it, or list it under the package's `autoload.files`.

### Fixed

- `mago analyze`, `mago compile` and `mago convert` read a plain PHP file of the vendor folder only once the project reaches a class it declares, found through Composer's autoload maps as PHP's autoloader finds it, and each file `autoload_files.php` lists. A class reaches the classes its parents, interfaces, traits and types name, and the project's code every class it names. The folder's `.sharp` files and `vendor/composer` load whole, and so does a vendor folder without the maps. A 2-file project with a 16,416-file framework vendor folder reads 1,071 of them, and `mago analyze` takes 1.10 s instead of 5.45 s, and 0.87 s of CPU time instead of 5.84 s. A 1,000-file PHP# project with that vendor folder reads 1,417 of its 16,694 files, and takes 1.83 s instead of 5.95 s, and 1.62 s of CPU time instead of 6.88 s. 0.6.25 read and scanned every file of the folder. A `.sharp` file of the folder reaches every class its `extern` lines, signatures and bodies name, as a project file does, so the standard library's effects on a vendor class load that class. A message names a class by its short name unless a class the run reached shares it, so the issues are the same as 0.6.25's but for a class name only an unreached vendor class shared: 0.6.25 wrote `StaticConstructors.Pool` where a vendor `Pool` existed, and now writes `Pool`.
- `mago compile` reports the issues of each `.sharp` file of the project it lowers, which it reads from each file it loaded, so it no longer resolves the path of each of the project's files on disk first. 0.6.25 resolved them one by one before its analysis, about 1 s of a compile in a project of 19,000 vendor files.
- A PHP# call of a method on a value gives `$this` and `static` as the value's class in its result, type arguments included, spec section 25, since the value's type is what the caller knows of it. So a relation of a plain PHP model that isn't `final` has the type it is declared with: `BelongsTo<Offer, Offer> parent(Offer offer) => offer.belongsTo(typeof(Offer));` and `BelongsToMany<Offer, Offer> offers(Offer offer) => offer.belongsToMany(typeof(Offer));` compile. 0.6.25 kept `static` in the declaring model's type argument and refused each, as "Returned type `BelongsToMany<Offer, Offer, Pivot, string>` is less specific than the declared return type `BelongsToMany<Offer, Offer, Pivot, "pivot">`", a message that also wrote the returned type's default `"pivot"` as `string`. A refused value now writes a literal type argument as it is, and a note names each type argument of a plain PHP generic class that differs, as "The type argument `TDeclaring` is `Offer` here, and `Pivot` in `BelongsToMany<Offer, Pivot, Pivot, "pivot">`.". A receiver whose type writes no type arguments takes each type parameter's default, and its bound only where it has none, as a type that leaves them out already did.
- A `.sharp` file deleted or moved while `mago compile` runs, or while `mago analyze` or `mago lint` compiles for its extension hosts, fails nothing. Gone before the run reads it, it is not part of the run's tree, as if the run started after the delete. Gone after, it is a stale input the next run leaves out. 0.6.25 stopped with exit 2 and "can't resolve the path" on the file when it went after the run read it, and so did `mago analyze --watch` after a batch of changes that moved a file. The watch now reads each changed file once: one it finds gone leaves its tree, so the files that used it are checked again and the watch goes on, and one it can't read for any other reason, as one it may not read, stops the watch naming the file, as it stops a run at its start. 0.6.25 logged that error and went on with the file's old contents.

## [0.6.25] - 2026-11-17

### Fixed

- The check that no two namespaces import each other, spec section 23, finds the project's import cycles once per analysis, so each `import` of a `.sharp` file costs the same however many imports the project has. 0.6.24 walked the namespaces an import leads to and, at each one, read every import of the project again, so a file cost time in proportion to its imports times the project's, and its workers waited on each other to look up the names they read: a 1,500-file application with 8 imports a file took 47 s to analyze, its `Module.sharp` of 100 imports 9 s alone.
- An error of a `.sharp` file that names a class, as "`Order.total` …", counts the classes that share its short name from a count each analysis makes once. 0.6.24 counted the short names of every class of the project, its vendors and the standard library again in each file that reported such an error, a quarter of the CPU time of a 2,449-file analysis.
- A module's check of its bindings, spec section 32, finds the binding that serves each service by an id each analysis gives every binding once. 0.6.24 worked out the id of every binding of a module again for each service it looked up, so a module's check grew with the cube of its bindings: a `Module.sharp` of 400 bindings took 7.2 s.
- The check that a `public` member is used outside its namespace, spec section 5, looks each member's uses up by its class. 0.6.24 read every use of the member's name in the project for each member, so in a project whose classes share member names, as `size` or `handle`, it took nine tenths of the 1.6 s `mago analyze --watch` spent on a one-line edit in a 1,500-file application.
- A `keyof` value, a member name `nameof` gives, keys a `Map` and fills a `Set`, spec section 6.3, as every `string` does: `Map<string, int> widths = [nameof(Order.total): 1];` compiles, and so do a `Map<keyof Order, int>` or `Set<keyof Order>` type and a `groupBy` whose function returns a member name. 0.6.24 refused each one with "A `Map`'s key is anything with `==` and `hash()`: an `int`, a `float`, a `string`, a `bool`, `null`, an enum, a struct, a class value, a `List`, `Set` or `Map` of them, or a class that declares `operator ==`, and `keyof Order` is none of them." A member name also backs a `string` enum case, compares with a string literal, as `column == "total"`, and is refused where a `string` is: by `==` with an `int`, and at a method a `string` lacks, with a `string`'s words. 0.6.24 refused the case with "Expected `string`, but got `keyof Order`", called `column == "total"` never true, let `nameof(Order.total) == 5` through as a redundant comparison, and called a member name a non-object.
- `mago compile` and `mago analyze <paths>` find which files a path names among each file's own folders. 0.6.24 compared each file of the project with every path named, and `mago compile` names each `.sharp` file, so a project of 1,000 `.sharp` files and 16,000 vendor files spent 6.7 s of CPU time on it.
- A local takes a `keyof` type, spec section 11.2, as a parameter does: `keyof Order column = nameof(Order.total);` compiles, with or without `const`, in parentheses as `(keyof Order)? column = null;`, and as a `for` counter's type, and `keyof Order column = "total";` is refused as a `keyof Order` parameter refuses `"total"`. 0.6.24 read `keyof` as an expression and stopped with "Expected `;`, found `Order`", or "Expected `=`, found `Order`" after `const`.

## [0.6.24] - 2026-11-17

### Upgrading from 0.6.23

- A name in `uses` resolves as any class name does, spec section 29: a class the file imports or declares under the name, or one in the file's own namespace, wins over the standard effect of that name, as `App.Math` wins over `Math`. 0.6.23 read a standard effect's name in `uses` as the effect whatever the file imported or declared, so `uses Database` in a namespace with its own `Database` named `Sharp.Data.Database`, and a file that imports Laravel's `Http` facade wrote `uses Http` for the effect. Such a file now imports the effect under another name, as `import Sharp.Net.Http as HttpEffect;`, and writes `uses HttpEffect`.

### Added

- A `Method` gives a value of a function type that calls it, spec section 25: `method.createDelegate<Function<Relation(Model) uses Database>>()` gives a function whose call runs the method, an instance method on the receiver the call passes first, of its class, a class below it or one it extends, `Object` included, and `property.getter().createDelegate<Function<int(Order)>>()` reads a computed property. The method's parameters, result and effects must fit the type, as a function's must to be assigned to it, so a PHP# method's effects are the ones the compile solved for its body, a plain PHP method's are those its `extern` lines declare, as `Function<string(RuntimeException)>` holds `getMessage`, and one with neither may have every effect. One that doesn't fit throws `ValueError` naming the method and the reason, as "`App.Shop.Order.notify` has the effect `App.Shop.Mailer`, and `Function<int(App.Shop.Order) uses Sharp.Data.Database>` allows only `Sharp.Data.Database`.". A call of the value has the effects its type lists, and making it is pure, so a base class held to `uses Database` walks its relation methods by an attribute and calls each, where `invoke` may have every effect and no `uses` accepts it.

### Fixed

- A closure of a PHP# method whose effects no function type can say, as one that calls plain PHP no `extern` declares, is of no function type, spec section 21, whatever its result is. 0.6.23 carried such a method's result type alone, and read a result that is itself a function type as the closure's own type, so a closure of `Function<int()> counter()` was a `Function<int()>`.
- Runs of `mago analyze`, `mago lint` and every other command that starts extension hosts take turns on the folder in Mago's cache they compile the project into for the hosts, as requests take turns on `.sharp/compile.lock`: one run compiles at a time under the folder's `compile.lock`, a run that waited reuses the tree the other compiled, and a run that dies frees the lock. A run's hosts read the tree it started them on until the run ends, so a run after an edit compiles once they are done, and `mago analyze --watch` lets other runs compile while it waits for a change. 0.6.23 let an editor's run and a terminal's run that start together write the folder at once, and one failed with "can't delete the file" on a file the other wrote.
- `mago analyze --watch` starts its extension hosts again whenever the hosts' compiled tree changed since its last analysis, whichever run compiled it, so its hosts never run a class from an older tree. 0.6.23 started them again only when its own compile wrote a file, so after a terminal's run compiled an edit first, the watch's hosts kept the class from before the edit.
- A compile, `mago compile`'s and the extension hosts' alike, has nothing to do when only a setting that changes nothing it writes changed: the threads, the stack size, the version checks, the linter's, the formatter's and the guard's settings, and the editor URL. So runs with their own `--threads`, as an editor's and a terminal's, share one compile. 0.6.23 compiled again whenever any setting differed from the last compile's.
- A class a law reaches compiles when its header names `Iterable<T>`, spec section 12, as `public class Bag : Iterable<string>, Countable`, whether the law is in its own file or reaches it from another one, as through a parameter or a property. The law's translation reads the classes the header names as the checker does, and `Iterable<T>` names none. 0.6.23 refused the class's file with "The lowering of app/Bag.sharp panicked though the checker accepted it, a defect of the checker: resolved name not found at position".
- A `.sharp` file that calls, reads or writes a member the analysis can't find on a class whose base class or interface is missing is refused at that member, with the error a member the class lacks gives, as "Method `Row.key` does not exist.", since PHP# runs only the member its analysis resolved. A plain PHP file still reports nothing there. 0.6.23 accepted the file, and its compile failed with "The lowering of app/Reader.sharp panicked though the checker accepted it, a defect of the checker: internal error: entered unreachable code: the analysis records what each call of a file the checker accepted runs" or "… the checker refuses `App\Row.key`, which names no member".
- A class that plain PHP names before `::` counts as used, spec section 5, whichever class declares the member the code reaches through it. So a PHP# class whose only use is a plain PHP `App::make()` or `App::$count`, where its plain PHP parent declares the static method or property, written in full or through a `use` import, is used, as one that plain PHP creates with `new` already was. 0.6.23 counted the use for the parent alone and refused the class with "Nothing uses `App`, so it never runs."

## [0.6.23] - 2026-11-17

### Upgrading from 0.6.22

- A property never narrows, spec section 21, a stored one, another object's and a static one included, because each read may give another value. A read after `!= null`, `== null` with an early exit, `!`, a `&&` chain, `??=`, an assignment, a `match` arm or the rest of a `?.` chain keeps the property's declared type, as a read after `is` without a name already did. 0.6.22 narrowed the property after each of them, so `if (this.visits != null) { return this.visits; }` compiled where `visits` is an `int?`. It is a compile error at the read, and the error says "`this.visits` is `int?`: a property never narrows, because each read may give another value." and "Bind the tested value, as `this.visits is int visits`.". Bind the tested value, as `if (this.visits is int visits) { return visits; }`, or read the property into a local once and test the local. A local, a parameter and an accessor's `field` narrow as before.
- An `Any?` property read as an operand after `is` gives the operand's own error, "Left operand in binary operation has type `Any?`.", with the binding as its help, where 0.6.22 said "`order.total` is `Any?` here: a property is not narrowed, because it could change between the test and the use."
- `mago convert` writes the binding the rule needs. An `if` or a `while` that tests a property its body reads again, by truthiness, `!== null` or `instanceof`, binds it, as `if ($this->visits) { return $this->visits; }` becomes `if (this.visits is int visits && visits != 0) { return visits; }`. An `if` that always leaves where `=== null` or `!… instanceof` holds binds it with `is not` for the statements after it, as `if ($this->logger === null) { return; } return $this->logger->info($message);` becomes `if (this.logger is not Logger logger) { return; } return logger.info(message);`. The binding takes the property's name, or `visits2` where the method has a `$visits`. Code that calls a method or writes the property before it reads it keeps the read, since a binding would hold a value PHP no longer reads there, and a read that needs the tested type keeps the file plain PHP. `$this->points ?: 0` on an `int?` reads the property once, as `match (this.points) { int value when value != 0 => value, default => 0 }`. 0.6.22 tested and read the property again, `this.visits != null && this.visits != 0`, which relied on the narrowing.
- A `TimeZone` takes a name of the time zone database only when PHP builds it with the database's rules, spec section 24. PHP reads `CET`, `EET`, `MET`, `WET`, `EST`, `HST`, `MST`, `GMT`, `GMT+0`, `GMT-0` and `UCT` as a fixed abbreviation without them, so 0.6.22's `new TimeZone("CET")` kept +01:00 all summer, where the database gives +02:00. `TimeZone.tryOf` gives null for each, `new TimeZone("CET")` throws `ValueError` with "PHP reads "CET" as a fixed abbreviation without them. Name the region instead, such as "Europe/Brussels".", and `TimeZone.names()` lists none of them. Name the region, as `Europe/Brussels` for `CET`, and `UTC` for `GMT` and `UCT`. A stored `"CET"` read back through `Json.decode<TimeZone>` throws that `ValueError` too, so rewrite stored names once to the region's name.

### Added

- A `TimeZone` has `fixedOffset`, spec section 24: the offset of a zone whose clocks never change, read from the zone's rules and never from its name. `"+03:00"`, `"UTC"` and `"Etc/GMT-3"` each have one, as `new TimeZone("Etc/GMT-3").fixedOffset` gives 3 hours, and a zone the time zone database gives any change of offset, as `"Europe/Sofia"` or `"Asia/Kolkata"`, has none. A home zone refuses any zone that never changes its clocks in its constructor, as `if (zone.fixedOffset is Duration) { throw new ValueError(…); }`, where 0.6.22 left a project testing the name's text. `TimeZone` still takes both kinds of name, and a zone still round-trips through `toString`, `DateTime.parse`, `Json.encode` and `Json.decode<TimeZone>`.
- A `.sharp` file reads and writes an XML document through PHP 8.4's DOM, spec section 23 and decision 83: the standard library names `Dom.XMLDocument` beside `Dom.HTMLDocument`, so `import Dom.XMLDocument;` compiles, `XMLDocument.createFromString(xml)` and `XMLDocument.createFromFile(path)` read a document, its `saveXml()` writes it, and an element's `innerHTML` and `outerHTML` give its markup. 0.6.22 refused the import, and had no `outerHTML`.

### Fixed

- A class with an `[Export]` method counts as used, spec section 5, since plain PHP calls the method through the global function it declares, spec section 23, as a class with a listener counts. 0.6.22 refused a class whose only members are `[Export]` methods with "Nothing uses `WordPressOverrides`, so it never runs."

## [0.6.22] - 2026-11-17

### Fixed

- A `match` arm that compares with a string fixed at compile time names the value the `match` tests in every arm, spec section 24, as "A pattern compares `method` with `"GET"`, a string fixed at compile time, so it selects one of a closed set of values.". 0.6.21 printed an empty subject, as "A pattern compares `` with `"GET"`", in every arm whose `match` tests a local, and in each arm after the first whose `match` tests a call, as `this.named()`.
- A struct's or a class's `parse`, `tryParse`, `Json.decode<T>` and `Query.decode<T>` read a field whose type is a union of scalars, as `(string|int|float|bool)?` or `int|string`, spec section 10: the first member, in the order the field writes them, that reads the value takes it, JSON's value as it is and a query's from its text, so `int|string` reads `"3"` from JSON as `"3"` and from a query as `3`. A value no member reads is the field's `InvalidFields` problem, and `Json.encode` writes the value as it is. 0.6.21 refused the call with "`ComponentProperty.parse` can't read `default`: `scalar` is no type `parse` reads."
- An extension reads a bound written `Equatable` as the compile resolves it, `Sharp\Equatable`, in every namespace, spec section 11, so `SourceFile::getResolvedNames()` names it in `App\Mappings` too. A plain PHP `Equatable` of the file's namespace wins over it in the checker, the compile and an extension alike, as `App\Math` wins over `Math`. 0.6.21's resolved names gave the file's own namespace, as `App\Mappings\Equatable`, a class no project declares.
- `mago move` deletes an import line whole when a move turns it into an import of the file's own namespace, whatever the order of the moves, and adds the file's new imports beside the ones it keeps. 0.6.21 deleted the line's text where it also added an import at the line's end, and left `mport App.Parts.Z;`, which doesn't parse.
- `mago convert` writes PHP# an application compiles. It reads whether a package is an application from the nearest `composer.json` at or above the file, as the checker does. A member of a class held at run time is found by its `nameof`, as `method.name == nameof(Order.label)`, where 0.6.21 wrote `method.name == "label"`, which an application refuses. A constant read through such a class stays plain PHP in an application. A property narrowed to `private` takes no `private set`, and a `protected` one keeps `{ get; private set; }`. A returned property keeps its declared type, as `int?`, where 0.6.21 wrote the type PHP had narrowed it to.
- `internal (int|string)? value` parses as an `internal` member of a grouped type, after an attribute too, spec section 5. 0.6.21 read the parentheses as a named reach and refused the line.

## [0.6.21] - 2026-11-16

### Upgrading from 0.6.20

- A comment is text in every file Mago reads, so `// @mago-ignore …` and `// @mago-expect …` hide no issue, in a `.php` file or a `.sharp` file, spec section 27. An issue one hid is reported again, and `unfulfilled-expect` and `unused-pragma` are gone. Turn the rule off for the project in `mago.toml`, as `no-eval = { exclude = ["src/Templating/Compiler.php"] }` under `[linter.rules]` or `{ code = "unknown-class-instantiation", in = ["src/Hydrator.php"] }` in the analyzer's `ignore` list, and delete the comment.
- In an application, `==`, `!=` and a `match` arm that compare a string with a string fixed at compile time, a literal, a constant or a backed case's `.value`, are a compile error, spec section 24, as "`==` compares `order.status` with `"paid"`, a string fixed at compile time, so it selects one of a closed set of values.". Declare the values as a backed enum, read them into it where the data enters, and compare with a case, as `order.status == OrderStatus.Paid`. Replace `name == ""` with `name.isEmpty()` and `name != ""` with `name.isNotEmpty()`. Two strings from data, as `password == confirmation`, still compare, and a library is exempt.
- In an application, `equals` with a string fixed at compile time on either side, as `status.equals("paid")`, and a `List`'s or a `Set`'s `contains`, `indexOf` or `remove` of one, as `statuses.contains("paid")`, or of a string in a literal of them, as `["paid", "due"].contains(order.status)`, are the same compile error as `==`, spec section 24. Compare with a backed enum's case, and search a `List<OrderStatus>` or a `Set<OrderStatus>`. A search for a string from data, as `tags.contains(tag)`, and `add`, as `tags.add("new")`, still compile.
- In an application, `mago convert` writes PHP's `$name === ''` as `name.isEmpty()` and `!== ''` as `isNotEmpty()`, and keeps plain PHP each file that compares a string with a string fixed at compile time, reads a `Map` at one, or tests a string's truthiness, since the `'0'` half of that test is such a comparison. The report names the comparison.
- In an application, a string fixed at compile time used as a `Map` key, read or written, as `row["plan"]`, `row["plan"] = "pro"` or `row.get("plan")`, is a compile error, spec section 24: parse the `Map` into a struct and read its property, as `Settings.parse(row).plan`. A key from data, as `counts[tag]`, still compiles.
- In an application, a `Map` literal passed where a plain PHP parameter declares an array shape is checked key by key, spec section 12, so a key the shape doesn't declare, a key it requires that the literal leaves out and a value of another type are each a compile error that names the key, as "`array{'code': string, 'raw': bool}` has no key `'cod'`.", where 0.6.20 gave one "possibly received" warning for the whole literal.

### Added

- A `string` has `isEmpty()` and `isNotEmpty()`, spec section 18, as a `List`, a `Map` and a `Set` have, so `"0".isNotEmpty()` is `true`.
- `keyof T` is the type of the names of `T`'s readable members, extern properties included, spec section 11.2, and `nameof(Order.total)` has the type `keyof Order`, spec section 6.3. A `keyof Order` is a `string`, so it passes to a `string` parameter and a plain PHP `literal-string` one, joins a template, and compares and keys a `Map` in an application, as `column == nameof(Order.total)` and `row[nameof(Order.total)]`, which a string fixed at compile time can't. A `keyof Order` parameter, as `public Query<T> orderBy(keyof T column)`, takes only a member name, so `query.orderBy("total")` is a compile error. A `keyof T` compiles to PHP's `string`.
- An `extern` line states a plain PHP method's or function's signature in PHP# method order, spec section 23, as `extern string Foundation.route(RouteName name);` and `extern Builder<TModel> Builder<TModel>.where(keyof TModel column, Any? value);`, and a PHP# call is checked against it instead of the library's docblock, so `Foundation.route("checkout")` is a compile error. The signature must fit the library's native types: a stated parameter that doesn't cross to the native one, a stated return type that doesn't take the native one and a native parameter without a default the line leaves out are compile errors at the line. A backed case passed where the native parameter declares only scalars crosses as its value, so `Foundation.route(RouteName.Checkout)` gives plain PHP `"checkout"`. `uses` follows the signature as on any `extern` line.

## [0.6.20] - 2026-11-16

### Upgrading from 0.6.19

- A `match` on a project's enum or sealed interface lists its cases and takes no `default`, spec section 21, so a case added later is a compile error at every `match` that misses it. A `default` there is a compile error that names the cases it stands for, as "A `match` on `TransactionStatus` lists its cases; missing: `TransactionStatus.Failed` and `TransactionStatus.Canceled`.". Replace the `default` with arms for those cases, and join the cases that share an arm with `or`, as in `TransactionStatus.Failed or TransactionStatus.Canceled => "closed"`. A `match` on a plain PHP enum, an installed package's enum, an `int` or a `string` keeps its `default`, and `mago convert` writes the cases a plain PHP `default` over a project's enum stands for.
- A class of an application that nothing uses is a compile error, spec section 5, as "Nothing uses `Ghost`, so it never runs.". A class is used when code outside it creates it, binds it in a module, names it as a type, extends or implements it, or names it in `typeof` or an attribute, plain PHP's code included. Remove the class, or use it where the application runs. A class a framework reaches by reflection carries an attribute whose class is marked `[DynamicallyAccessed]`, on the class or on one of its members. A class a framework finds by scanning folders is refused too: bind it in a module or name it. A library, a module, an enum's case, a class with a listener and a class in a test folder, as a PHPUnit test class, are exempt.
- A call of a `Closure` whose plain PHP source declares no signature has an unknown effect, spec section 21, as a plain PHP call no `extern` declares has, so a law or a `uses` list that reaches one is a compile error, as "Law `handled` reaches `Shows.held`, which calls `handler` with no `extern` declaration.". Call it outside the law, or through a method an `extern` declares.
- `Json.encode` writes a struct or a class by the fields its `parse` reads, spec section 10, so its output changes for data a 0.6.19 program stored. A field is written under its `[Key]` name, as `"return_url"`, where 0.6.19 wrote the property's name, as `"returnUrl"`. A value as text is written as its `toString()`, so a `Decimal` is `"1.50"` where 0.6.19 wrote `{"scale":2}`, and an `Instant` is `"2026-10-22T18:30:00Z"` where 0.6.19 wrote `{"epochSeconds":…}`. A variant of a family carries its name under the tag's key. Read stored JSON once with 0.6.19's shape and write it again, or give each field the `[Key]` its stored key needs. A type whose `parse` reads a main constructor parameter that holds no property can't be written, so `Json.encode` of it is a compile error that names the parameter: declare the parameter with `{ get; }`.
- `Json.decode<T>` of JSON that holds no value of `T` throws `InvalidFields`, spec section 24, as "expected App.Point, got int 3", where 0.6.19 threw a plain `ValueError` for JSON that holds no object. `catch (ValueError e)` still catches it.

### Added

- A family reads and writes a variant by its name, spec section 10. `[Tag("type")]` on a sealed interface, an abstract class or an enum whose cases carry data names the key that holds the variant's name, and `[Key("card")]` on each variant names it, so `PaymentMethod.parse` and `Json.decode<PaymentMethod>` build the variant `{"type":"card",…}` names, and `Json.encode` writes `{"type":"card","last4":"4242"}`. A variant without a name, two variants with one name and a field read by the tag's key are compile errors. `typeof(Self).withTag(name)` gives the variant's class, or null, spec section 25, so a single-table-inheritance base builds the class a row stores.
- `[Key(omitNull: true)]` leaves a field out of `Json.encode`'s output when it is null, and a missing key reads as null, spec section 10.
- `Json.decode<T>` reads any type a field `parse` reads can hold, as `Json.decode<List<Payment>>(body)`, spec section 24. `Query.decode<T>` reads a query string the same way, a number or a bool from its text, so `Query.decode<Search>("q=shoes&page=2")` reads `page` as `2`, and `page=x` is the one problem "page: expected int, got string 'x'".
- A struct's or a class's `parse` reads a value as text, a class with a static `parse(string)` and a `toString()` as `Decimal` and `Instant`, through its own `parse`, spec section 10, and a field of a union of classes as the first member, in the order the field writes them, that reads the value.

- An `extern` property states the type of a property a plain PHP parent's `__get` gives, spec section 14.3, as `public extern Collection<int, Product> products { get; }` on a PHP# Eloquent model. It is get-only, PHP declares no property for it, so `offer.products` reaches `__get`, and the read has the declared type. The value `__get` gives is checked against it where it enters PHP#, so a value of another class throws `TypeError` there. It may share its name with a method, as the relation `products()` does. In a class that inherits no `__get` it is a compile error.
- `mago move <name> <namespace>` moves a PHP# class, as `mago move App.Shop.Order App.Billing`, or every class of a namespace, into the namespace. It moves each file into the namespace's folder, rewrites its `namespace` line, and rewrites every file that names a moved class: PHP# `import` lines, plain PHP `use` lines, fully qualified names and PHPDoc types. Code that reached a moved class through its own namespace gets an import, and an import of the file's own namespace goes. A moved namespace takes every PHP# file of its folder, its `Module.sharp` included, and each module's `imports` line and `module :` that names it is rewritten. A folder the move empties is deleted. Each place a string names a moved class is printed with its line and column, and the string stays as it is. `mago analyze` after the move reports an import cycle the move made.

### Fixed

- A public member's class value of a type parameter names that type parameter, whatever its bound, spec section 5, so `public T? find<T : Object>(Class<T> type)` compiles in an application. 0.6.19 refused it with "`Finder.find` is public, and a public member of an application names its types: `Object` in `Class<T>`."
- Two interface members that declare one generic signature agree, spec section 22, as two interfaces that each declare `MorphOne<TRelated, Model> morphOne<TRelated : Model>(…)` do, so a class that implements both takes a plain PHP `morphOne()` with no return type. The type parameters are compared by their place, as an override's are. 0.6.19 refused the class for members that declare different return types.
- `attributes<T>()` reads the attributes of a class value of any type, spec section 25, as each element of `Class.of(service).interfaces()` is a `Class<Any?>`. 0.6.19 refused it with "A class value reflects, and calls no static. Call `attributes` by its class's name, or through a type parameter."
- A list literal becomes the tuple its place declares, spec section 30, an empty `[:]` or `[]` element included, which becomes the `Map` or the `List` the tuple declares in its place, so `[string base, Map<string, string> breakpoints] split() { return ["a", [:]]; }` compiles. The field, the local or the `Map` value it is written to keeps the tuple's names, so `this.pair = [owner, css];` reads `this.pair.css` after it. 0.6.19 refused the return with "expected `[string base, Map<string, string> breakpoints]`, but found `List<string|Map<string, string>>`", and the read with "Attempting to access a property on a non-object type (`List<(string|Designable)?>`)."
- A getter may call a method that changes an object a field of `this` holds, spec section 6.1, since only a write of its own `this` is refused, so `public lazy int types => this.schemaTypes.types("order");` compiles when `SchemaTypes.types` writes its own `catalog`. A `readonly` value still may not, since readonly reaches everything read through it. 0.6.19 refused the getter with "Getter `types` reaches `SchemaTypes.types`, which changes `this.catalog[name]`."
- `element is Class<FormRegion>` can be true for a `Class<Element>` when `FormRegion` is an interface and `Element` a class a subclass may extend, spec section 21, as `element is FormRegion` can for an `Element`. A final class that does not implement the interface still never matches. 0.6.19 refused the test with "This pattern never matches the value it tests."
- A module's `imports` line lists a namespace an `extern namespace` line gives a library's folder, spec sections 23 and 32, whatever the folder holds, as a class import from it compiles. After `extern namespace WooCommerce.ActionScheduler for ActionScheduler;`, `imports WooCommerce.ActionScheduler;` compiles for a folder of classes only. 0.6.19 accepted it only for a folder that holds a function or a constant, and refused it otherwise with "Imported namespace `WooCommerce.ActionScheduler` does not exist."
- Plain PHP's reflection, `get_class_methods()` and `method_exists()` find no method whose name holds `#`, spec section 25, as no PHP code can declare one: the compile makes them for the engine, as a class's `#fields` and `#json`, a static property's `name#get` and `name#set`, and an indexer's `#get` and `#set`. So PHPUnit doubles a PHP# class, as `$this->createMock(Catalog::class)`. 0.6.19 listed a static property's `name#get` and `name#set` in `ReflectionClass::getMethods()`.
- `Property.getter()` and `setter()` of a static property read its accessors' attributes, spec section 25, as an instance property's do, so `[Searchable] get => …` on a static reads as `getter().attributes<Searchable>()`. The `value` a `set` takes reads its attributes through `setter().parameters()[0]`, a static's or an instance property's. 0.6.19 threw "Property … does not exist" for a computed static and `ValueError` for one with storage, since PHP has no hooks on a static, and "Method … does not exist" for a `set`'s `value`.

## [0.6.19] - 2026-11-16

### Upgrading from 0.6.18

- `Class<T>.properties()` lists properties and no field, spec section 25, and `Class<T>.fields()` lists what an object stores. Code that read fields through `properties()` reads them through `fields()`, whose `Field` has the `name`, `type`, `declaringClass`, `visibility`, `isStatic`, `hasDefaultValue`, `defaultValue()`, `attributes<T>()`, `isInitialized`, `get` and `set` a `Property` has.
- A PHP# attribute class whose constructor, its own or one it inherits, or whose initial values have an effect is a compile error that names the effect, spec section 1, a plain PHP call no `extern` declares included. Move the work into the code that reads the attribute, and give the attribute constants.

### Added

- `Class<T>.fields()` gives a `List<Field>` of every value a class's objects and the class store, spec section 25: each field, at every visibility, statics included, and the storage of each auto-property that is neither `virtual` nor `abstract`, its own first and then those it inherits, in the order they are declared. `Field.get`, `Field.set` and `Field.isInitialized` run no code a project wrote, so they are pure, and a storage layer held to `uses Database` reads and writes a row through them. They reach the storage as a direct use would, an `init` property's storage takes a write only while it holds no value, and on a lazy object that has not loaded they throw `Error` rather than load it. `Field.defaultValue()` still may have every effect.

### Fixed

- `attributes<T>()` of a PHP# attribute class `T` is pure on a `Class<T>`, a `Property`, a `Field`, a `Method`, a `Parameter`, a `Constructor`, a `Constant` and an `EnumCase`, spec section 29, so a method held to `uses Database` reads a PHP# class's attributes. With a plain PHP `T` or `Object` it still may have every effect. 0.6.18 refused `typeof(Entry).attributes<Column>()` there with "`Entry.columns` reaches `ClassMembers.attributes`, which may have every effect, so `uses Database` on `Ledger.columns` cannot accept it."
- An interface member declared `uses Database` accepts an implementation that reads and writes a row's fields through `fields()`, spec section 25. 0.6.18 listed fields in `properties()` alone and refused the read with "`RowStore.toStored` reaches `Property.get`, which may have every effect, so `uses Database` on `Stored.toStored` cannot accept it."

## [0.6.18] - 2026-11-16

### Fixed

- Each error on the left side of a `??` whose right side never returns prints once, spec section 11, so `app.make("migrator") as Migrator ?? throw new RuntimeException("migrator")` reports an error in `app.make("migrator")` one time. 0.6.17 printed each such error twice.
- A plain PHP function whose name differs from a class's only by case is what its namespace calls, spec section 23: beside the class `Lib\Request`, `Lib.request()` and `Lib.Request()` call the function `Lib\request`, and `Lib.request` is its function value. `Lib.Request` without a call still names the class. 0.6.17 refused the call with "Full names appear only in `import` lines: add `import Lib.request;` and write `request`."

## [0.6.17] - 2026-11-16

### Upgrading from 0.6.16

- `heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer` requires `ext-zip`, and the runtime image builds `zip`, since `Sharp.IO.ZipArchive` runs on it. A source build configures `--with-zip` and installs `libzip-dev`, as `sharp/docker/runtime/configure-options` and the README's Linux packages list them. An app's image no longer needs `install-php-extensions zip`.

### Added

- `Sharp.IO.ZipArchive` reads and writes a ZIP archive, spec section 29: `ZipArchive.open(path)` and `ZipArchive.create(path)`, then `list()`, `read(name)`, `write(name, text)`, `addFile(file, name)`, `extractTo(directory)` and `close()`, which writes the changes. Each member has the effect `Files`, and a failure throws `RuntimeException` with the path and libzip's reason, and prints no PHP warning: the standard library's call of a built-in method whose declared return holds its failure runs without PHP's warning, as its call of a built-in function already did. It runs on PHP's `zip` extension.

### Fixed

- An `[Import]` method whose result type holds `null` and no `bool` reads the `false` its built-in returns for a failure as `null`, without PHP's warning, spec section 24, so decision 93's `[Import("file_get_contents")] public static string? read(string path) uses Files;` gives `null` for a missing file. 0.6.16 printed PHP's warning and threw `TypeError: file_get_contents(): Return value must be of type string?, false returned`.
- A module binds a framework's own object from a plain PHP call in its binding's expression, spec section 32: `scoped Request = Foundation.request();` compiles when `Request` is a plain PHP class, and so does an `as` on such a call. The same call outside the binding, a call there that gives another bound class, and a binding of a PHP# class from plain PHP stay `invalid-binding`. 0.6.16 refused the binding with "`Request` is bound in `App`'s module, so it reaches code through a constructor or a parameter."
- `Property.isInitialized(receiver)` and `Property.hasDefaultValue` are pure, spec section 25, so a method held to `uses Database` calls them: `Builtins.sharp` declares pure each PHP reflection member the standard library calls that runs no code a project wrote. `invoke`, `get`, `set`, `setBeforeLoad`, `defaultValue()` and `attributes()` still may have every effect. 0.6.16 refused the call with "`SiteLiveState.variables` reaches `Sharp.Property.isInitialized`, which may have every effect, so `uses Database` on `LiveState.variables` cannot accept it."

## [0.6.16] - 2026-11-16

### Upgrading from 0.6.15

- A `Map` or a `Set` that user code passes to plain PHP, or to an `[Import]` function, arrives as a PHP array whatever its PHP# type, spec section 12, a type parameter, `Any?` and a union included. Code that handed plain PHP the `Sharp\Map` object through one of those passes it to a parameter that takes only objects, such as `object` or `\Countable`, which still takes the `Map` itself.

### Fixed

- `sharp.compile_command` compiles a project once at a time: a request holds the lock of `.sharp/compile.lock` while it compiles, and a request that finds a file stale meanwhile waits, checks its file again, and compiles only when it is still stale. A failed `mago compile` keeps its record with what it printed, so the next compile with every input as it was prints the same errors and exits 1 without compiling, unless the law checker refused a file, and the command runs in a process group of its own that ends with the request. 0.6.15 started one whole compile per request that found a file stale, compiled a refused file again on every request, and left a compile running after its request ended, so 30 requests ran 30 compiles at once.
