- A `Map` passed to plain PHP arrives as a PHP array by what it is when the call runs, at every depth, whatever the parameter's PHP# type, spec section 12: an `Any?`, a type parameter, a union that holds a `Map`, and a `List` or a `Map` of `Map`s, at a call of plain PHP and at an `[Import]` call, so `update_post_meta($id, $key, $value)` with a `Map` held as `Any?` stores an array. 0.6.15 passed it as a `Sharp\Map` object everywhere its type wasn't a `Map` itself.
- The result of a plain PHP call whose declared type is conditional reaches PHP# with the keys of the branch the call's arguments pick, so `array_slice(list, 1, null, true)` into a `List<int>` arrives renumbered from 0, spec section 12. 0.6.15 renumbered only where the branch was a shape, and gave a `List` whose keys started at 1.
- The extension hosts' compile keeps its record whatever the loader and the analysis log from their worker threads, as a symbolic link loop the walk skips, so it compiles only after an input changes. 0.6.15 counted their warnings as the compile's own, kept no record, and compiled again on every run while the warning's cause stayed.
- The README's FrankenPHP deploy restarts FrankenPHP's PHP threads through its admin API, `POST /frankenphp/workers/restart`, with a Caddyfile that keeps the root the `current` link, so the server answers every request through a deploy, as `sharp/bin/hosts` checks with requests sent during one. 0.6.15's `docker restart app` left a moment with no server running.

## [0.6.15] - 2026-11-15

### Upgrading from 0.6.14

- A release publishes `mago` for macOS on Apple silicon and for glibc Linux on arm64 and x86_64, the platforms PHP# runs on, and no longer for Intel macOS (`x86_64-apple-darwin`) or musl Linux (`aarch64-unknown-linux-musl`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`). On one of those, `install.sh`, `mago self-update` and `heyjordanparker/mago-sharp` stop and say that no release has a binary for it: build `mago` from `sharp/mago/` with `cargo install --path .`. A Linux host without `ldd` takes the glibc binary, where 0.6.14 took the musl one.
- `Dns.addresses(host)` gives a `List<IpAddress>`, spec section 29, where 0.6.14 gave a `List<string>`. Compare an address with `IpAddress.parse("127.0.0.1")`, read its `isLoopback()` or `isPrivate()`, or call `toString()` for its text.
- `heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer` requires `ext-bcmath`, and the runtime image builds `bcmath`, since `Decimal` and `BigInteger` run on it. A source build configures `--enable-bcmath`, as `sharp/docker/runtime/configure-options` prints it.
- The PHP# engine is thread-safe only, built with `--enable-zts --enable-embed --disable-zend-signals`, as FrankenPHP runs it. A source build configures with what `sharp/docker/runtime/configure-options` prints, and builds no `php-fpm`.
- The php-fpm runtime image is gone, and `ghcr.io/heyjordanparker/php-sharp:<version>` is the FrankenPHP image, which 0.6.14 published as `php-sharp:<version>-frankenphp`. No `-frankenphp` tag is published any more. An app on the php-fpm image moves to FrankenPHP: `FROM ghcr.io/heyjordanparker/php-sharp:<version>`, its code in `/app/public`, served by FrankenPHP's Caddyfile on ports 80 and 443.

### Added

- A struct takes every visibility a class takes, spec sections 5 and 10, so `internal struct CheckoutTotals` compiles and reaches only its namespace and the namespaces below it. 0.6.14 refused it with "A struct takes only `public`".
- `Decimal`, an exact decimal number, spec section 24: `Decimal.parse`, `Decimal.tryParse`, `Decimal.of(int)`, `+`, `-` and `*` with no rounding, so `0.1 + 0.2 == 0.3`, `<=>` and `==`, which compare the number whatever its `scale`, `divide(divisor, scale, mode)`, `round(scale, mode)` and `toString()`, with the enum `RoundingMode`: `HalfEven`, the default, `HalfUp`, `HalfDown`, `Up`, `Down`, `Ceiling` and `Floor`. There is no `/`, because an exact quotient needs a scale.
- `BigInteger`, an integer of any size, spec section 24: `parse`, `tryParse`, `of(int)`, `+`, `-`, `*`, and `/` and `%`, which round toward 0 as an `int`'s do, `pow(int)`, `<=>`, `==`, `toString(radix)` and `toIntOrNull()`.
- `Sharp.Collections.SortedMap<TKey, TValue>` and `SortedSet<T>`, which keep their keys in order, spec section 12: every member a `Map` or a `Set` has, a loop in key order, and `first()`, `last()`, `floor(key)`, `ceiling(key)` and `range(from, to)`. Each orders by the key's `operator <=>`, or by the `Function<int(T, T)>` its constructor takes.
- `Sharp.Collections.PriorityQueue<TElement, TPriority>`, spec section 12: `enqueue(element, priority)`, `dequeue()`, `peek()`, `count()` and `isEmpty()`, the lowest priority first.
- URL-safe Base64, spec section 24: `Base64.encode(data, urlSafe: true)` writes `-` and `_` and no padding, and `Base64.decode(text, urlSafe: true)` reads it with or without padding.
- An `int`'s 8 bytes, spec section 24: `n.toBytes(order)` and `Int.fromBytes(bytes, order)`, with the enum `ByteOrder`, `Big` by default or `Little`.
- `fields.toCsv()` on a `List<string>` writes one CSV record of RFC 4180, the write side of `text.parseCsv()`, spec section 12.
- `Sharp.Net.IpAddress`, an IPv4 or IPv6 address, spec section 29: `parse`, `tryParse`, `family`, the enum `IpFamily` with `V4` and `V6`, `isLoopback()`, `isPrivate()`, `toString()` and `==`.
- `process.start(program, arguments)` starts a program without waiting, spec section 29: the `ChildProcess` it gives has `writeInput(text)`, `closeInput()`, `readOutput()`, `readError()`, `wait()`, which gives the exit code, and `kill()`. `Environment` gains `processorCount()` and `machineName()`.
- `Sharp.Interop.PhpGlobals`, a standard effect, spec section 29, shares PHP's globals with plain PHP such as a legacy page: `get(name)` and `set(name, value)` for a global, `read(which, key)` and `write(which, key, value)` for a superglobal's key, with the enum `Superglobal`, and `include(path)`, which runs a PHP file in the global scope.
- `type.constants()` on a `Class<T>` gives a `List<Constant>`, the constants the class declares and inherits, spec section 25. A `Constant` has `name`, `type`, the class value of its declared type as `Property.type` holds one, `value()`, `attributes<T>()`, `==` and `hash()`, so `typeof(Limits).constants().first(constant => constant.name == "MAX").value()` reads `Limits.MAX` on a class known only while the code runs.

### Fixed

- `mago convert` keeps PHP's value of `/` of two `int`s, which gives a `float` where the quotient is not whole and PHP#'s truncates: into a `float` place it divides as floats, as `return (float) (total * rate) / 100;`, and into a place of both an `int` and a `float`, as an `int|float` return or a local, it tells the two apart, as `parts == 0 || total % parts == 0 ? total / parts : (float) total / parts`. A place of only an `int` keeps the file plain PHP with "PHP's `/` of two `int`s gives an `int` where the quotient is whole and a `float` where it isn't, and this place takes only one of them (spec section 24)".
- `mago convert` reads and writes a static property of a class held at run time through reflection, `$kind::$count` as `kind.properties().first(property => property.name == "count").get(null)` and `$kind::$count = 0;` as `….set(null, 0)`, spec section 25, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` writes a static method value of a class held at run time, `$kind::find(...)`, as the lambda that calls it through reflection, `(int id) => kind.methods().first(method => method.name == "find").invoke(null, [id]) as Order`, spec section 25, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` reads a constant of a class held at run time through reflection, `$kind::MAX` as `kind.constants().first(constant => constant.name == "MAX").value()` with the cast the constant's declared type gives, as `as int ?? throw new TypeError("Value must be of type int")`, spec section 25, where it kept the file plain PHP. A constant that declares no type, in a class that is not `final`, keeps the file plain PHP, since a subclass may give it a value of another type.
- `mago convert` writes a condition of an object that is never null, which PHP reads as true, as `true`, or as `match (this.other()) { default => true }` where the value is a call it still makes, and `$a ?: $b` of one as `a`, and reads a condition of a `mixed` value, or of a union of scalars, through a `match` on the values PHP reads as false, as `match (value) { null or false or 0 or 0.0 or "" or "0" or [] => false, default => true }`, spec section 21, where it kept the file plain PHP. A union that holds a `Map` still keeps the file plain PHP, since no pattern matches an empty `Map`, and so does a condition of a type parameter that may hold a scalar, which 0.6.14 wrote as `value != null` where it may be null, so `0` read as true.
- `mago convert` imports a class whose name a root class of `Builtins.sharp` takes, as `use App\Errors\OutOfRangeException;`, under its namespace's last part before it, `import App.Errors.OutOfRangeException as ErrorsOutOfRangeException;`, spec section 23, so the file still names the root `OutOfRangeException` a `Map` read throws, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` declares a local assigned the call of a method that declares no return, as `$size = $this->guess()`, as an `Any?`, the PHP# return of such a method, decision 2, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` writes `intdiv($a, $b)` of two `int`s as `a / b`, spec section 24, where it kept the file plain PHP for `intdiv`.
- `mago convert` writes `(bool)` of a `bool` a declaration makes one at runtime, as a parameter, as the value, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` reads a condition of an `int`, a `float` or a string a second read may not give again, as a call's, once, through a `match` on the values PHP reads as false, as `match (this.name()) { "" or "0" => false, default => true }`, spec section 21, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` declares a local first assigned in a block with the type of an operator of declared values, as `int? size = null;` for `$size = $count * 2`, decision 2, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` writes a `return` whose value first assigns a local, as `return ($found = $text) !== null ? $found : '';`, as the assignment on its own line before it, `const found = text;`, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` writes a file's one namespace in braces, `namespace App { … }`, as `namespace App;` with its body out of the braces, spec section 27, where it kept the file plain PHP.
- `x is bool` of a value PHP gives as `false` or another type, as `tmpfile()`'s `false|resource`, can hold on the right of `||` as on its own line, spec section 21, where 0.6.14 warned that the value "can never be `bool`".
- An `internal` class's constructor called from outside its namespace is named as PHP# code writes it, "`new CheckoutTotals` is internal to `App.Shop`", spec section 10, where 0.6.14 named `CheckoutTotals.__construct`.

## [0.6.14] - 2026-11-15

### Upgrading from 0.6.13

- PHP's name lookups, `dns_get_record`, `dns_check_record`, `checkdnsrr`, `dns_get_mx`, `getmxrr`, `gethostbyname`, `gethostbynamel` and `gethostbyaddr`, have the effect `Dns`, spec section 29, where 0.6.13 gave them `Http`. Code that calls one declares `uses Dns` where it declared `uses Http`, or calls `Dns.addresses` instead.

### Added

- `Sharp.Net.Dns`, the effect class `Dns`, spec section 29: `dns.addresses(host)` gives every IPv4 and IPv6 address of a host, once, in the order the system resolver gives them, the hosts file's included. A name that is no host name throws `ValueError`, and a host the resolver can't find throws `RuntimeException`. 0.6.13 reached a host's addresses only through PHP's `dns_get_record`, which asks a DNS server and never reads the hosts file.

## [0.6.13] - 2026-11-15

### Upgrading from 0.6.12

- `Property.type`, `Method.type` and `Parameter.type` hold the declared type whole, `?` included, and their `isNullable` is gone, spec section 25. Compare a nullable type whole, as `p.type == typeof(int?)`, where 0.6.12 compared `p.type == typeof(int)` and read `p.isNullable`. A member typed by a type parameter, read on a class value without type arguments, as `typeof(Crate)`, is that type parameter: `p.type.name()` is `"TItem"`, where 0.6.12 gave its bound. `Method.invoke`, `Property.get` and `Property.set` take an `Object?` receiver.
- `Json.encode` writes `<`, `>`, `&`, `'` and `"` inside a string as `\u003C`, `\u003E`, `\u0026`, `\u0027` and `\u0022`, spec section 22. Pass `escapeHtml: false` for 0.6.12's bytes.
- A class value reflects, and reaches no static, spec section 25. `type.find(id)` on a `Class<Order>`, a static property's read or write and a constant's read through it are compile errors, as "A class value reflects, and calls no static. Call `Order.find` by its class's name, or through a type parameter.". Write `Order.find(id)`, or take the class as a type parameter, `TEntity.find(id)` on a `TEntity : Order`. For a class known only while the code runs, `type.methods().first(method => method.name == "find").invoke(null, [id])` runs the static on it. 0.6.12 called the static on the class the value held, and refused a static named like a member of `Class`, which nothing reaches through a class value now.
- No two namespaces import each other, directly or through other namespaces, a parent and its child among them, spec section 23. 0.6.12 refused a cycle only between two modules' `imports` lists. Each import that closes a cycle is now a compile error, as "`App.Tenant.Store.Entities` and `App.Tenant.Store` import each other (CartService.sharp imports Cart)". Move the code into one namespace, or declare an interface in one namespace that the other implements.
- A `public` member of an application that nothing outside its namespace uses is a compile error, spec section 5, where 0.6.12 warned: "`Cart.recount` is public, and nothing outside `App.Shop` uses it." A library, whose `composer.json` has no `"type": "project"`, is never checked. Declare such a member `internal`, a public member only a view template uses included, since the checker never reads a template. `[DynamicallyAccessed]` replaces `[ReachedByReflection]`: rename the mark on each attribute class, `#[\Sharp\DynamicallyAccessed]` in plain PHP. A marked attribute on a class now counts every public member of the class as used, and the mark on a member is a compile error.
- A property's `set` reaches its own namespace at most, spec section 6.1, so code outside a namespace never changes the state of its classes. A write from another namespace, in a plain PHP file too, is a compile error: "`Transaction.status` is set only inside `App.Shop.Payments`." 0.6.12 let any code write a public `set`. Move each such write into a method of the owning class, and call it. `public set` and `protected set` are compile errors: write `set` or `private set`. An interface property states `{ get; }` or `{ get; init; }`, and each class gives its own `set`. `init` keeps the property's level, and an override of a plain PHP parent's property keeps the parent's `set`.
- A class a module binds reaches code only through injection, spec section 32. A plain PHP call whose result is one, or whose result PHP# code narrows to one with `as` or `is`, is a compile error: "`StoreService` is bound in `App.Shop`'s module, so it reaches code through a constructor or a parameter." 0.6.12 let a service locator, as Laravel's `app(StoreService::class)`, hand one over. Take the class as a constructor or method parameter instead.
- A struct and an enum have no effects, spec sections 10, 20 and 29: each method, `get` body and operator, a data case's included, reaches no `foreign` object, plain PHP with an `extern` effect, or `emit`. Such a member is a compile error: "`Money.inEuros` has the effect `Http`, and a struct has no effects." Move the work into a class that takes the value.
- In a project, a public member's types hold no `Any`, `Any?` or `Object` at any depth, spec section 5: "`Store.checkoutPayload` is public, and a public member of an application names its types: `Any` in `Map<string, Any>`." A type parameter is a named type, and an override, a library's members and `internal` members are exempt. Name the type, as a struct that parses the value, or make the member `internal`.

### Added

- Indexers, spec section 6.6: `public Line this[int index] { get => …; set => …; }` makes `cart[0]` run `get` and `cart[0] = line` run `set`, and `cart[0] ?? fallback` and `cart[0] += 1` go through both. A `get` alone is read-only, an interface declares one with `get;`, and a struct's has no `set`. It runs as PHP's `ArrayAccess`, so plain PHP's `$cart[0]` and `isset` reach it, each checked against its types.
- A member's type reads as declared, spec section 25: `Class<Tone>`, `Function<int(string, Box<int>)>?`, `Box<Tone>|Tone`, `List<Box<Tone>?>`, `[string method, int count]` and a type parameter `TItem` each read whole. 0.6.12 gave `string`, `Closure`, a text that named no type, `Box<Tone>?` without its arguments, a tuple without its names, and `TItem`'s bound. The engine reads each member's declared type from its class's metadata, which `mago compile` writes.
- `Class<T>.properties()` lists static properties too, a computed one and one with accessors included, which `get`, `set`, `getter()` and `setter()` reach through its accessors, and a `Property` has its `setVisibility`, null for a get-only property, `isInitOnly`, `isStatic`, `isVirtual`, `isAbstract`, `isOverride`, `hasDefaultValue` and `defaultValue()`, spec section 25, which hold for an initial value that is not constant too and evaluate it on each read, as `Parameter.defaultValue()` does. A `Method` has `isVirtual`, `isAbstract` and `isOverride`. `get(null)` and `set(null, value)` use a static property, and a static method's `invoke(null, arguments)` runs it on the class value `methods()` read it from. An instance member given null throws `InvalidArgumentException`. A `void` method's `type` is `typeof(void)`, which `typeof` now takes.
- A class value takes any type apart, spec section 25: `kind()`, a `TypeKind`, `isNullable()`, `nonNullable()`, `genericDefinition()`, `typeParameters()`, a type parameter's `bound()`, `unionMembers()`, `intersectionMembers()` for `A & B`, `tupleElements()`, each a `TupleElement` with its `name` and `type`, `parameterTypes()`, `returnType()` and `makeGenericType(arguments)`. It reads every fact its class's declaration states: `baseType()`, `interfaces()`, `isAbstract()`, `isInterface()`, `isStruct()`, `isEnum()`, `enumBackingType()` and `enumCases()`, each an `EnumCase` with its `name`, `value`, `backingValue` and attributes.
- A class value gives its class's `constructor()`, a `Constructor` with its `visibility`, `parameters()` and attributes, and creates an object with `createInstance(arguments)`, and an empty lazy one with `createLazy(initialize)`, on PHP 8.4's `newLazyGhost`, spec section 25. A `Property` reads `isInitialized(receiver)`, gives its accessors through `getter()` and `setter()`, null for a plain stored property, and writes a lazy object without loading it through `setBeforeLoad(receiver, value)`, which the calling code reaches as a direct write would, as `set` does.
- A method declared to return `Self` reads, through reflection on a class value, as that class value's class, spec section 25, an inherited one as the subclass, as `static` binds in PHP.
- `Method`, `Property`, `Parameter`, `Constructor`, `EnumCase` and `TupleElement` have `==` and `hash()` over the member they reflect, spec section 25, so two reads of one method are one `Map` key and `Map<Method, List<Relation>>` compiles.
- A `.sharp` file reads an HTML page through PHP 8.4's HTML DOM, decision 83: the standard library names `Dom.HTMLDocument`, `Dom.Element`, `Dom.Node`, `Dom.NodeList`, `Dom.HTMLCollection`, `Dom.Text`, `Dom.Attr` and the other classes a page reads, so `import Dom.HTMLDocument;` compiles and `HTMLDocument.createFromString(html).querySelector("p")?.textContent` runs. 0.6.12 refused the import, as it still refuses a PHP class the standard library names not, as `DOMDocument`.
- `Class<void>` and `Class<never>` hold `typeof(void)` and `typeof(never)` as any class value, spec section 25. 0.6.12 refused `Class<void>` with "A type argument cannot be `void`".
- `WeakMap<TKey : Object, TValue>` keys by object identity and keeps no key alive, as PHP's `WeakMap` does, spec section 12: `get`, `set`, `containsKey`, `remove` and `count`.
- `Json.encode` takes `escapeHtml`, which defaults to `true`, so JSON put inside a `<script>` can't close it, spec section 22.
- A class value of a built-in generic type answers `is` as a user generic's does, spec section 25: `typeof(List<string>)` is a `Class<List>`, a `Class<List<Any?>>` and a `Class<Iterable<Any?>>`, and a `Map`'s and a `Set`'s class values are a `Class<Map>`, a `Class<Set>` and a `Class<Iterable<Any?>>`. 0.6.12 found each false.

- The standard library has the text, math, time, file and collection members PHP# lacked, spec sections 12, 18 and 24. Text: `ignoreCase` on `compareTo`, `equals`, `indexOf`, `lastIndexOf`, `contains`, `startsWith` and `endsWith`, `titlecase()`, `replace(old, new, limit)`, an `int`'s `toCharacter()`, a `List<int>`'s `toByteString()`, the character classes `isLetter()`, `isDigit()`, `isLetterOrDigit()`, `isWhitespace()`, `isUppercase()` and `isLowercase()`, and `normalized(form)` with the enum `NormalizationForm`. `normalized()` runs on PHP's `intl` extension, so both runtime images now build `intl`, and `heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer` requires `ext-intl`, so Composer refuses to install it on a PHP without it. Math: `Math.log10`, `Math.exp`, the trigonometric functions, `Math.PI`, `Math.E`, a `float`'s `isNaN()`, an `int`'s `checkedAdd`, `checkedSubtract` and `checkedMultiply`, which give null past `Int.MAX` and `Int.MIN`, and `saturatingAdd`, `saturatingSubtract` and `saturatingMultiply`, which stop there, and `Int.parse(text, radix)` and `Int.tryParse(text, radix)`. Time and files: the struct `TimeOfDay`, a `DateTime`'s `timeOfDay`, `Path.join`, `Path.absolute`, and `Files.temporaryDirectory()` and `Files.walk(directory)`, which reads a folder tree lazily. Collections: `firstOrNull` on a `List`, a `Map` and a `Set`, and on a `List` `indexOfFirst`, `minBy`, `maxBy`, `sortedByDescending`, `sortedWith`, `zip`, `windowed`, `partition`, `binarySearch`, `partitionPoint` and `removeAll`, a `Map`'s `getOrPut`, and a `Set`'s `union`, `intersect`, `subtract` and `isSubsetOf`. A `sortedBy` key that is a tuple orders element by element, so `people.sortedBy(p => [p.last, p.first])` sorts by the last name, then the first, each element by its own `operator <=>`, where 0.6.12 refused it with "`List<string>` is none of them".
- An `extern` line takes any attribute, which the plain PHP class, method or function it declares carries as its own, spec sections 1 and 29. `[DynamicallyAccessed] extern AsEventListener;` marks a library's attribute class whose source carries no mark, so a method marked `[AsEventListener]` is never too wide. At run time PHP#'s `typeof(X).attributes<T>()` and `Method.attributes<T>()`, and plain PHP's `ReflectionClass`, `ReflectionMethod` and `ReflectionFunction` `getAttributes()`, give the declaration's own attributes and then the line's, though no code loads the file that holds the line: `mago compile` names that file in `.sharp/listeners.php`, and the engine compiles the line's attributes when reflection first reads the declaration's. 0.6.12 refused an attribute before `extern` as a parse error.

### Changed

- Spec section 29 says an exception a listener throws leaves the `emit` that ran it, and the listeners after it don't run, as with Symfony's dispatcher. The standard library's dispatcher already ran them so.
- `willSet` and `didSet` take attributes, which go on the `set` hook they run inside, after the `set`'s own, so reflection reads them there, spec section 1. `method:` is their one target. 0.6.12 refused one with "`willSet` runs inside the `set` accessor, so it has no place of its own for an attribute".
- An override may widen the access level of the property it replaces, `protected` to `public`, as PHP lets it, spec section 6.1, and its `set` may be the parent's level or wider. 0.6.12 refused it with "The override `Order.with` is `public`, but `Model.with` is `protected`".
- A `return` in `willSet` ends `willSet`, and the write still runs, spec section 6.2, from a loop or after `??` too. 0.6.12 refused it with "`willSet` cannot return: the write runs after it". Writing `value` in `willSet`, a `return` with a value in it, and a `return` in a `set` body before `didSet` stay compile errors, and so does a `return` that leaves a `finally` block in `willSet`, "`return` in `willSet` cannot leave a `finally` block.", as `break` can't.
- A static auto-property takes `via`, spec section 6.4. Its class creates its behaviors on the property's first read or write and keeps them for the app's lifetime, one set for each type argument of a generic class. Its initial value fills its storage without the behaviors' `set`, as a static's initial value runs no `set` body. 0.6.12 refused it with "A static property takes no `via`: each object keeps its own behaviors".
- An override of a method's `...` parameter may write it as the `List` it arrives as, `List<string> parts` for `string ...$parts`, spec section 7. A call still passes it any number of arguments, and the compiled PHP keeps the `...`. 0.6.12 refused it with "`Listed.join` must declare parameter `parts` variadic like `Joiner.join`".
- `new` takes its type arguments as a generic method call does, from its arguments or from the declared type its object goes into, spec sections 11 and 25: `new Box(5)` is a `Box<int>`, `Shelf<string> shelf = new Shelf()` compiles, and so does `new (pages)()` of a `Class<PaginatedList>` whose object goes into a `PaginatedList<Order>`. Written type arguments still compile. A `new` that nothing fixes a type argument of is refused with "Nothing fixes the type argument `T` of `new Shelf`.". 0.6.12 refused every `new` of a generic class without them with "`Box` needs its type argument.".
- `Object` joins other bounds with `&`, spec section 11: `<TValue : Object & Shareable>` compiles. 0.6.12 refused it with "`Object` is a bound alone".
- A generic PHP# class may be PHP's `Serializable` through a plain PHP library's interface, spec section 11. `serialize` keeps the object's type arguments ahead of the text its `serialize` writes, and `unserialize` gives that text alone to its `unserialize`, so the copy keeps its type arguments. 0.6.12 refused the class with "`Page` has type parameters, so it cannot be a `Serializable`".
- The lazy operations of `Iterable<T>` apply to every implementation, spec section 12: a plain PHP `Traversable`, such as Laravel's `LazyCollection`, takes `filter`, `map`, `take` and `toList` wherever it declares no member of the name, and its own member wins. 0.6.12 refused `cursor.toList()` on a value typed as the plain PHP class with "Method `toList` does not exist".
- A struct and an interface may name `Iterable<T>` in their header, spec section 12. A struct declares `elements()` as a class does, and each class that implements such an interface declares it, and gets the `getIterator()` plain PHP reads through the interface's trait. 0.6.12 refused both with "Only a class names `Iterable<T>` in its header".
- A `readonly`, a `?`, a `?.`, a `??`, a null check or a method's `uses none` that can't matter warns, and the code compiles, spec sections 13, 14.4 and 29. 0.6.12 refused each, as "Redundant null coalesce" and "A method without `uses` is pure already, so delete `uses none`.".
- A nullable return type on a method that never returns null compiles with no warning, spec section 14.4. 0.6.12 refused it with "Declared return type `Customer?` for `current` has unused branches".
- A read of a property `__get` serves with no type compiles with no warning, spec section 14.3. 0.6.12 warned "The type of `row.owner` is unknown, so the read is `Any?`.".
- A template's `${…}` takes a union of `int`, `float`, `string` and `bool`, as `int|string`, spec section 18, and each value prints as its own type does. 0.6.12 refused it with "`int|string` is none of them".
- `replace` with empty text to replace and `split` with an empty delimiter match before each character and after the last, spec section 18, so `"ab".replace("", "-")` is `"-a-b-"` and `"ab".split("")` is `["", "a", "b", ""]`. 0.6.12 threw `ValueError` for both. A negative limit and a negative count still throw.
- A `match` statement takes expression arms beside block arms, spec section 21.
- In a pattern, `not` binds tighter than `or`, spec section 21, so `result is not Paid or Refunded` compiles and means `(not Paid) or Refunded`. 0.6.12 refused it with "Write `not (Paid or Refunded)`, or `(not Paid) or Refunded`.".
- `x is int?` matches an `int` or null, spec section 21. 0.6.12 refused it with "A type pattern is never nullable".
- An import or a class of the file's own namespace named `Error` shadows the standard library's `Error`, spec sections 23 and 31, as it shadows every default name. 0.6.12 refused an import whose last part is `Error`. An import of PHP's engine class `Error` is still refused.
- `List`, `Map` and `Set` share a union, spec section 24, as every other type does, and `is` tells them apart. 0.6.12 refused it with "A type can't be both a `List<int>` and a `Map<string, int>`.".
- A fixed offset from `-18:00` to `+18:00`, written as `+03:00`, names a `TimeZone`, spec section 24. 0.6.12 threw `ValueError` for it.
- Any code extends `Class<T>`, spec section 25, and `Class`'s own members still win. 0.6.12 refused it outside the standard library.
- Any code takes an extension's receiver by `ref`, spec section 26, so an extension changes the collection it is called on. 0.6.12 refused it outside the standard library.
- Spec section 26 says an extension reaches what code in its own namespace reaches, an `internal` member included, as the checker already decided. 0.6.12's spec said only public members.
- A type parameter named without the `T` prefix, an interface named with an `I` prefix or an `Interface` suffix, and a namespace part out of Pascal case are naming warnings, spec sections 11, 19 and 23, as a name out of its case is. 0.6.12 refused a type parameter without the prefix and a lowercase namespace part, and the spec named the interface rule without a check.
- A stored property never narrows, spec section 21, because each read may give another value, so code binds its tested value with `is T name`, as in `if (order.total is int total)`. 0.6.12 narrowed a stored property until the next call or assignment.
- Spec section 23 says a constant a library declares outside any namespace is read through the namespace an `extern namespace` line gives its folder, as a function is. The checker already decided it so.
- A parameter's default reads one field of the caller's position, as `int line = Position.current().line`, spec section 27. 0.6.12 refused it with "Take the whole position". A default computed from the position, as `Position.current().line + 1`, is still refused.
- `for (x in y)` is a plain parse error at the first token PHP#'s grammar can't take, spec section 17, as every form outside PHP# is. 0.6.12 recognized it with "A loop over a collection takes `of`".
- A class value keys a `Map`, joins a `Set` and satisfies `Equatable`, spec sections 11 and 12, through the `==` and the hash of its whole type, so `typeof(Box<int>)` and `typeof(Box<string>)` are two keys. 0.6.12 refused it with "`Class<Order>` is none of them", and a `Map` or `Set` of the engine compared two class values by their text.
- `never` is a whole type, spec section 7: a type argument, as in `Result<int, never>`, and the type of a parameter, a property or a local, none of which then holds a value. PHP types only a result `never`, so plain PHP sees a parameter or a property of it as PHP's `null`. 0.6.12 refused it everywhere but a whole return type with "PHP# has no `never` type outside a return type", and refused a `Result.Ok(1)` returned as `Result<int, never>` with "The type argument `TError` of the call to `Result.Ok` would be `never`, which PHP# can't write". `Never` is refused as `Void` is, with "PHP# writes the type `never`."

### Fixed

- `[]` fits wherever an `Iterable<T>` is declared, as `Iterable<Object> owners() => [];` and `Iterable<string> names = []`, spec section 12, since `[]` is an empty `List` and a `List` is an `Iterable`. 0.6.12 refused it with "expected `Iterable<Object>`, but found `Map<never, never>`".
- An empty plain PHP array passes both `as Map<K, V>` and `as List<T>`, and `is` agrees, spec section 12, since it holds no element that contradicts either, so a request's empty query array read `as Map<string, Any?>` is an empty `Map`. 0.6.12 gave null for the `Map`.
- `typeof(List).name()`, `typeof(Map).name()` and `typeof(Set).name()` are `"List"`, `"Map"` and `"Set"`, spec section 25, as `typeof(List<int>).name()` is `"List<int>"`. 0.6.12 gave `"Sharp.List"`, the engine's class.
- `Json.parse("{}").value` is an empty `Map` and `Json.parse("[]").value` an empty `List`, spec section 22, the reverse of what `Json.encode` writes. 0.6.12 read `{}` as an empty `List`.
- `mago convert` writes a method's own PHPDoc type parameters after its name, each with its bound, as `TEntity save<TEntity : Entity>(TEntity entity)` for `@template TEntity of Entity`, spec section 11, and checks a value the analysis types wider than one where the method returns it, as `as TEntity ?? throw new TypeError(…)`, as it checks a type parameter of the class. 0.6.12 left them out, so the converted method named a type no declaration gives, the checker refused it with "Cannot find type `App.TEntity`", and the file stayed plain PHP.
- Each reason `mago convert` gives for keeping a file plain PHP names its cause, what PHP# can't write and why, and no longer says what to write. "`mixed` reaches a method call, which PHP# allows only from a known type, and no declaration gives this value one" ended in "narrow it with `is`, or declare its type", and a local whose type no declaration gives ended in "declare its type with a `@var` tag". 0.6.12 ended 30 reasons with such advice.
- `mago convert` keeps a class plain PHP when code of another namespace, plain PHP or PHP#, assigns one of its properties, with "`Transaction::$status` is assigned from `App\Shop\Checkout`, and PHP# sets a property only inside its own namespace", since a PHP# property's `set` reaches its own namespace at most. A property no code outside its class sets is `{ get; private set; }`, and one other code of its namespace sets keeps `{ get; set; }`; a `virtual` one, an `override` and an interface's or abstract one keep `{ get; set; }` too, since a `private set` closes a property to a subclass's replacement. 0.6.12 wrote `{ get; set; }` for each and converted the class whoever assigned it.
- `mago convert` keeps a class plain PHP when it imports a namespace that imports the class's own namespace back, a parent and its child included, with "`App\Tenant\Store\Entities` and `App\Tenant\Store` import each other (CartService.php imports Cart)", since PHP# lets no two namespaces import each other. A class imports each namespace whose names its code reaches, a plain PHP class's as a PHP# one's. 0.6.12 converted such a class.
- `mago convert` keeps a file plain PHP where a call of plain PHP returns an object of a class a module binds, as a container's `app(StoreService::class)` does, with "`StoreService` is bound in `App\Shop`'s module, so PHP# takes it through a constructor or a parameter", spec section 32. 0.6.12 converted the call.
- `mago convert` keeps an enum plain PHP where one of its methods calls plain PHP whose `extern` declares an effect, or a method of a `foreign` class, with "`Translator::get` has the effect `Sharp\Net\Http`, and a PHP# enum has no effects", spec section 29, as the checker refuses the enum. A plain PHP call no `extern` covers has no effect the checker names, so an enum that makes one converts. 0.6.12 converted an enum whatever it called.
- In a project, whose `composer.json` says `"type": "project"`, `mago convert` keeps a file plain PHP where a public member's signature would name `Any`, `Any?` or `Object` at any depth, from a PHP `mixed`, an `array` no PHPDoc types, a missing type, an `object` or a `class-string` of no class, which is `Class<Object>`, with "`Store::checkoutPayload` returns `mixed`, and a public member of an application names its types". A narrower PHPDoc type still converts, as does a library's member and a private or protected one. 0.6.12 converted such a member.
- `mago convert` checks a value wider than a nullable or a union type that PHP declares for a parameter or a return, as `?string` or `string|Loggable`, with one read through `match`, as `match (this.raw()) { string value => value, null => null, default => throw new TypeError("Return value must be of type ?string") }`, spec section 21, so the value's call runs once and the converted file throws PHP's `TypeError` where PHP does. A type only PHPDoc gives, which PHP never checks, still keeps the file plain PHP. 0.6.12 refused each such value with "`mixed` reaches a parameter of a known type" or "is wider than".
- `virtual`, `override` and the other member modifiers go before a parenthesized type, as `public override (int|string)? getKey()`, whose `?` makes the whole union nullable, spec section 24. 0.6.12 read the modifier as a call and refused the member with "Unexpected `)`".
- A method written `override` keeps the name of the plain PHP method it replaces, as `public override List<string> __sleep()` does, since the name is the library's. A method of the class's own still fails at a name that starts with `__`. 0.6.12 refused the override with "Unexpected `__sleep`".
- A `Self` override of a plain PHP method that returns `$this` fits it, since PHP# writes PHP's `$this` as `Self`, spec section 25, as it already fit one that returns `static`. 0.6.12 refused it with "Return type `Order` of `Order.touched` is incompatible with parent return type `Order` of `Model.touched`", naming one type twice.
- `mago convert` converts more of PHP, where 0.6.12 kept the file plain PHP with "is not converted yet" or a reason the converter gave for its own limit:
  - `$value ?: $other` of a call or an index, with one read, as `match (this.find()) { string value when value != "" && value != "0" => value, default => 'untitled' }`, and as `this.parent() ?? this` for an object that may be null, spec section 21. A `?:` of a value that is never null and of a scalar type still refuses, since its `match` arm tests a type the value already has, which the checker warns on.
  - `never` as a whole type, as a return and as a type argument, as `List<never>`, spec section 7.
  - a value wider than a `float` PHP declares, as `match (v) { float value => value, int value => (float) value, default => throw new TypeError(…) }`, since PHP passes an `int` there as that number.
  - a public method of a project that declares `mixed`, or no return type, and that no subclass replaces, as the join of the values its body returns, as `public string label()`, and a closed method whose `?` return never gives `null` without the `?`. An open method still refuses, since a subclass may replace it with one whose PHP return is the wider one, which PHP refuses to link.
  - a body between `:` and `endif`, `endforeach`, `endfor`, `endwhile` or `endswitch`, in braces.
  - `str_replace` of a literal text to replace other than `""`, as `title.replace(' ', '-')`. The bridge folds a standard library body's comparison of a parameter with a literal the call passes, so `replace(' ', '-')` inlines PHP's `str_replace` where `old != ""` holds. `str_replace` of a text that may be empty still refuses, since `replace` puts the new text before each character for an empty one, where PHP leaves the text as it is.
  - a `match` on an `int` with no `default` arm, which gains `default => throw new UnhandledMatchError(`Unhandled match case ${code}`)`, PHP's own message, and a `match` arm on a value that is no constant, as a chain of `==` tests, `code == limit ? 'at the limit' : 'under'`, spec section 21.
  - a `case` that joins `default`, which `default` covers, an interface's constant, a plain PHP trait an enum uses, as a header trait, `class-string<T>` as `Class<T>`, and a `for` with several initializations that assign locals declared before it.
  - `break 2` and `continue 2`, as the name of the loop they leave, `outer: for (…)` and `break outer;`, spec section 17, and `break 1` as `break`.
  - a method with `yield`, which returns an `Iterable` of what it yields, and `yield from items` as `yield ...items`, spec section 12.
  - an integer literal past `PHP_INT_MAX`, as the `float` PHP reads it as.
  - `__FUNCTION__` and `__METHOD__` outside a method, as `""`, and in a property's accessor, as `"$label::get"`.
  - a `static` local, as a private static field before its method, `private static int count = 0;`, which the method reads and writes as `Ticker.count`, spec section 6. Its type joins the values written to it, and its initial value is one computed from constants. One in a lambda, a generic class or an enum still refuses.
  - a `for` whose initialization declares a local beside others, as a statement of its own before the loop for each initialization, in its order, and `for (; index < last; index++)`, spec section 17.
  - a `switch` with a `case` on a value that is no constant, as an `if … else if` chain of PHP's loose `==` tests, `if (code == limit) { … } else { … }`, where reading the value again runs nothing, spec section 21.
  - a promoted parameter with hooks, as a parameter property whose accessors are its hooks, `public string label { get; private set => field = value + '!'; } = 'guest'`, spec section 9.
  - a built-in function as a value, as the lambda that calls the standard library member that runs it, `(string value) => value.trim()` for `mb_trim(...)`, with as many parameters as the function type it lands in passes.
- `mago convert` writes a static reached through a class value as PHP# reaches one, spec sections 11 and 25: through `class-string<T>` of a type parameter as `T.find(7)`, `T.find`, `T.count` and `T.MAX`, and a static call through a class held at run time as the call through reflection, `kind.methods().first(method => method.name == "label").invoke(null, [7])`, whose `Any?` result `as` checks into the call's type. 0.6.12 wrote `kind.find(7)`, which the checker now refuses, and typed the call's arguments against a method of the converted class of that name. A static property, a constant or a method value through a class held at run time keeps the file plain PHP.
- In a project, whose `composer.json` says `"type": "project"`, `mago convert` writes each member PHP makes public with the narrowest access level its uses need, by the rule `too-wide-member` reads them with, spec section 5: `private` where only its class uses it, `protected` where a subclass does too, `internal` where only its namespace does, or where nothing the analysis reads uses it, and `public` otherwise. A member that replaces or implements another, a constructor, and a member an attribute marked `[DynamicallyAccessed]` says a framework reaches keep `public`, and a library's members stay as PHP declares them. 0.6.12 wrote every such member `public`, which the checker now refuses as too wide, so the file stayed plain PHP.
- `mago convert` writes PHP's `protected(set)` and `public(set)` as a `set` that reaches the namespace, or `private set` where the class alone sets the property, spec section 6.1, and keeps an interface plain PHP where one of its properties has a `set` hook, with "an interface property with a `set` hook has no PHP# form: an interface property states `get` or `init` (spec section 22)". One with a `get` hook alone converts as `string title { get; }`. 0.6.12 wrote `protected set` and an interface's `{ get; set; }`, which the checker now refuses.
- `mago convert`'s reason for a built-in call that a standard library member fits but for the literal its body needs names that member alone, as "`str_replace` has no PHP# form here: `StringMembers.replace` runs it only where argument 1 is a literal other than `""`". 0.6.12 also named each member that passes the function other values.

## [0.6.12] - 2026-11-14

### Upgrading from 0.6.11

#### Use the value `as` gives, never the local it converts

`as` gives a converted value and leaves the local it converts as it was, spec section 21, since only `is` narrows. 0.6.11 also read the local as the type parameter `as` named, as `event` after `const due = event as TDue;`. Code that used `event` as a `TDue` after that line is now refused, with the error a value of `event`'s own type gets. Use `due`, or test with `if (event is TDue due)`.

### Added

- `mago convert` converts an argument or a returned value the analysis types wider than the declared type it lands in, as Laravel's `trans()`, typed `array|string`, passed to a `string` parameter, or a `mixed` value returned as an `int`, where it kept the file plain PHP. It writes `title as string ?? throw new TypeError("Argument #1 ($title) must be of type string")`, which throws where PHP's own check of the parameter or the return throws a `TypeError`. It writes it for a declared type `as` tests as PHP checks it: one `int`, `string`, `bool`, object, enum or class with no type parameters, and a nullable one only for a value that is never null.
- `mago convert` converts a file with a `use` line of a class of PHP's root namespace, as `use Closure;` beside a `Closure(int): int` parameter, which becomes `Function<int(int)> next`, or `use RuntimeException as Failure;`, where it refused the line. The line goes, and the file names each such class where it uses it: bare for a root class of `Builtins.sharp`, through its folder's namespace for a library's, and a refusal there for any other, as `new DateTimeImmutable()`.
- `mago convert` converts a read of a `Map` whose values are never null at a key it may lack, as `$sizes['small']` of an `array<string, int>`, where it kept the file plain PHP. It writes `(sizes['small'] ?? throw new OutOfRangeException("Undefined array key \"small\""))`, which throws where PHP warns on the missing key and gives null, and gives the value PHP gives anywhere else. A read of a `Map` whose values may be null still keeps the file plain PHP, since a stored null would throw too.
- `mago convert` writes a generic class PHP names without type arguments with its bounds where each type parameter is covariant, as `Relation<Model, Model>` for a `Relation` declared `@template-covariant TRelated of Model`, since its bounds hold every object of the class. Where a type parameter is not covariant it still keeps the file plain PHP, now with "`App\Box` is written without type arguments, which PHP takes as any `Box`, and a PHP# type names them, which hold every `Box` only where each type parameter is covariant", where it said to give them in PHPDoc.
- `mago convert` converts a condition of a `bool` that only PHPDoc types, as a framework model's `@property bool $active` in `if ($row->active)`, `!$active`, `&&`, `||` and `?:`, where it kept the file plain PHP. A `bool` condition compiles to a test of the value itself, which reads PHP's truthiness of whatever the value holds, as `1` or `"abc"`. A condition of an `int`, a `float` or a string only PHPDoc types, `(bool)` of one, and a `match (true)` arm or `xor` of such a `bool` still keep the file plain PHP.
- `mago convert` checks a value the analysis types wider than a type parameter of the converted class, returned or passed where the class's PHPDoc says `T`, as `this.found as T ?? throw new TypeError("Return value must be of type T")`, where `T`'s bound excludes null, as `@template T of object`. It kept the file plain PHP with "`object` is wider than `('T.app\holder extends object)`", or let a `mixed` returned as `T` through to a proof that refused it. Where `T`'s bound holds null, as an unbounded `@template T`, it keeps the file plain PHP with "`mixed` is wider than `T`, a type parameter whose bound holds null, which has no PHP# test", since `as T` gives null for a null `T` too.

### Fixed

- `attributes<Object>()` gives every attribute of a class, a method, a property or a parameter, spec section 25, as any other bound gives the attributes of its class. 0.6.11 threw `Error` with "Class "Object" not found", since PHP's `getAttributes` takes a class to filter by, and `Object` is the root bound and no class.
- The class value of a type that is no class, as `typeof(Object)`, `typeof(int)` or `typeof(List<int>)`, has no `properties()`, no `methods()` and no `attributes<T>()`, spec section 25. 0.6.11 threw `Error` with "Class "Object" not found" from the first two and `ReflectionException` from the third.
- `Class.of(value)` of a value of a type parameter with no bound, as `TValue`, is refused with "`TValue` does not satisfy the bound `Object` of type parameter `T`.", spec section 11, as `Any` and `Object?` are, so the error names the type parameter. 0.6.11 said "Invalid argument type for argument #1 of `Class.of`: expected `T`, but found `TValue`.", which named no bound.
- A local that `as` converts to a type parameter keeps its own type, spec section 21, since only `is` narrows. After `const due = event as TDue;`, `event` is still an `Event`, where 0.6.11 read it as `TDue|Event`, as if the `as` had tested it. `as` to a class already left the local's type as it was.
- `mago analyze --watch` analyzes only the edited file after an edit of a library's polyfill of a built-in function, class or constant, as `if (!function_exists('str_starts_with')) { function str_starts_with(…) }` declares, that changes the polyfill's signature. The codebase keeps the built-in, so the edit changes no caller's result. 0.6.11 analyzed every caller of `str_starts_with` again.
- `mago convert` writes `Externs.sharp` in the first PSR-4 folder `composer.json` lists, and a folder of root functions with no namespaced class, in a `composer.json` that names no package, takes the first PSR-4 namespace it lists. 0.6.11 took the first in alphabetical order, so `"Zoo\\": "zoo/", "App\\": "app/"` put the file in `app/` and gave the functions `App`.
- `mago convert` ends the body of a method PHP declares no return type for with `return null;` where PHP runs on past its last statement, as after a `try` whose block can finish, since PHP gives `null` there and a PHP# method returns on every path. 0.6.11 left the body as it was, which `mago compile` refused with "Missing return statement", so the file stayed plain PHP.
- `mago convert` proves a file again when its own issue may come from another file the same proof keeps plain PHP, as a caller's lambda that a converted class's stored function can't hold. 0.6.11 kept both plain PHP, though the caller converts once the class stays plain.

## [0.6.11] - 2026-11-14

### Upgrading from 0.6.10

#### Read a reflection boolean by its `is` name

The booleans `Class<T>.methods()` and `properties()` give are named as the standard library names every boolean, as `List.isEmpty()` is, spec section 25. `Method.nullable`, `Property.nullable` and `Parameter.nullable` are `isNullable`, and `Parameter.optional` is `isOptional`. `mago` refuses the old names with "Property `Parameter.optional` does not exist.". Write `isNullable` and `isOptional`.

#### Mark the attribute classes a framework reads by reflection

A public member with an attribute no longer escapes spec section 5's warning that it is too wide just for having one. Only an attribute whose class is marked `[ReachedByReflection]` turns the warning off, since the mark says reflection reaches the member. A member marked `[Deprecated]`, `[Import]` or `[Key]` that nothing outside its namespace uses now warns "`Hooks.voided` is public, and nothing outside `App.Payments` uses it.", where 0.6.10 said nothing. An `[Export]` method still never warns, since plain PHP calls it through its global function. Mark each attribute class whose framework reaches the member: `[ReachedByReflection]` on a PHP# class, and `#[\Sharp\ReachedByReflection]` on a plain PHP one.

#### Give each library folder of root functions a namespace with `extern namespace`

A function or constant a plain PHP library declares in PHP's root namespace no longer takes the namespace of the classes in its folder, decision 97. `extern namespace` is now the one way to give a library's root names a namespace: it names a class, function or constant of the folder, and gives that folder's root classes, functions and constants the namespace. Without a line, `Foundation.abort(404)` is refused with "Function `Foundation.abort` does not exist.", where 0.6.10 called Laravel's `abort()`. Add one line for each folder the code reaches, in a `.sharp` file of `extern` declarations, with the namespace written in full or through its import:

```csharp
// 0.6.10: no declaration
import Illuminate.Foundation;

Foundation.abort(404);
```

```csharp
// 0.6.11: app/Stubs/Laravel.sharp
namespace App.Stubs;

extern namespace Illuminate.Foundation for abort;   // abort(), now() and every root name of helpers.php's folder
```

A line may give a namespace a class has only to a folder that holds such a class, as `helpers.php` sits beside `Illuminate\Foundation`'s classes. A line also gives its namespace only to the root classes of its own folder, so `WordPress.WP_Screen` of another WordPress folder names no class.

#### Name a query's allowed columns with a string-backed enum

`Sql.identifier` and the `Sharp.Data.Sql` class are deleted, decision 98, since a string-backed enum's `value` is a `literal-string` wherever the case comes from, and the enum holds the allow-list as a type. `import Sharp.Data.Sql;` is refused with "Imported class, interface, trait, or enum `Sharp.Data.Sql` does not exist.". Declare the allowed names as an enum, and pass its case's `value`:

```csharp
// 0.6.10
import Sharp.Data.Sql;

query.orderByRaw(Sql.identifier(column, ["name", "created_at"]));
```

```csharp
// 0.6.11
public enum SortColumn : string
{
    case Name = "name";
    case CreatedAt = "created_at";
}

query.orderByRaw(column.value);   // column is a SortColumn
```

#### Move the engine, mago and the standard library to 0.6.11 together, then compile again

The 0.6.11 engine refuses every `.sharpc` file an older mago wrote, because `SHARP_UNIT_ABI` changed: each `emit` now passes the engine the dispatcher it hands the event to, the interface the standard library marks, where 0.6.10 passed the event alone.

```text
Shop.sharp was compiled for a different PHP# engine. Install the mago-sharp release that matches this engine.
```

With `sharp.compile_command` set, the engine runs it and reads the files it writes. Otherwise, compile again after upgrading.

mago now refuses to compile against a standard library of another release, since it reads the library it ships with, as the `Dispatcher` the library marks. So does the compile for the extension hosts that `mago analyze`, `lint` and `convert` run, which 0.6.10 ran against no library when it couldn't read the installed one. mago 0.6.11 says so where 0.6.10 compiled:

```text
This project's PHP# standard library is 0.6.10, and mago is 0.6.11. Move mago and the standard library to the same release: require heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer 0.6.11, or heyjordanparker/mago-sharp 0.6.10.
```

A Composer dev version of the library, as `dev-master`, names no release, so it compiles. An `emit` in a project without the standard library, which has no dispatcher, is refused with "`emit` hands its event to a dispatcher, and this project has none.", where 0.6.10 compiled it and it threw at run time.

```sh
composer require "heyjordanparker/php-sharp-composer:^0.6.11"
composer require --dev "heyjordanparker/mago-sharp:^0.6.11"
vendor/bin/mago compile
```

### Added

- `Class<T>.methods()` and `properties()` read more of each member, spec section 25. A `Method` gives its `declaringClass`, `isStatic` and `visibility`, a `Property` its `declaringClass` and `visibility`, and a `Parameter` `isVariadic` and `defaultValue()`. A `Visibility` is `Public`, `Internal`, `Protected` or `Private`, the access modifier that declares the member, so an `internal` member reads `Internal` though plain PHP reaches it as a public one. `defaultValue()` evaluates the default on each read, a `new` in it included, and throws `OutOfRangeException` for a parameter without one.
- `Method.invoke(receiver, arguments)`, `Property.get(receiver)` and `Property.set(receiver, value)` call, read and write the member, spec section 25 and decision 94. Each reaches the member as a direct use from the calling code would, a private member only from its class and an `internal` one only from its namespace, and throws `Error` otherwise. Each argument and value is checked against its type, so `add.invoke(counter, ["2"])` for `add(int amount)` throws `TypeError`. A method that isn't private runs the receiver's override, and `get` runs a computed property's `get`. All three may have every effect, so an implementation of a pure interface method can't call them.

### Changed

- `mago convert` writes the `extern namespace` lines a converted file needs into `Externs.sharp` in the project's first PSR-4 folder, decision 97: one line for each library folder whose root functions, constants or classes the file reaches and no line of the project names, as `extern namespace Illuminate.Foundation for abort;`. The namespace is the first of the folder's classes' namespaces, or the one the library's Composer package name gives when it has no namespaced class, as `Acme.StringHelpers` for `acme/string-helpers`. The command creates the file or adds the missing lines in sorted order, never one twice. A plain PHP library's class of PHP's root namespace converts too, as `import Acme.Legacy.Legacy_Order;`, where 0.6.10 refused the file.
- Spec section 5's too-wide warning skips only a member whose attribute's class is marked `[ReachedByReflection]`, and an `[Export]` method, decision 95, where 0.6.10 skipped a member with any attribute.
- `List.first(predicate)` that finds nothing throws `OutOfRangeException` with "`first` finds an element the predicate matches, and none does.", where 0.6.10 said "No element matches the predicate".
- A refusal of an effect that code outside the standard library reaches through a standard library member names that member, as "`Calling.run` reaches `Method.invoke`, which may have every effect", and tells the code to call it where no `uses` limits the effects. 0.6.10 named the library's own plain PHP call, as `ReflectionMethod.getAttributes` for `Method.attributes<T>()`, and told the code to declare it with `extern`.

### Fixed

- A PHP# override of a plain PHP method reads the method's types as a call of it reads them, spec section 22. A method declared `@return array{string|null, string}` gives `List<string?>`, so an override returning `List<string?>` and calling `super.split(text)` compiles, where 0.6.10 refused it as incompatible with "`Map<0|1, string?>`". An override whose parameter takes `List<string|int>` where the plain method's `array` takes `array{string, int}` compiles too, where 0.6.10 asked it to take "PHP's `Sharp\Map`".
- An extension's `CallExpression::fromNode` reads a PHP# call that passes type arguments, as `typeof(Order).methods()[0].attributes<Action>()`, `maybe?.wrap<int>(2)` or `Calc.pair<int>(3)`, and gives them as its new `typeArguments` node, apart from its `arguments`. 0.6.10 refused such a call with "A call node has no argument list.", which stopped the file's analysis. It also reads a null-safe call of a function value, `doubler?.(4)`, as a call of an unnamed function, where 0.6.10 refused it as no call.
- `as` to a type parameter gives the type parameter or null, spec section 21, as `as` to any other type does. Inside `class Sweep<TDue : Event>`, `events.map(e => e as TDue)` of a `List<Event>` is a `List<TDue?>`, so a later `item != null` compiles, where 0.6.10 read a `List<TDue>`, reported the test as redundant and `e` as "already a `TDue`". `value as T` of an `Event` with an unbounded `T` is a `T?`, where 0.6.10 refused `T? any<T>(Event value) => value as T;` as returning `Event`. `is` and `is not` of a type parameter narrow the same way: `ev is TDue` of an `Event` is no longer "always true", and `ev is not TDue` no longer "never matches". In a `.sharp` file, every comparison the checker makes follows PHP#'s rules (spec section 11), where some followed PHP's.
- A library's polyfill of a function the engine builds in, as `if (!function_exists('str_starts_with')) { function str_starts_with(…) }`, no longer stands for the function. A call of `str_starts_with`, as `"ab".startsWith("a")` makes, has the built-in's signature, and the polyfill's file is no input of the compiled file, so an edit of it leaves the file current. 0.6.10 typed the call by the polyfill, `Any?` for one with no return type, and refused the compiled file as out of date after each edit of the polyfill. A polyfill of a function with no built-in of its name is still that function, and its file still an input. The same holds for a polyfill of a built-in class or constant.
- A `.sharp` file that reads plain PHP from an includes folder that is a symlink to a folder outside the project runs right after `mago compile`. The compile records such a file by its absolute path, and the engine read every input's path as one under the project, so 0.6.10 refused the file as "out of date (/…/lib/Helper.php changed)" though nothing had changed. An edit of the file behind the symlink still makes it out of date.
- `Class.of(other).name()` on an `Object` compiles, spec section 25: the `Class<Object>` of any object has every member of a class value, as `name()` and `typeArguments()` of the standard library's `Class<Any?>`. 0.6.10 refused it with "Attempting to access a method on a non-object type (`Class<Object>`).", with or without an extension host.
- `Class<T>.methods()` reads a method whose last parameter is variadic, as `join(string ...parts)`, and gives that parameter as `parts`, where 0.6.10's engine crashed.
- `mago analyze --watch` and the language server drop the uses an edited method of a plain PHP class with exact names made, as the plain `Room` that implements a PHP# interface keys `syncedId`, spec section 24. An edit of the body or the signature of `Room::syncedId` that stops calling `Cart.total` brings back spec section 5's warning "`Cart.total` is public, and nothing outside `App.Shop` uses it.", as a full run reports it. 0.6.10 named the edited method `syncedid`, which no use of `syncedId` meets, and kept the uses its old body made.
- Two PHP# names that differ only in case name two things everywhere, spec section 24, where 0.6.10 still compared some names ignoring case and took one twin for the other. Each compare now keys a name by the engine's rule, a PHP# class-like and the methods of a class with exact names as written, and any other in any case:
  - A host's return-type, assertion, signature and call-forwarding provider, its method-call hook and its entry point of `Demo\Box::total` reach `total` and never its twin `Total`, and a class-like pattern names a PHP# class-like as written. The SDK's `FunctionLikeIdentifier` carries its class's rule as Mago answers it, so `equals` tells `Box::total` from `Box::Total`, and a worker accepts one hook of both `Box::open` and `Box::Open`, where 0.6.10 refused it as a target named twice. The analyzer protocol moves to 1.14, so a mixed pair is refused at its first message.
  - An effect is a class-like named by its key, so `uses DISK` allows no call of the `foreign` class `Disk`.
  - A struct's own `Parse` leaves it the generated `parse` and `tryParse`, a PHP# enum must implement a `Cases` its interface declares, its own static `Cases` keeps its own type, and a plain PHP class with exact names has no `ToString` that runs its `__toString()`.
  - A PHP# class-like `App\RANDOM`, declared in the file or anywhere in its namespace, leaves a bare `Random` in `App` naming the standard library's `Sharp\Random`, where 0.6.10 reported "Cannot find type `Random`; did you mean `App.RANDOM`?".
  - A type that names two twins, as `Parcel.Box | Parcel.BOX` of an enum with the cases `Box` and `BOX`, holds both and takes a `BOX` value, where 0.6.10's checker refused it with "Duplicate type `Parcel.BOX` is redundant." The engine compares the class names of a declared type exactly in a `.sharp` file, so plain PHP's `Foo|FOO` is still a duplicate type.
  - An override whose signature names a PHP# class's twin, as `ship(Parcel\BOX $parcel)` where the parent takes `Parcel\Box`, is refused with PHP's "Declaration of … must be compatible with …", where 0.6.10 took the two for one class. Two names in a signature are one type when they reach one class, so plain PHP's `foo` is still its parent's `Foo`, loaded, autoloaded while the child links, or preloaded.
  - An `unchecked-exception-classes` or `unchecked-exceptions` entry names a PHP# exception as written, so `App\Errors\NotFound` leaves its twin `NOTFOUND` checked.
  - In a `.sharp` file, a method `priceof` is no clash with a property `priceOf`, the laws `same` and `Same` are two laws, a method `later` is no clash with the case `Later`, a method `Seconds` none with a case's field `seconds`, `status()` is no constructor of the enum `Status`, `Order.order()` is a named constructor of `Order`, `GetIterator` is no `getIterator`, a bare `Total` names no method `total`, and a constant of `Report` reads no static member through its twin `REPORT`.
- A plain PHP class that implements a PHP# interface whose default bodies include twins, as `total()` and `Total()`, and uses its `Defaults` trait, takes both as two methods, spec section 24, and its own `Total()` overrides only the default `Total()`. 0.6.10's engine refused the class with "Trait method …\Defaults::Total has not been applied as …::Total, because of collision with …\Defaults::total", or, beside an override, as leaving `total` abstract. The class gained exact names from its interface only after its traits were bound, and it now gains them before.
- `mago` reads a plain PHP class that takes a PHP# interface's default bodies as the engine runs it, spec section 24:
  - `use …\Defaults { recordEntry as entry; Total as grandTotal; }` gives the class `entry` and `grandTotal`, and `grandTotal` is the twin `Total`, never `total`. 0.6.10 keyed the names of an `as` in lowercase before the class had exact names, so it reported `entry` and `grandTotal` as methods that don't exist, and `Codebase::findMethods()` named an alias by the trait's method.
  - A call in another case of a method the class takes from the trait names the method it meant, as "`Demo\TakesDefaults` has no member `Count`; did you mean `count`?", where 0.6.10 said only "Method `Count` does not exist".
  - A value of an intersection with a PHP# part, as `Ledger&Countable`, is of a class with the part's exact names, so `$o->Count()` is the error the engine throws, where 0.6.10 took the plain `Countable`'s `count` for it. The error names the PHP# part, in either order of the intersection.
- `mago` reads a class's trait adaptations as PHP applies them. `B::m insteadof A` gives the class `B`'s `m`, where 0.6.10 kept the first trait's. A qualified `A::m as am` aliases only `A`'s `m`, where 0.6.10 aliased every used trait's `m`, and a method keeps each of its aliases, as `am` and `amAgain`, where 0.6.10 kept only the last alias of each method name. A visibility change applies only to the trait method the class takes, so `A::n as protected` after `B::n insteadof A`, or `k as protected` beside the class's own `k`, leaves `n` and `k` public, where 0.6.10 refused their calls as calls of a protected method.

## [0.6.10] - 2026-11-13

### Upgrading from 0.6.9

0.6.10 fixes regressions 0.6.9 shipped with exact names, among them a plain PHP class whose override of a camelCase default body of a PHP# interface never ran. Upgrade from 0.6.9 straight away. From 0.6.8, follow 0.6.9's upgrade notes. 0.6.9 left out one rule there: a plain PHP class that implements a PHP# interface has exact names, as a class below a PHP# class does, so every method of it, inherited from a plain parent too, is reached only by the name it declares.

### Fixed

- A plain PHP closure answers `is Function<…>` by its declared PHP signature, spec section 21, where 0.6.9 answered false for every one. `static fn (): ?Item => new Item()` and `static fn (): Item => new Item()` are a `Function<Item?() uses none>`, a parameter it declares no type for takes anything, and a result it declares no type for is `Any?`, so `static fn () => new Item()` is a `Function<Any?()>` and not a `Function<Item?()>`. Its type lists no effects, so it fits every `uses`, as the checker takes a plain PHP callable.
- `mago` names a method that a plain PHP class's `__call` serves as the call writes it, so a call of `whereKey` that the `__call` of a `ForwardedQuery` serves warns "Ambiguous method call to `whereKey` on class `ForwardedQuery`.", where 0.6.9 wrote `wherekey`.
- A lazy `map` of a plain PHP closure is an `Iterable` of the result type the closure declares, spec section 12, so `ids.map(label)` with `static fn (int $id): string => "#$id"` is an `Iterable<string>` without running it, where 0.6.9 read every plain closure's result as `Any?`. A closure that declares no result type still gives an `Iterable<Any?>`.
- `self`, `parent` and `static` in a plain PHP declaration read as the class each names, and `never` as PHP#'s `never`, alike in `Class<T>.properties()`, `Class<T>.methods()` and `is Function<…>`. `self` is the declaring class, a trait's `self` and `parent` the class that uses the trait and its parent, and `static` the class value's class or a closure's called class, so `static fn (): static => new static()` made through `Chain::maker()` is a `Function<Chain()>`. A `never` result fits every result type. 0.6.9 threw "The engine gave a type text that names no type." from `properties()` and `methods()` for a trait's `self` and a method's `static`.
- A PHP# lambda that always throws, as `() => throw new ValueError("stopped")`, and a closure of a `never` method have the function type of their parameters and the result `never`, so each fits every function type's result, as a plain PHP `fn (): never` does. 0.6.9 gave them no function type, so `is Function<…>` answered false for every one.
- A lambda whose every path ends in `return;`, as `() => { return; }`, returns `void`, so `Function<int()> skip = () => { return; };` is refused, where 0.6.9 typed its result `never` and took it for any function type.
- A call of a camelCase PHP# interface's method, PHP#'s or plain PHP's, and a plain PHP first-class callable of it reach it through an intersection whose first part is a plain PHP interface, as `item.priceInCents()` on a `Named & Priced item` does, where 0.6.9 reported "Method `Named.priceInCents` does not exist." It keyed the name once by the first part's rule, lowercase for plain PHP, and looked that key up in the PHP# part, which keys it exactly. Each part now keys the name as written by its own rule, as a parent class, a required class and a mixin do.
- A plain PHP class's own `recordEntry` overrides the camelCase default body of a PHP# interface it implements, as its own `count` already overrode a lowercase one, so `(new Ledger)->recordEntry(7)` runs the class's body. 0.6.9 kept both methods, ran the default body, and listed `recordEntry` twice in `getMethods()`. The engine keyed a trait's method in the class that uses it by the trait's rule, exact for a PHP# interface's `Defaults` trait, so it never met the class's own lowercase `recordentry`. A class now keys each method a trait gives it by its own rule, so a plain PHP class that uses a PHP# trait without implementing its interface also runs the trait's camelCase method by any call, and `insteadof` and `as` across a PHP# and a plain trait keep one method of each name, where 0.6.9 threw "Call to undefined method".
- `mago` keys every name it looks up by the rule the engine keys it by, spec section 24: a PHP# class-like and the methods of a class with exact names by their exact names, and any other in lowercase. 0.6.9 still lowercased a name in places and then missed the PHP# symbol, which these now find:
  - `Codebase::findMethods(withAnyAttribute: ['Probe\Marked'])` finds a method marked with the PHP# attribute `Marked`. The SDK sends each class, method and attribute name as written, and its cache keeps the case of a class or method name.
  - `SymbolReferences::getReferencesFrom()` and `getReferencesTo()` find the references from and to a PHP# class and its methods.
  - `array_map(Room::findOrFail(...), [1, 2])` types the callable `Room::findOrFail` when the plain PHP `Room` implements a PHP# interface, as the engine names it, where 0.6.9 wrote `Room::findorfail` and refused the call.
  - A host's method, property and class initializer provider for a plain PHP class serves a PHP# subclass.
  - A `class-initializers` entry such as `setUp` initializes the properties of a plain PHP class that has exact names from a PHP# interface.
  - A `class_alias()` of a PHP# class names it, and the references a string callable `'Probe\Box::openLid'`, a PHP first-class callable and an effect record name a PHP# class by its exact name.

## [0.6.9] - 2026-11-13

### Upgrading from 0.6.8

#### Call a PHP# method by its exact name

PHP# names are case-sensitive, spec section 24, so `total()` and `Total()` are two methods. Code reaches a PHP# class's method, or the method of a class below a PHP# class, only by the name it declares, plain PHP code included. A call in another case reaches nothing, so `$order->Total()` on a class that declares only `total()` throws "Call to undefined method App\Order::Total(); did you mean total()?" where 0.6.8 ran `total()`, and `mago` refuses it with "`App\Order` has no member `Total`; did you mean `total`?". Write the name as the method declares it. A method that differs from an inherited one only in case is a second method, and an override names its parent's method exactly. Plain PHP classes keep PHP's rule.

#### Name a PHP# type by its exact name

Code finds a PHP# class, interface, enum or trait only by the name it declares, plain PHP code included. `new App\ORDER()` for a class `App\Order` throws `Class "App\ORDER" not found` where 0.6.8 made an `App\Order`, `class_exists('App\ORDER')`, `instanceof` and `is_a` give false, reflection throws, `unserialize` gives an incomplete class, and a class an autoloader loads for the name counts only when its name is the one asked for. `mago` refuses the name with "Cannot find type `ORDER`; did you mean `Order`?". A plain PHP class whose name differs from a PHP# class's only in case is a second class.

### Added

- `Class<T>.methods()` gives a class's instance and static methods, its own first and then those it inherits, spec section 25, as `properties()` gives its properties. A `Method` has its `name`, its result `type`, `nullable`, `attributes<TAttribute : Object>()` and `parameters()`, and a `Parameter` its `name`, `type`, `nullable`, `optional` and `attributes<TAttribute : Object>()`. `typeof(Box<Order>).methods()` reads `Order` where `Box` writes its type parameter. The `parse` and `tryParse` a class gets take a `Map<string, Any?>|Object`.
- `Json.encode` takes `indent`, spec section 24. `indent: 2` writes each nested value on its own line, 2 spaces deeper than its parent, with `": "` after a key and `[]` and `{}` for an empty value. `indent: 4` writes the bytes of PHP's `JSON_PRETTY_PRINT`, the default `0` writes one line, and a negative `indent` throws a `ValueError`.

### Changed

- A call whose result crosses into PHP#, as a PHP# call of a virtual method that plain PHP may override, finds the method that ran once per receiver class at each call site, where 0.6.8 looked it up on every call. A loop of camelCase method calls runs in about two thirds of the instructions.

### Fixed

- `Class<T>.properties()` gives a property of a union of scalars, as `int|string`, and of a nullable one, as `(int|string)?`, where 0.6.8 left both out. A property's class type with type arguments, as `Holder<Box>`, keeps them: its `typeArguments()` is `[typeof(Box)]`, as `typeof(Holder<Box>).typeArguments()` is, where 0.6.8 gave none.
- A law without a body that proves case by case over an enum it takes reports each case it does not prove, one error each, as "when retry is Retry.ExponentialRamp, still to show: …", where 0.6.8 reported the whole claim once and named no case.
- A PHP# class overrides the field or the method of a plain PHP ancestor that declares both with one name, as Eloquent's `$casts` and `casts()`, where 0.6.8 refused both overrides with `name-already-in-use`. A call runs the method and a read reads the field, as PHP resolves them. A PHP# class that declares one and inherits the other from a PHP# class is still refused.
- `mago` knows two PHP# classes whose names differ only in case, as `Order` and `ORDER`, as two classes, where 0.6.8 refused the second with "Class `ORDER` is already defined". A plain PHP class still matches in any case.
- `Sharp\Container` knows a PHP# service class by its exact name, so `get('app\shop\stamp')` names no binding of `App\Shop\Stamp`, and a test file's module applies only to its class's exact name. A plain PHP class still matches in any case.
- A law tells two fields whose names differ only in case apart, as PHP does, where 0.6.8 read a stored `raw` for an accessor `Raw`.
- phpdbg finds a PHP# class by its exact name: `break App\Order::total#1` resolves in a loaded PHP# class where 0.6.8 left it pending, and `break App\Order::Total` no longer stops in its twin `total()`.
- phpdbg keeps `break App\Order::total` and `break App\Order::Total` as two breakpoints of a PHP# class, each stopping in its own method, where 0.6.8 refused the second with "Breakpoint exists". A plain PHP class still names its methods in any case.
- The `parse` and `tryParse` a struct or class gets list the effects of what they run in their function type: the main constructor, the `set` accessor of each property they write, and the `parse` of each struct or class a field holds, where 0.6.8 gave them none.
- A constant's type is its declared type, which its value may only narrow, so `private const string LABEL = "1.0 / " + Weights.COUNT + " as weight";` reads as a `string` where 0.6.8 read it as an `int`. Its value is still refused at its declaration, and the help of a `+` that joins a string with a number now writes its whole chain as a template, as `` `1.0 / ${Weights.COUNT} as weight` ``.
- A string fixed while the code compiles reaches a plain PHP `literal-string` parameter, as Laravel's `selectRaw` declares one: a template or a `+` join whose parts are all literals or constants of type `int`, `float` or `string`, and a constant that holds one. 0.6.8 refused `query.selectRaw(Weights.LINEAR)` for `` private const string LINEAR = `1.0 / ${Weights.TOUCHES} as weight`; ``.
